# Design — Statement review: every figure traced, the unclear lines first

Revision 31, 2026-11-21, after D301 (changes requested on 2026-11-21, the owner's decision of 2026-11-20 given in chat, after T04's fifty-seventh review, verify-192). What changed from revision 30, keeping every id. D290 and D291 answered revision 30's two questions with their defaults. The app the MCP server hands out is the SDK's app behind a lock check, as R53 already says. A lock turned on takes effect from the next run, which D291 asked R29 and P18 to say in the next revision. D292 approved revision 30. T04 was then reviewed four more times, verify-187, verify-188, verify-190 and verify-192, and every review failed on R2; the owner took the first three over (D294, D295, D297). From verify-188 on, each R2 major found one more check that relates records to one another made later than one record ahead: the balance chain, a newest-first file's order, a balance compared across a record with none, and the Monzo importer's walk of the balance after sorting. D301 clarifies revision 30's limit, which R2 now states. The one-record-ahead limit is about each record's own values. The checks across records are made from the records read once reading has stopped, with no order promised between them and a record's own problem beyond what R2 says the Monzo importer names. bedf5ff already builds that, so before its review T04 changes only the two docstrings and the comment that still say nothing past the first problem is read (Context). T17 was reviewed four more times, verify-186, verify-189, verify-191 and verify-193, and every review failed; the owner took the first three over (D293, D296, D298), and the fix for the fourth, 9b10daa, is not yet reviewed. D301 keeps everything else in revision 30, so R29, R30, P5 and P18 do not change. Revision 31 asks the owner four questions for T17: when R29 and P18 should say D291, how far R29 reaches at a native MCP runner's bind and at a callback a caller hands a run, and whether P5's sentinels exclude strings the policy in use holds (Options considered). The design takes in verify-186 to verify-193, D290 to D298 and D301. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C4, C7, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, Interfaces, Failure modes, Migration and Consumer trace (a new item 32).

Revision 30, 2026-11-21, after D289 (changes requested on 2026-11-21, the owner's decision of 2026-11-20 given in chat, after T17's fifth review, verify-184, and T04's fifty-third, verify-185). What changed from revision 29, keeping every id. D259 answered revision 29's question with its default, an output path taken while the review waits being outside the export guarantee, and D260 approved revision 29. T04 was then reviewed twenty-six more times, verify-154 to verify-185, and every review failed on R2; the owner took the first twenty-five over (D261 to D284 and D286), and verify-185 passed the owner's 82758a3 on every criterion but R2. T17 was reviewed three more times, verify-157, verify-182 and verify-184, and every review failed; the owner took the fourth over (D285) and built 6b43212 for the fifth, not yet reviewed. In each task one run of majors reached an edge the package cannot hold. In T17, R29 and R53 met at the MCP server's ASGI apps: an app saved while unlocked could bind after the lock was on (verify-122), refusing every app broke R53's embedders (verify-182), and a lock-checked app cannot stop an embedder's server that binds before start-up (verify-184). In T04, each review found one more way the reader met a later part of a file before naming the first problem, until verify-185 found the whole export read, decoded and split, and the next record read, before the first was checked. D289 accepts both as outside the guarantee and asks for two scope limits. R29, R53 and, through R29, P18 now say that while the lock is off the MCP server hands out its ASGI apps behind a lock check that fails start-up, answers 503 and closes websockets once the lock is on; that under the lock none is handed out; and that a listening socket an embedder's own server opens for a saved app before start-up is that server's doing. R2 now says that the loader and the Monzo importer read a file into memory, decode and split it, then read its records in one pass, one record ahead, and that reading as a stream is outside R2. 82758a3 and 6b43212 already build both; before its review T04 rewords two docstrings, a comment and a test name that claim nothing past the first problem is read (Context). Revision 30 asks the owner whether the app handed out may be a wrapper of the SDK's app, as 6b43212 builds, and when a lock turned on takes effect for a run already going (Options considered). The design takes in verify-154 to verify-185, D259 to D286 and D289. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C4, C7, C12, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, Interfaces, Failure modes, Migration and Consumer trace (a new item 31).

Revision 29, 2026-11-20, after D258 (changes requested by the owner on 2026-11-20, given in chat, after T04's twenty-seventh review, verify-153). What changed from revision 28, keeping every id. D250 approved revision 28. T04 was then reviewed seven more times, verify-147 to verify-153, and every review failed. The owner took the first six over (D251 to D256), and the owner's fix for verify-153, c2460a0, is not yet reviewed. The two gaps revision 28 named are closed (76f230c, 23acaa1, 52914cc). The majors since fell into two runs. Against R2, each found one more thing a refusal left unnamed: the line a record starts on, a field too long to read taken for an amount at the limit, or, in the Monzo importer, money in already at the limit among the rows read, hidden by a problem later in the export. Against R53, each found one more interleaving of `cma` processes sharing a checkpoint file, or a failure writing the memo and its evidence after the decision was recorded. D258 accepts these as outside the guarantee and asks for four scope limits, which R2 and R53 now state. The Monzo importer reads rows in order, a counted row's amount first, and stops at its first problem, saying with it money in or money out at the limit among the rows read so far. A line is named by the line its record starts on, blank lines counted, and a field too long to read is never named as an amount at the limit. Processes sharing a checkpoint file serialise three steps with a lock file beside it, and interference with those files is outside the single-decision guarantee. The memo and its evidence are made before either is written and moved into place, evidence first, and a failure after the decision is recorded is outside the guarantee. c2460a0 builds most of this. Before its review T04 reads a Monzo row's amount before its Transaction ID, and drops the clause in its too-long refusal that says such a field would be past the limit (Context). Revision 29 asks the owner whether an output path another program takes while the review waits is outside the export guarantee too (Options considered). The design takes in verify-147 to verify-153, D250 to D256 and D258. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C4, C12, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, Interfaces, Failure modes, Migration and Consumer trace (a new item 30).

Revision 28, 2026-11-20, after D249 (changes requested by the owner on 2026-11-20, given in chat, after T04's twentieth review, verify-146). What changed from revision 27, keeping every id. D235 approved revision 27. T04 was then reviewed thirteen more times, verify-134 to verify-146, and every review failed. The owner took the first twelve over (D236 to D247), and the owner's fix for verify-146, 3f4987c, is not yet reviewed. Since verify-142, `cma resume` decides a review that an earlier release left pending on the figures this release computes (5e7d90c). verify-144 to verify-146 each failed R53 on such a review whose policy outcome the resume could not settle, and so refused. D249 accepts these as outside R53's guarantee and asks for one scope limit, which R53 now states. Such a review is decided, finalised and recorded on this release's figures. A rule whose figure differs takes its outcome from the stored policy result when that settles it, and otherwise from the policy file the review was drafted with, known by its stored hash. When neither settles it, `cma resume` refuses, naming the figure and saying to run `cma run` on the statement again, with the stored review untouched and nothing recorded. The owner's 94ced9c already builds this (src/cma/policy.py:101-154 and src/cma/graph.py:216-244 at 3f4987c). Two gaps remain, and T04 closes both before its review: a refusal raised when the reviewer's answer resumes the graph reaches the reviewer uncaught, and a stored rule without its comparison raises instead of falling to the policy file (Context). Revision 28 asks no question. The design takes in verify-134 to verify-146, D235 to D247 and D249. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C12, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, Interfaces, Failure modes, Migration and Consumer trace (a new item 29).

Revision 27, 2026-11-20, after D234 (changes requested by the owner on 2026-11-20, after T04's seventh review, verify-133). What changed from revision 26, keeping every id. D230 answered revision 26's question with its default, the pack's memo-ready section text quoting money to the whole pound, and D231 approved revision 26. verify-132 then failed the owner's 74ad8af and verify-133 the owner's 3886917, both on R2, R3 and P2; T04 is blocked, with D233, its unblock, pending, and the owner's f147255, which answers verify-133's R2 and R3 findings, is not yet reviewed. verify-133's P2 finding is where R2 meets R54. On Python 3.11 the previous release's float sum of a statement whose loan repayments are all returned unpaid can round to a penny of debt service (M8), so its debt service cover (M10) cites lines that the exact pence, which give no debt service, do not. D234 settles it. R54 now gives M1 to M16 the previous release's evidence wherever the previous release's arithmetic was exact, which it defines by the six money figures the previous release computed by adding amounts as floats; elsewhere R2's exact pence prevail, and each figure and its evidence follow them. R2 now says that the exact pence prevail. P2, P7, R10 and the term derived figure say the same through R54, so that none promises what R54 now limits. No figure's value or evidence moves on any committed or synthetic case, all far below where a float sum drifts half a penny. T04 needs no code for it, since f147255 computes every figure from the exact pence already; it adds one test of verify-133's statement before its review. Revision 27 asks no question. The design takes in verify-132, verify-133 and D230 to D234. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C2, C6, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, Data changes, Interfaces, Failure modes, Migration and Consumer trace (a new item 28).

Revision 26, 2026-11-20, after D229 (changes requested by the owner on 2026-11-20, after T04's fifth review, verify-131). What changed from revision 25, keeping every id. D224 and D225 answered revision 25's two questions with their defaults, and D226 approved revision 25. verify-130 then failed the owner's 0c63ca4 and verify-131 the owner's aa04f0c, both on R2, R3 and P2; T04 is blocked, with D228, its unblock, pending, and the owner's 74ad8af, which answers verify-131's R3 finding, is not yet reviewed. R41 now refuses an import that would bring a case's combined money in or money out to £10,000,000,000,000, naming the account (D224), and R11 cites D225 for the month's lowest end-of-day balance. D229 settles the other two findings. R2 now says that a money figure is recorded and tabled to the penny, in the evidence record, the appendix's figure table, the interactive table and every table built later, while prose, and the prompt table and rule sentences it is drafted from, keep the previous release's whole pound, which the guard checks at the precision written. R54 now keeps the previous release's evidence for M1 to M16 for a statement categorised by v1 on its own with no case profile, and makes a case profile new behaviour under any ruleset, with which M16 and M26 leave own-account transfers out of their share. P2, P7, R10 and the term derived figure say the same through R54, so that none promises what R54 now limits. No figure's value or evidence moves on any committed or synthetic case, none of which carries a profile. T04's remaining build is to regenerate the committed example, whose appendix still shows whole pounds, and to test a profile's own-account credit leaving M16 and M26; 74ad8af already does in code what revision 26 asks of R2 and R54. Revision 26 asks the owner whether the pack's memo-ready section text quotes money to the whole pound, as R2 now says, or to the penny (Options considered). The design takes in verify-130, verify-131 and D224 to D229. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C1, C2, C4, C6, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, Data changes, Interfaces, Failure modes, Migration, Rollout and Consumer trace (a new item 27).

Revision 25, 2026-11-20, after D223 (changes requested by the owner on 2026-11-20, after T04's third review, verify-129). What changed from revision 24, keeping every id. D220 answered revision 24's question with its default, the monthly money-out cell an outflow figure, and D221 approved revision 24. verify-129 then failed T04, which has used its attempts; D222, its unblock, is pending, and the owner built a fix in 0c63ca4, not yet reviewed. D223 asks for two clarifications. R2 now says that amounts and balances are read exactly from the statement's text and counted in whole pence, and that a statement with an amount or balance of £10,000,000,000,000 or more, or whose money in or money out totals that much, is refused at import, named, since no figure could then be reported to the penny as the float the figures are handed on in (fifteen significant digits); below that, every money figure is reported to the penny. P2, R10 and R54, which D223 names as 'R1/R54', now say that M1 to M16 keep the evidence the previous release gave them, which the dual run compares, so M6 cites the line that closed each overdrawn day; the new derived figures and the monthly balance and overdrawn-day cells cite every line their calculation reads. R11 cites D220 and says which lines its balance and overdrawn-day cells cite. No figure's value changes. The build changes are the loader's exact reading and limit and the display from the counted pence, which 0c63ca4 builds, and the Monzo importer's exact reading, which T04 adds before its review. Revision 25 asks the owner whether R41 should hold a multi-account case's combined money in and money out to the same limit, and whether the month's lowest end-of-day balance cites every close in the month (Options considered). The design takes in verify-129 and D220 to D223. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C2, C4, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, Data changes, Interfaces, Failure modes, Migration and Consumer trace (a new item 26).

Revision 24, 2026-11-20, after D219 (changes requested by the owner on 2026-11-20, after T04's first two reviews, verify-127 and verify-128). What changed from revision 23, keeping every id. D211 and D212 answered revision 23's two questions with their defaults, and D213 approved it. T03 then passed (verify-126, at 2576e07) and was merged. T12 is blocked at 04ff28a (verify-124), with D217, its unblock, pending, and T17 failed its first review (verify-122). T04, the figures layer, failed both its reviews on P2, among other findings. M8, M11, M12 and M14 report money paid out as positive amounts whose lines total them negated, as they have since before this change, and M9 cites in full a returned transfer that it counts only in part; P2 never said which figures are sums. D219 asks for three kinds, which P2, R1, R11 and R16 now state, with three new terms. A sum figure's lines total it. An outflow figure is reported as a positive amount and its lines total it negated. A derived figure cites every line its calculation reads and is not asked to total them, and M9 names in an evidence note the lines it counts only in part. No figure's value, sign or evidence changes; the one build change is M9's note, which T04 adds before its next review. Revision 24 reads D219's 'every monthly cell' as the cells that add lines up, and asks the owner whether the monthly money-out cell is an outflow figure, positive (the default), or a signed sum (Options considered). The design takes in verify-120 to verify-128 and D211 to D219. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C2, C3, the traceability table, Consequences, the perspective check, Data changes, Interfaces, Failure modes, Migration and Consumer trace (a new item 25).

Revision 23, 2026-11-20, after D210 (changes requested by the owner on 2026-11-20, given in chat, after T12's forty-seventh review, verify-118, and T03's forty-sixth, verify-119). What changed from revision 22, keeping every id. D193 approved revision 22. Since then each task was reviewed eight more times, every review failed, and the owner took each over seven times (D194 to D207); D208 and D209, the unblocks the eighth round raised, are pending. verify-118 failed T12 at 43332c6 on a KeyboardInterrupt during a read-back across a replaced case lock. verify-119 failed T03 at 3ae10cc on a mapping key YAML cannot hash beside a repeated pattern, and on a repeated direction that leaves the bank's code debit-only in one version. D210 accepts such corners as edge cases outside the guarantee, to push development forward, and asks for two scope limits. R4 and R54 now say that a rule table that is not well formed is refused as a whole, naming the faults its loader finds, at least one, and that naming every fault, or every combination of a repeated key's values, is outside R4; a well-formed table is checked rule by rule as before. R42, R43 and P15 now say that removing, renaming or replacing a case lock file while the case is in use, and interrupting a save at an arbitrary instant, are outside their guarantees: the tool reports as it can, and a check after the interference stops gives the true answer. Revision 23 reads D210's 'as it can' as reaching the two guarantees R42 and P15 have stated under such interference since revision 13, and keeps R42's refusal of a save begun inside another; it asks the owner about both (Options considered). No code changes: both builds already do what revision 23 asks, and both latest majors fall outside it. The design takes in verify-104 to verify-119 and D193 to D210. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C1's table validation, C3, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, the case log contract, Failure modes, Migration, Rollout, Security notes and Consumer trace.

Revision 22, 2026-11-20, after D192 (changes requested under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19, after T12's thirty-ninth review, verify-102, and T03's thirty-eighth, verify-103). What changed from revision 21, keeping every id. D169 approved revision 21. Between it and D192 each task was reviewed eleven times, every review failed, and the owner took each task over ten times (D170 to D189). R43 and P15 now say how a reader's check after its read looks at the lock files: at each one it counts on and then at the first again, and, for a reader that holds the case, also at whether each file it holds still has a name. They also state for readers the window R42 has stated for writers since revision 10: a lock file removed or replaced, renamed aside and replaced included, after the check's last look at it is outside what any check can know (D192, after verify-102; 523c5df). R4 now says that only a digest that matches the pin spares v1 the exception check. It also says that a key given more than once is a fault whose every value is still checked, the rules of every rules list and each value of a repeated field, whichever comes first (D192, after verify-103; 4c017d4). No other requirement changes; R42 already puts lock-file interference outside its guarantee. The owner built these before this revision, and neither commit is reviewed yet; D190 and D191, the unblocks, are pending. At 4c017d4 the compile check and the matcher build reach only the last value of a repeated field, and the checks for the pattern forms a v2 table refuses read none of its values (Context). D192's own words ask for every value, so T03 extends those checks before its review, and revision 22 asks no question. The design takes in verify-82 to verify-103 and D169 to D192. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C1's table validation, C3, the traceability table, Consequences, the perspective check, the case log contract, Failure modes, Migration, Rollout and Consumer trace items 17, 20, 23 and 24.

Revision 21, 2026-11-20, after D168 (changes requested under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19, after T12's twenty-eighth review, verify-80, and T03's twenty-seventh, verify-81). What changed from revision 20, keeping every id. D164 answered revision 20's question with its default: one answer per report, the take-back's refusal worded for the save; the owner built it in 7fb3a23. R42 now says that each report a save's writer gets gives one answer, its causes included: a not-confirmed report names what stopped its take-back in the save's own words, and no part of it says the save was recorded, was not recorded or that nothing was written (D164, D168, after verify-80). R42 also says that whether a save's head record was published is known from its rename, never inferred from a head record that may be unreadable, so a head record that cannot be read before its rename answers not recorded (D168, after verify-80; 26bc51e). R4 now says that only v1 as its bytes were verified is spared the exception check, so a changed v1 names its forbidden exceptions after the changed-table refusal, and is otherwise judged as the kind of table it says it is. It also says that every rule's matchers are built while the table is checked, so a pattern that cannot be used is named with the table's other faults (D168, after verify-81; 5f5bb2e). No other requirement changes: P15 asks less than R42 here, and R54 refers to R4 for what else a changed v1 breaks. The owner built these before this revision, and none is reviewed yet: T12's 26bc51e and 7fb3a23, and T03's 5f5bb2e. Revision 21 asks no question. At 7fb3a23 a save refused before any part of its line is in the log is told only that the step was refused, where R42 asks for not recorded. At 5f5bb2e an unusable pattern on a rule with another malformed field is still found only once that field is mended (Context). The design takes in verify-80 and verify-81 and D164 to D168. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C1's table validation, C3, C5, the traceability table, Consequences, the perspective check, the case log contract, the workspace API, Failure modes, Migration, Rollout, Observability and Consumer trace items 17, 20, 23 and 24.

Revision 20, 2026-11-20, after D163 (changes requested under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19, after T12's twenty-seventh review, verify-78, and T03's twenty-sixth, verify-79). What changed from revision 19, keeping every id. D159 answered revision 19's question with its default: the reads a save makes only to check something name an operating-system error, and a re-check that cannot read answers not recorded, never a conflict; the owner built it in 1e35352. R42 now names those reads: the comparisons of the log and the head record with the save's check, the read-back and the examination of the case lock files. It says that one that cannot be made is reported as its error, never as a conflict to reload or a lock file removed or replaced (D163). R42 and P15 now say that once any part of a save's line is in the log, no part of what its writer is told says nothing was written (D163, after verify-78; de6a56e). R4 now says that a malformed value is a fault of its rule whatever else the rule carries, the bank-code flag included. It also says that whatever a change to v1 makes fail, the refusal names the changed frozen table first (D163, after verify-79; 5139e22). No other requirement changes; R54 already says what R4 now adds for a changed v1. The owner built these before this revision, and none is reviewed yet: T12's de6a56e and 1e35352, and T03's 5139e22. At 1e35352 a save whose take-back is refused quotes the refusal in its not-confirmed report. With a case lock file replaced, the quoted words say nothing was written, which R42 and P15 now rule out. With the head record no longer the save's own, they say the save was not recorded, so revision 20 asks the owner whether a not-confirmed report may quote what stopped its take-back (Options considered). At 5139e22 no type error escapes the table checks, but a failure no check foresees is refused naming no rule (Context, T03's twenty-sixth review). The design takes in verify-78 and verify-79 and D159 to D163. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C1's table validation, C3, C5, the traceability table, Consequences, the perspective check, the case log contract, the workspace API, Failure modes, Migration, Rollout, Observability and Consumer trace items 17, 20, 23 and 24.

Revision 19, 2026-11-20, after D158 (changes requested under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19, after T12's twenty-sixth review, verify-76, and T03's twenty-fifth, verify-77). What changed from revision 18, keeping every id. D154 answered revision 18's question with its default: R42's answer for a save the file system fails reaches every step the save takes after its check, and 51c8b68 maps the steps that change a file. R42 now states that answer as one rule: every operating-system error a save meets after its check is reported, naming the error, as not recorded, or as not confirmed when the save's head record was published and not taken back (D158). It names the steps the rule covers, and counts among them the reads a save makes to re-check something or to read itself back. R42 and P15 now say that a writer refused once any part of its line is written reports its save as not recorded, never as nothing written, the part staying past the recorded entries as a torn entry (D158, after verify-76; 9c265a3). R4 and R54 now say that the table loaded as v1 is judged by its bytes before anything else in it is checked, with whatever else keeps it from loading named after the changed-table refusal, and R4 that a value the format does not allow in a rule's field is named by rule, never raised as a type error (D158, after verify-77; 7cb2e71). No other requirement changes. The owner built these before this revision, and none is reviewed yet: T12's 9c265a3 and 51c8b68, and T03's 7cb2e71. At 51c8b68 the reads a save makes only to check something still turn the file system's error into an answer that names nothing, and before the line into a conflict that asks the person to reload; R42 as revision 19 writes it rules that out, so revision 19 asks the owner whether R42's naming reaches those reads (Options considered). At 7cb2e71 one validator path still raises a type error, for a bank-code rule whose pattern is a list (Context, T03's twenty-fifth review). The design takes in verify-76 and verify-77 and D154 to D158. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C1's table validation, C3, C5, the traceability table, Consequences, the perspective check, the case log contract, the workspace API, Failure modes, Migration, Rollout, Observability and Consumer trace items 17, 20, 23 and 24.

Revision 18, 2026-11-20, after D153 (changes requested under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19, after T12's twenty-fifth review, verify-74, and T03's twenty-fourth, verify-75). What changed from revision 17, keeping every id. D149 answered revision 17's question with its default: the take-back re-checks both lock files before its rename, as every head rename does, and a take-back that re-check stops answers not confirmed; R42 has said so since revision 17, and 7c8fba2 builds it. R42 now also says that when the file system fails a step a save takes after its check, the save answers, naming the error, which never reaches the caller as itself: not recorded before the rename that publishes its head record, and not confirmed after it, when the head record cannot be made durable (D153, after verify-74). R43 now says that a read taken again goes through the gate and the case lock, so a busy-gate read whose case lock is removed during it looks again, never reading as a reader that finds no gate (D153, after verify-74). R4 now says that only the frozen v1 table, known by the hash pinned in code, is spared the check that no rule carries an exception, and that every other table, whether or not it calls itself frozen, is checked rule by rule and refused naming the rule (D153, after verify-75). The term not confirmed widens to its second cause. No other requirement changes, and P15, which asks less than R42 and R43 here, does not. The owner built these before this revision, and neither commit is reviewed yet: T12's 7c8fba2 and T03's 9042fb4. 7c8fba2 maps the file system's errors where the head record is published; at a save's other steps they still reach the caller as themselves, which R42 as revision 18 writes it rules out, so revision 18 asks the owner how far R42's clause should reach (Options considered). The design takes in verify-74 and verify-75 and D149 to D153. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C1's table validation, C3, C5, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, the case log contract, the workspace API, Failure modes, Migration, Rollout, Observability and Consumer trace items 17, 20 and 23 and a new item 24.

Revision 17, 2026-11-20, after D148 (changes requested under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19, after T12's twenty-fourth review, verify-72, and T03's twenty-third, verify-73). What changed from revision 16, keeping every id. R4 now says that the set-aside rule is judged match by match, from the longest down: a match set aside inside a longer phrase sets nothing aside even where its rule counts by another match, and a rule counts where any match of its own counts (D142, the owner's answer to revision 16's first question). R42 and P15 now name a third answer for a save, not confirmed, when its read-back fails and its head record is not then taken back (D143). R43 and P15 now say which lock files each reader counts on, so that a holder's read and a busy-gate read count only while theirs are still the case's (D148, after verify-72). They also say that lock-file interference beyond a read across such a lock file is outside what a reader can know, and that a check made after it stops gives the true answer (D144). No other requirement changes. The owner built these before this revision, and none is reviewed yet: T03's 4b3b21c, and T12's 0d339cc and 3076547. D148 also asks R42 to say that the take-back checks only that the head record is still the save's own, as 0d339cc builds. Revision 17 does not write that clause: under lock-file interference it lets a take-back unrecord a repair that was told it was recorded, which R42's own guarantee rules out (Context, T12's twenty-fourth review). R42 keeps the take-back among the steps a writer re-checks both lock files before, a take-back that re-check stops answers not confirmed, and revision 17 asks the owner. The design takes in verify-72 and verify-73 and D142 to D148. The changes are in this header, Context, Options considered, C1's verdict step 1, C3, C5, the traceability table, Out of scope, Consequences, the perspective check, the case log contract, the workspace API, Failure modes, Migration, Rollout, Observability and Consumer trace items 17, 19, 20 and 23.

Revision 16, 2026-11-20, after D141 (changes requested under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19, after T12's twenty-third review, verify-70, and T03's twenty-second, verify-71). What changed from revision 15, keeping every id. R4 now says that a v2 pattern may carry an anchor (^, $, \A or \Z) only on the bank's returned-payment code (D137, the owner's answer to revision 15's question), and that only a match that itself counts can set another aside inside its longer phrase (D141, after verify-71). R42 now says that a save whose read-back fails takes back the head record it published, when that record is still its own, leaving its entry past the recorded entries (D141, after verify-70). R43 and P15 now say that a reader's read counts only while the lock files it holds are still the case's, and that a read across one removed or replaced is taken again (D141, after verify-70). No other requirement changes. The owner built all four before this revision, and none is reviewed yet: T03's 5c9a5bd and 2c72469, and T12's f5436bb. The design takes in verify-70 and verify-71 and D137 to D141. It asked the three questions that D142 to D144 answered.

Earlier revisions. Revision 15 (2026-11-20, after D136) wrote D132 and D136 into R4 and R42: cash paid-in wording and a payment-method cue never take each other in, whichever list holds the longer phrase, a lookahead or lookbehind in a v2 pattern is an exception refused at load, and a writer's re-check before each step covers the gate as well as the case lock. It took in verify-68 and verify-69 and asked the question that D137 answered. Revision 14 (2026-11-20, after D131) wrote D125 to D127 into R4 and R42: cash paid-in wording is never taken in by a payment-method cue's longer phrase, no v2 rule carries an unless or a requires, a save is told recorded only after reading back an intact log that holds its entry, and a repair re-checks the log and the head record before its renames. It took in verify-66 and verify-67 and asked the question that D132 answered. Revision 13 (2026-11-20, after D124) wrote into R42 and P15 one save at a time per holder and D103's repair boundary. A writer refused after its line reports its save as not recorded, two saves told recorded at one log position leave a log reported broken, and under lock-file interference a log that reads intact holds every save told recorded. It took in T03's reviews up to verify-65, found a schedule the boundary did not yet cover at b428d56, and asked the three questions that D125 to D127 answered. Revision 12 (2026-11-19, after D112) wrote D108 and D109 into R42, R43 and P15: a writer re-checks its lock before each step that changes a file a reader reads (the empty head record, the line and each rename), and a steady read gives the recorded entries only when one writer held the case through it, so a reader that finds the gate busy and the case lock held names only a fault at or before the recorded entries. It took in the owner's D108 work for T12 and D110's answer for T03, and raised no question. Revision 11 (2026-11-19, after D106) wrote into R4 that a returned-payment code the bank prints at the start of the line settles the line type before the longer-phrase rule (D101), and that a match's length is the longest any alternative of the rule's pattern or names can make where it starts (D102). It took in D100 for T03 and D105 for T12, and asked the three questions that D108 to D110 answered. Revision 10 (2026-11-19, after D99) stated in R42 and P15 that the case lock files, the gate and the case lock beside the log, are the tool's own: removing or replacing either while the case is in use is outside the single-writer guarantee, and two appends that such interference lets through leave a log reported broken, never intact (D98, D99). It took in T12's gate (D95, D98), which replaced the probe after the read and the hold counts, and T03's table checks (D94, D96, D97), and asked the three questions that D101 to D103 answered. Revision 9 (2026-11-19, after D88) made a credit that both a director's and an own account's names match, with no protected verdict, ambiguous whatever the table finds, with every rule it matched and both profile kinds as candidates (D86, D90, D91). It also took in D85 and D87 for T12: checks share the lock on Windows too, a recorded entry cut short and a missing head record are damage, and being saved stands only while another writer still holds the case after the read. Revision 8 (2026-11-19, after D82) wrote into R5 and R6 the precedence C1 has held since revision 3, a protected verdict deciding a line before a case-profile name in either direction (D81, D83), and took in D80 for T12: a check holds the case's lock shared, and a writer waits for checks. Revision 7 (2026-11-19, after D79) let only a per-line override or an own-account pairing settle an ambiguous credit, a matching case-profile name adding one more candidate (D78), named cheque wording beside the rail as a payment-method cue in R4's text (D76), and took in verify-42 for T12 under D74 and D75: a reader takes the lock first and says intact only after finding the case free. Revision 6 (2026-11-19, after D74) gave R43 and P15 a simpler reader rule (judge only the recorded entries from a read the head record held still across; the rest is being saved) and took in D70-D72 for T03 (the cheque as a payment-method cue, separators inside listed names, advance wording on the loan list, separator-only profile values). Revision 5 (2026-11-19, after D65) stated the case log's concurrency scope where R42, R43 and P15 sit (one writer at a time per case, no fork while the lock is held, case folders on a local disk) and took in D64 for T03 (a listed phrase is found wherever it starts; a combining mark joins its word). Revision 4 (2026-11-18, after D60) moved R4's queue clause to R15, so that T03, which builds the categoriser, can complete R4; it also put the rail-plus-cash check ahead of the cues, made words whole in any script, and declared the category order inside a bucket. Revision 3 (after D52) gave R4 its ambiguity policy; the owner kept its defaults (D53, D54) and approved it (D55). Revision 2 (2026-11-16, after D34) counted cash deposits as trading receipts and flagged the large ones (D31), showed pass/refer/fail with an illustrative label (D28), kept timing out of the tool (D32) and wrote the pack once, at sign-off. The owner confirmed local-first (D29), OBIE v3.1 and TrueLayer first (D30) and Tier 1 only by 2026-11-29 (D33).

## Context

**The bet as signed.** One underwriter at a UK lender (D1) reads months of business bank statements by hand before writing each credit memo. The firm bans generic AI, and the friend's fear is a made-up number on a file they sign. The owner chose to treat this as a showcase for one firm (D15, D24): build a working model, show it, then ask the firm's approver for a pilot (D14, D25). The demo runs on the owner's own statements, as a demonstration of the mechanism rather than of the time saving (D23).

**What the evidence changed.** The underwriter's written reply of 2026-11-16 is one anecdotal source and possibly templated (evidence-synthesis.md). It moves the value from adding up to judging:
- 25-45 of a 45-60 minute review goes on 'unusual transactions, internal transfers, director loans, cash deposits'. Adding up takes 10-20.
- They want to click any number and see its transactions, correct categories themselves, get a summary they can paste into their memo, and keep the lending decision.
- The pilot blockers they expect are data protection and accuracy. A tool that runs on the firm's own machine 'could be attractive', and IT/compliance must approve it.
- Formats: an estimated 9 of 15 files are PDF, 4 Open Banking and 2 mixed. This is an estimate, not a count.

**What the owner decided on revision 1** (D28-D35, 2026-11-16)
- D31: cash deposits count as trading receipts, and deposits above a threshold are flagged. Revision 1 kept every cash deposit out of trading receipts until a person moved it.
- D28: pass/refer/fail is shown, labelled as illustrative thresholds, not the firm's policy. Revision 1 hid it unless the firm loaded its own policy.
- D32: no timing in the tool; files are timed with a stopwatch. R49 is withdrawn and R50 reports no durations.
- D29, D30, D33: the design defaults stand: local-first, OBIE v3.1 and TrueLayer before Plaid or Yapily, Tier 1 only by 2026-11-29.
- D35: the shipped cash limits are £2,500 for a single deposit and 50% of a month's trading receipts.

**What T03's first four reviews showed, and what the owner decided** (D46-D52, 2026-11-18)
- T03 builds the v2 tables and the categoriser for R4. It failed review four times. Each round fixed the cases found, and the next round found a new pair of lists where rule order, or one rule's exception, picked a bucket without telling anyone. First, CAPITAL in a savings account's name or a merchant's name made money director funding (D46). Next came a counter deposit at a branch with CAPITAL in its name, a bare TAX POT label in a refund, and SAVINGS in a compensation payer's name (D47). Last came HMRC credits with savings wording, cash at a branch whose name holds a generic explained word, a bare pot label in an external payment, and a refund from a merchant named with savings wording (D49).
- D46: classify in two stages, explicit line-type wording before the name lists, and the line-type cue wins.
- D47: a line whose matching rules span two or more buckets goes to the judgement queue with every candidate recorded.
- D48: bare words are not evidence. CAPITAL counts only in phrases (capital introduced, injected or contributed). A move between own accounts needs move wording or an account named in the profile, so bare SAVINGS stays unexplained. Generic explained words are a name list below explicit cues.
- D52 puts the policy into R4: a credit whose wording is ambiguous or conflicting goes to the judgement queue and is never counted as trading receipts.
- The lesson: the defect was never one missing word. The categoriser settled disagreement between lists by order, and each fix steered one more pair. Revision 3 makes disagreement a verdict of its own, which the underwriter sees.

**What T03's fifth review showed, and what the owner decided** (D53-D60, 2026-11-18)
- D53 and D54 kept revision 3's defaults: cues that agree outrank names, and only an override, a profile name or a pairing settles an ambiguous credit, never a firm rule. D55 approved revision 3, and D49 sent T03 back to rebuild the verdict as C1 said.
- The fifth attempt (verify-32) passed 644 tests and failed R4 on four major findings. A cue with a bucket returned before the rail-plus-cash check, so BACS CREDIT HMRC CASH PAID IN became an HMRC repayment. Candidates came only from the stage that decided, so FPS CREDIT HMRC TO SAVINGS STRIPE recorded the savings and HMRC rules but not the rail or the gateway. `cma run` showed no judgement queue. Word boundaries counted only ASCII letters, so STRIPE inside CAFÉSTRIPE LTD counted as trading receipts. A minor finding added that the category inside the trading bucket followed rule order, and the round-trip check reads the category, so reordering rules could move a credit out of M2.
- D58 and D60: R4 asks only for what the categoriser does: mark the credit, place it in unexplained money in, and record every rule it matched in either stage. Listing it in the judgement queue moves to R15, whose task builds the queue, and R15 now also shows each candidate's bucket.
- Revision 4 settles the other findings in C1: the rail-plus-cash check runs before the cues decide, words are whole in any script, and a declared category order picks the category inside a bucket. A queue in `cma run`'s terminal review is out of scope.

**What T03's sixth review showed, and what the owner decided** (D64, 2026-11-19)
- The sixth attempt (verify-35) passed 688 tests, with the rail-plus-cash check, stage precedence and both-stage candidates in place, and failed R4 on two major findings. A rule's phrases were searched one after another without overlap, so a longer phrase that starts inside a shorter match of the same rule was missed: in FROM POT TRANSFER CASH DEPOSIT, FROM POT hid POT TRANSFER, and the leftover TRANSFER counted as a payment rail and made the credit ambiguous. And word boundaries treated combining marks outside a few hard-coded ranges as separators, so STRIPE after a Devanagari vowel sign counted as a gateway and as trading receipts. Revision 4's own wording (a boundary is anything that is not a letter or a digit) allowed the second. A minor finding added that debit categories still follow rule order, which the design intends.
- D64: the owner took T03 over: find every place a listed phrase starts, overlaps included, and decide word boundaries with Unicode's own character categories, a combining mark joining the word. C1 now says both, and says that the rule-order shuffle covers credits only.

**What T03's reviews after revision 5 showed, and what the owner decided** (D70-D72, 2026-11-19)
- T03 is with the owner (D64), and each take-over was reviewed. Each review found one more way the bank's spelling hid a list. verify-37: the cash rule's exception for cheque wording hid explicit cash wording, so BACS CREDIT CASH DEPOSIT REF CHEQUE BOOK INV 42 counted £500 as a customer transfer in M2 instead of being ambiguous. verify-38: FASTER-PAYMENT CASH DEPOSIT counted as cash in M2, because the hyphen hid the rail; and SUM UP CASH ADVANCE counted as card takings, because the lender phrase is spelt SUMUP. verify-39 (710 tests passed): an en dash in FASTER–PAYMENT still hid the rail; and a profile value of only separators (---) became an empty name that settled an ambiguous credit as an own-account transfer.
- D70: cheque wording is a payment-method cue with no bucket, like the rail; a cheque with cash paid-in wording is ambiguous; the cash rule's hidden 'unless cheque' exception goes.
- D71: listed names match whatever spacing, hyphens or dots stand between their letters (SUMUP = SUM UP = SUM-UP); generic advance wording joins the loan wording, so a card provider with advance wording is ambiguous, never trading. Unlisted wording stays unexplained (R7).
- D72: every Unicode dash, the minus sign and every invisible format character separate like a space; a profile value of only separators names no one and matches nothing.
- Two minor findings recurred in all three reviews. The table validator accepts a pattern with a global inline flag, such as (?i)INV[0-9]+, which loads and then fails on the first credit it matches, because the matcher wraps it. And the producer claimed the rule-order shuffle for debits, which take the first matching rule by design.
- The lesson: one separator set, applied to every listed name and profile name, carries the bank's typography, so no list has to hold every spelling.

**What T03's ninth review showed, and what the owner decided** (D76, D78, D79, 2026-11-19)
- After D72, the ninth review (verify-41) passed 723 tests and failed R4 on one major finding. A profile name found anywhere in the description settled an ambiguous credit: FPS CREDIT ACME LTD REF CASH DEPOSIT FOR JANE DOE, with Jane Doe a director in the profile and ACME LTD the payer, became director funding with profile:director, and its rail and cash candidates were dropped. A bank line has no separate payer field, so a director's name in a reference cannot be told from the payer's name. The bucket was non-trading, so M2 was safe, but the disagreement left the judgement queue without anyone seeing it.
- Three minor findings. Debit categories change when two unprotected rules swap places (PAYROLL RENT), which the design intends; the producer claimed the shuffle for every category. A pattern with a global inline flag, (?i)INV[0-9]+, still loads and then fails on the first credit it matches, the fifth review in a row to find it (verify-37 to verify-41). And the producer claimed compileall over evals and tests while the runner compiled src only.
- D78: profile names do not settle an ambiguous credit. A matching profile name adds its bucket as one more candidate; only a per-line override, or an own-account pairing (R8, matched by amount and date, not text), settles it. Credits the table does not find ambiguous still follow R5 and R6. The owner took T03 over with that fix.
- D76, answering revision 6's question, and D79: name cheque wording beside the rail in R4's text, as a cue with no bucket, and amend R4, R5 and R6 only as far as they must; keep every other requirement, property and id. D75 answered revision 6's other question: `cma case check` exits 4 whenever the case is being saved. D77 approved revision 6.
- The lesson: a name in a bank description says who is mentioned, not who paid. A profile name is no better evidence than a listed name, so it joins the disagreement instead of settling it; what settles one is a person, or a match on amount and date in the applicant's own accounts that no reference can fake.

**What the owner decided on revision 7** (D81, D82, 2026-11-19)
- Revision 7 asked whether R5 and R6 should say what C1's precedence has said since revision 3 and T03 builds and tests: a protected verdict decides a credit before a case-profile name does (tests/test_rules_v2.py:335-346 in the T03 worktree). Read by their text, R5 put every credit naming a director in director funding and R6 every line naming an own account in own-account transfers, so REFUND JANE DOE would be director funding. T05, which builds R5 and R6, would then be judged against a rule the categoriser does not follow.
- D81: keep the precedence, and say it in R5 and R6. D82: say it in this revision, keep revision 7's other changes and every id.
- R6 covers lines in either direction, and T03 reads the protected rules before an own account's name on a debit as well (src/cma/categorise.py:580-585 in the T03 worktree; tests/test_rules_v2.py:438-449). Revision 8 wrote the exception for both directions, and the owner confirmed it (D83) and approved revision 8 (D84).
- The lesson: a requirement is judged by its own text, so an exception the build relies on belongs in the requirement, not only in the design's precedence.

**What T03's tenth review showed, and what the owner decided** (D86, D88, 2026-11-19)
- The tenth review (verify-44) passed 724 tests and failed R4 on one major finding. An ambiguous credit that matched both a director's and an own account's name recorded only the director candidate, because the director match came first and ended the search: FPS CREDIT CASH DEPOSIT REF JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS gave v2:conflict:payment_rail+cash_paid_in+profile:director. Three minor findings: README.md:78 still says a profile name settles an ambiguous credit; the producer claimed the rule-order shuffle for debits (PAYROLL RENT), which take the first matching rule by design; and the producer claimed compileall over evals and tests while the runner compiles src only. The last two recur from earlier reviews.
- The finding pointed at a wider gap. When the table found a credit unambiguous, R5 before R6 settled a credit both names match as director funding. That was an order between two lists, the silent choice D52 ended for the table's own lists.
- D86: an ambiguous credit records every kind of profile name it matches, and a credit whose counterparty matches both a director (R5) and an own account (R6) goes to the judgement queue with both as candidates; the owner took T03 over with that fix. D88: amend R5 and R6 only as far as needed, and keep every other requirement, property and id.
- The owner's D86 change recorded, for a credit the table did not find ambiguous, only the rule that would have decided. D90 answered revision 9's question: record every rule matched in either stage, as the candidates term says, and T03 changed that line (e24f466). D91: R5 and R6 point to R15 for the queue. D92 approved revision 9.
- The lesson is D52's again, one level up: an order between two lists is a choice nobody sees, whether the lists ship with the tool or come from the case profile.

**What T03's reviews after revision 9 showed** (verify-48, verify-50, verify-51, verify-53; D94, D96, D97, 2026-11-19)
- verify-48 reviewed the D90 change (e24f466) and failed R4 on one major: a pattern that compiled at load could still fail in the form that finds matches at every position, on the first credit it met. D94: every pattern form a line will meet is compiled when the table loads, and a failure refuses the table by rule id.
- verify-50 reviewed D94 (d02e25f) and failed R4 on two majors. Wrapping a pattern to find matches at every position renumbers its groups, so a back-reference silently changed meaning and the recorded conflict with it. And any line-type cue anchored at the start of the line gained the bank-code exception and skipped the ambiguity check. D96: the bank-code exception is an explicit flag, allowed only on the returned-payment rule; v2 tables refuse back-references and conditional groups.
- verify-51 reviewed D96 (2b533d3) and failed R4 on two majors. Ordinary returned-payment wording could still take the flag. And a rule's pattern and names, joined in one alternation, found only the first part that matched at a start, so a longer listed name was missed. D97: the flag is allowed only on a bank return code listed in code (^UNPAID DD), alone; a rule's pattern and names are scanned apart and the longest match at each start is kept.
- verify-53 reviewed D97 (d507393; 732 tests passed) and failed R4 on two majors, both from table edits. First, a listed phrase that begins with the bank's code (UNPAID DD TO SAVINGS added to the own-pot list) took in the code as a match inside a longer phrase, so the credit became an own-account transfer, not a returned payment. R4 says both that the bank's code settles the line type on its own and that a match inside a longer listed phrase counts only as the longer phrase; this edit makes the two meet. Second, a pattern whose two alternatives start at the same place (ACME, and ACME FASTER PAYMENT LTD) still returned its first alternative, so the rail inside the longer one survived and made the credit ambiguous. Four minors: README.md:78 still says a profile name settles an ambiguous credit, the sixth review to find it; a returned payment worded RETURNED DD is not linked to the debit it undoes, because its payee keeps the returned wording; and a timing figure and the compileall scope in the claim are not proven by the runner.
- T03 had used its attempts, and verify-53 went to the owner (next section).
- The lesson is D52's, one level down: order inside a pattern is as silent as order between rules, and a listed phrase can reach into the bank's own code. Each needs the design, not the order, to say which reading counts.

**What the owner decided on revision 10, and what T03's take-over does** (D100-D104, D106, 2026-11-19)
- D101: the bank's code settles the line type before the longer-phrase rule, and R4 should say so. D102: a match's length is the longest any alternative of the rule's pattern or names can make at its start. D103: a repair under lock-file interference is stated in the design only. All three were the design's defaults, chosen under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19, and D104 approved revision 10. D106 asked for D101 and D102 in R4 and kept every other requirement, property and id; revision 11 does that.
- D100, the owner's take-over of T03 (b6494fd, not yet reviewed), builds both. The bank-code rule is never counted as inside another rule's longer match (src/cma/categorise.py:625-627), so step 2 still finds it (:670-672). When the table loads, the matcher marks each rule part whose pattern has alternatives or a lazy repeat (:557-584). At each start it tries that part's latest possible end first, forcing the part to end there and judging its word edges against the real text on both sides. So it keeps the longest match any alternative can make (:587-611). Each of verify-53's table edits is a test with the R4 outcome (tests/test_rules_v2.py:1668-1693). The standard library does it, so there is no new dependency. It costs up to one extra match attempt per character after each start, on parts with alternatives, and descriptions are short.
- Still open at b6494fd: README.md:78 says the profile names the payer, as six reviews have found. And reversal linking still sets aside a fixed prefix list that has UNPAID DD but not RETURNED DD (src/cma/categorise.py:767-780), so RETURNED DD IWOCA LTD does not link to DD IWOCA LTD (verify-53's minor). C1 says what v2 should do, and R14's returned-DD indicator reads the link.
- D102 makes one more table edit matter. A match is now as long as its pattern can make it, and R4 counts a match inside a longer one only as the longer one. So a v2 pattern with a repeat that can match across a space (.*) would take in every other list's wording after its start. No shipped pattern has one. Of the ten patterns in src/cma/rules/v2.yaml at b6494fd, only the invoice reference repeats without limit, and only whitespace and digits. A table edit could add one, and verify-53's two majors were table edits (revision 11 asked the owner; D110 refuses such a pattern at load).
- T03 waited for revision 11 before its next review, with D110's validator check to build first; ef057ad built it (the section on T03's reviews after revision 11, below).

**What T12's reviews up to revision 5 showed, and what the owner decided** (D50-D65, 2026-11-18 to 2026-11-19)
- T12 builds the hash-chained case log and its single-writer lock for P15; R42 and R43 complete later, when sign-off (T27) and the other writers record into it. Detection passed from the start. Every failed round was about concurrency, and each found a new corner: a lock error read as another writer, which excused a torn entry (D50); a failed unlock left the lock held (D51); a forked child entered, or released, its parent's lock (D56, D57, D59); then, in the fifth review (verify-34), concurrent forks raced inside the fork hooks themselves, and a second handle opened through a symlinked folder took its own thread's lock for another writer's and reported a torn log as intact.
- D63: scope it, simplify, take over. Forking a process while it holds the case-log lock is not supported, because the tool never forks, and the fork machinery is removed. A child writing through an inherited CaseLog is refused, and leaving a writer block it inherited releases nothing. Locks are recognised by the lock file itself (its device and inode), not by the path's spelling, so a symlinked path is the same case.
- D65: state that scope where R42, R43 and P15 sit, without changing their ids: one writer at a time per case across windows, commands, threads and separate CaseLog objects in one process; a reader never excuses a torn or tampered log as another writer's append; no fork while the lock is held; case folders on a local disk.
- The lesson is T03's again: when a requirement leaves its scope open, each review explores the next corner of it. Writing down what is supported, and what is not, ends the search, and the corners outside it become stated limits.

**What T12's reviews after revision 5 showed, and what the owner decided** (D69, D73, D74, 2026-11-19)
- verify-36, after D63, failed P15 on three majors. A thread holding the lock only to check made a torn entry a crash left look like a live append, and a CLI test expected exit 0 for it. After exhausting its retries, a reader judged what was recorded from the head it read before its last log read, so damage to an entry the current head records was excused. And a forked child wrote through an inherited CaseLog whose lock was free at the fork. D69: the holder records in the lock file when it is mid-append, and only a holder mid-append excuses; a contended reader decides from the latest head; a CaseLog belongs to the process that opened it.
- verify-40, after D69, failed P15 on three majors and a minor, all in the reader. The mid-append mark could not tell one append from the next by the same holder, so a committed entry changed between the reader's reads passed as intact with an append in progress. On exhausted retries, an entry removed after it was recorded was cut away with the head, and the log returned intact. A live repair (dropping a torn entry) read as incomplete. And the CLI test for a live append never paused a real append.
- D73 and D74: a simpler reader rule, written into R43 and P15. While another writer holds the case, a reader judges only the recorded entries, from a read across which the head record did not change, and reports the rest as being saved; with no such read it claims nothing. The holder and any reader of a free case report an incomplete last entry as incomplete. The append and drop marks are removed.
- The lesson: each round taught the reader to tell one more kind of writer apart, and each mark it read brought a new race. The new rule claims less: it never says intact or broken about what it did not see held still, so there is no writer state for it to misread.

**What T12's review after revision 6 showed** (verify-42, 2026-11-19)
- The owner's D73 take-over passed the detection, lock, conflict and CLI contract tests and failed P15 on three majors and two minors. A reader returned a verdict on the recorded entries from a read across which the head record changed, on its first read and again after contention. A steady read with nothing after the recorded entries returned intact without testing the lock, so `cma case check` exited 0 with 'case log intact' while another writer held the case. And a lock that could not be tested, with reads that never held still, came back as being saved, and the CLI said another window or command was saving. The minors: the producer claimed exit 0 for a live append where the contract test expects 4, and claimed owner-only access that `cma case check` does not check (R31 covers the case root).
- The first and third departed from C3 as written. The second was C3's own gap: revision 6 let a steady read with nothing after the recorded entries stand without asking whether anyone held the case, while D74 says a reader judges only the recorded entries while anyone holds it, and D75 that exit 0 means a whole intact log.
- Since verify-42 the reader takes the lock first, as revision 7's C3 says: it says intact only after finding the case free and reading it under the lock, and a writer waits a moment for a reader's check instead of being refused at once. R43 and P15 do not change.
- The lesson: claiming less needs the holder known first. A reader can call a whole log intact only when nobody else holds the case, so it asks that before it reads, not after.

**What the owner's T12 work after revision 7 does** (D80, 2026-11-19)
- The owner's D80 take-over (a65685d, reviewed as verify-43) makes a check a shared holder of the case's lock where the platform has shared locks: checks never exclude each other, a writer that finds only checks holding the case waits for them to finish, and one that finds another writer waits a moment and is then refused. On Windows a check's hold stayed exclusive and brief until D85. R42 already reads a check as no writer (a case-log writer is anything that appends), so R42, R43 and P15 do not change; C3 and the case log contract say it.

**What T12's reviews after revision 8 showed, and what the owner decided** (verify-43, verify-45, verify-46; D85, D87, D89, 2026-11-19)
- verify-43 reviewed D80 and failed P15 on three majors. A recorded last entry whose line was cut short was named missing instead of incomplete. A check that found no lock file, and then a steady empty log, said intact although a writer had since created and taken the lock. And on Windows a check held the lock exclusively, so a second check that ran out of retries took the first for a writer and reported being saved over an incomplete entry. D85: a recorded entry with a cut-short line is named incomplete, as damage, and never offered for dropping; a check that read without a lock file probes again if a writer took the lock meanwhile; Windows takes shared locks through LockFileEx, so checks share the case there too.
- verify-45 reviewed D85 and failed P15 on two majors. A writer that let go between the reader's probe and its read left an incomplete entry that the reader still reported as being saved. And when the head record of a one-entry log was lost, its only entry looked like a torn first append and could be dropped. D87: a reader that saw the case held probes again after its read, and names an incomplete entry if the holder let go; the first append writes an empty head record before its line, so a missing head record beside any entry is damage, never a torn write to drop.
- verify-46 reviewed D87 (fe880a9) and failed P15 on one major: when every retry saw the case held at the probe and free after the read, the final fallback still reported being saved, so a free case's incomplete entry went unnamed. D89: a case that changed hands on every attempt gets no verdict.
- The lesson is revision 7's: a reader's answer must rest on what it knows about the holder after its read, not before.

**What T12's reviews after revision 9 showed, and what the owner decided** (verify-47, verify-49, verify-52; D93, D95, D98, D99, 2026-11-19)
- verify-47 reviewed D89 (c74f2b1) and failed P15 on one major: the probe before the read and the probe after it could see two different writers' holds, with the case free between them, so a reader reported a read of a free case with an incomplete last entry as being saved and named nothing. D93: writers count their holds in the lock file, and being saved stands only when one hold lasted through the read.
- verify-49 reviewed D93 (7b7b7a5) and failed P15 on one major: a second writer took the operating-system lock before it raised the count, so two holds could share one count and the same mistake returned. A minor added that a malformed later line could be named before an earlier entry the head record contradicts. D95: a gate. A writer passes it alone and briefly to take the case; a reader holds it shared through its read, so no writer can take the case while a reader reads, and a writer that holds the case when the reader looks held it throughout until it let go. The counts went, and the lock file again holds no data. In the same take-over a missing case lock file behind the gate is read as free, and a writer whose lock file was replaced stops before writing.
- verify-52 reviewed D95 (d2c52f8) and failed P15 on two majors. A writer paused while it held the gate, before it took the case, made every reader attempt look contended although the case was free, so a torn entry went unnamed. And a writer that had re-checked its lock file could still append after someone replaced the file and a second writer had taken the new one, so both appended at the same position; the next check reported the log broken at that entry. Three minors recurred: the producer claimed exit 0 with 'appending' for a held torn tail where the contract test expects 4 and 'being saved'; a stress test was claimed to prove an interleaving it does not schedule; and owner-only access was claimed for `cma case check`, which relies on the case root's permissions (R31) and checks no owner.
- D98: a writer paused at the gate does not stop a check reading the free case. A check that finds the gate busy tries the case lock shared and, if it gets it, reads holding it (b5a85e4, reviewed as verify-54). D99: the lock files are the tool's own; removing or replacing them while the case is in use is outside R42's single-writer guarantee, as hand-editing the log is. A writer re-checks its lock before each write and refuses if it was removed or replaced; if such interference still lets two writers append, the log they leave is reported broken (R43), never intact.
- T12 had used its three attempts; D98's take-over went to review as verify-54 (next section). R43 did not change.
- The lesson is revision 5's again, for the files the tool keeps beside the log. No check of a file's identity can be joined to the next write, so a check and a write always leave a window. The tool's lock files order its own writers. Someone who deletes them is editing the case folder by hand, and what shows that is the hash chain, not the lock.

**What T12's reviews after revision 10 showed** (verify-54, verify-55; D105, 2026-11-19)
- verify-54 reviewed D98 (b5a85e4) and failed P15 on two majors. A check found a writer paused at the gate and another writer holding the case, and then skipped the recorded entries on every attempt. So a changed entry 1, under a head record that held still, went unnamed. And an append re-checked its lock file before its line but not before its head record. Three minors: the claimed exit 0 with 'appending' and the claimed owner-only access for `cma case check` recurred, and the stress test does not arrange the schedule the claim gave it.
- D105: with the gate and the case both held, a steady read still names a fault at or before the recorded entries, and the lock file is re-checked before the head record too (6e73302).
- verify-55 reviewed D105 and failed P15 on three majors. First, with the gate and the case both held and a steady read showing nothing wrong at or before the recorded entries, the check still claims nothing (src/cma/case.py:483-488, :499-500). The reviewer reads R43 as asking for the recorded entries and the rest as being saved (revision 11 asked the owner; D109). Second, the first append writes its empty head record and then its line with no re-check between them (:818-831). Third, a re-check comes before a head record's temporary file is written, not before the rename that publishes it. A repair's re-check likewise comes before its temporary log, not before the rename over the log (:761-777, :798-812). The reviewer reads R42's 'before each write' as before each step that changes a file the case holds (revision 11 asked the owner; D108). Three minors: the exit 0 claim and the stress test's schedule recurred, and the claim's diff-stat describes another revision.
- T12 is blocked, with the owner.
- The lesson: R42 says 'before each write' and R43 'a read across which the head record did not change'. Each review reads those words at the finest grain they allow, and where the design read them more coarsely, the build failed. Revision 11 asked the owner, for each, whether the build follows the words or the words follow the design; the next section gives the answers.

**What the owner decided on revision 11, and what the T12 worktree held at revision 12** (D108-D112, 2026-11-19)
- D108: R42's re-check before each write means before each step that changes a file a reader reads: the empty head record, the line and each rename; writing a temporary file is not one. Here the build follows R42's words, as verify-55 read them.
- D109: when the gate is busy and the case lock is held, a reader names a fault at or before the recorded entries from a steady read and otherwise claims nothing. A steady read gives the recorded entries only when one writer held the case through it: the reader held the gate, or found the case lock free and held it shared. Here R43's words follow the design, and what T12 has built since D105.
- D110: a v2 pattern with a repeat that can match across a space is refused at load, naming the rule; a repeat of spaces, digits or letters alone stays allowed. D111 approved revision 11. D108 to D110 were the design's defaults, chosen under the owner's standing approval of 2026-11-19.
- D112: write D108 and D109 into R42, R43 and P15, keeping their ids and every other requirement, property and id. Revision 12 does that. It also changes P15's 'any reader of a free case' to 'any reader that finds the case free', as R43 already says. A reader in D109's corner never finds the case free, so it is not a free case's reader that fails to name an incomplete entry.
- At revision 12 the T12 worktree held uncommitted work on 6e73302 that built D108, committed under D107 as 20cdbf0. A head record's rename, the empty one's included, was re-checked just before it happened (src/cma/case.py:807-812 in the worktree then), as were the line, after any empty head record (:830), and a repair's rename over the log (:776-778). Two new P15 tests replaced the lock file after the empty head record and before a repair's rename, and asserted that nothing further was published (tests/test_case_log.py:1866-1903 there), beside the earlier one that replaces it after the line (:1847-1863). The reader was as D105 left it (:413-500), which D109 keeps.
- Still open in that work: every refusal said 'nothing was written' (:693-694), including one after the line, when the line is already on disk past the recorded entries. The reader treats such a line as a torn write for the next holder to drop (:308-311). C3, C5 and Observability say the writer reports that the save was not recorded, and R42 says so since revision 13.
- At revision 12, T12 was blocked, with the owner, until that revision was approved, and T03 had no commit since b6494fd. The next two sections say what followed.
- The lesson: where the design picks the grain of a requirement's words, the words now carry that grain, so the next review judges the build against text that says it.

**What T12's reviews after revision 12 showed, and what the owner decided** (verify-56, verify-58, verify-60, verify-62, verify-64; D107, D114, D116, D118, D120, D124, 2026-11-19 to 2026-11-20)
- verify-56 reviewed the owner's D107 work (20cdbf0) and failed P15 on two majors. An append re-checked the case lock before opening the log, so a lock removed while the log was being opened still let the line through. And a rename that a Windows reader blocks is retried, but the lock was checked once, before the retries. D114: re-check after opening the log, immediately before the line, and before every rename attempt (c12251d).
- verify-58 reviewed D114 and failed P15 on two majors. A short write of the line went on in a second write call with no re-check. And with the gate and the case both held, an unreadable head record, so an unknown recorded count, let the reader name entry 1 as a fault at or before the recorded entries. D116: re-check before every write call, and name a busy-case fault only within a head record that was read (ff14ebd).
- verify-60 reviewed D116 and failed P15 on two majors. Writers staged the head record under one shared temporary name, so a writer that had lost its lock could still write, through its open descriptor, to a file another writer had renamed into place. And a thread's identifier is reused once the thread ends, so a new thread passed for one whose writer block still held the case. D118: each write stages in a temporary file of its own, and holders are told apart by a per-thread number that is never reused (926dc3e).
- verify-62 reviewed D118 and failed P15 on two majors. A busy gate with no case lock file behind it ended as being saved, though no attempt had seen the case held. And an interrupt after the case lock was taken and before writer() had noted it left the case locked with no writer. D120: a busy gate alone is never a save, and writer() releases by depth, only the level its own entry added, for the identity that entered (1ff4112). A minor added that a failed staging write leaves its temporary file, which holds case history.
- verify-64, the twentieth review, reviewed D120 and failed P15 on two majors. First, a save begun inside another on the same thread, as a signal handler can begin one, was admitted by the writer lock, which belongs to the thread, and both saves committed at one position. Second, with the case lock file replaced, A wrote its line and was refused before its head record; B took the new lock, dropped A's entry as torn and appended, and the log read intact although both had appended. Four minors: the claimed exit 0 with 'appending' (exit 4 with 'being saved' is right) and the stress test's schedule recurred, a failed staging write or fsync still leaves its temporary file, and the claim's diff-stat describes an earlier patch.
- The owner's review-20 fix (b428d56, reviewed as verify-66) closes the first: one save runs at a time per holder (src/cma/case.py:745-760 there), and an append refuses if the log grew since its check (:874-877). Its two tests are marked invariants but not named p15 (tests/test_case_log.py:2060 and :2082 there), which the project's gotcha asks for.
- D124: the second is within the guarantee once R42 says so. A was refused after its line, so its save was not recorded, and B's confirmed drop lost no recorded save. Write D103's repair boundary into R42 and P15: under lock-file interference the log is reported broken, never intact, when two writers were each told their save was recorded, and a writer refused after its line reports its save as not recorded; and state one save at a time per holder. Revision 13 does that, adding 'at the same log position' (the requirements' revision note says why). D122 then sent b428d56 to review as verify-66 (the section after next).
- Still open at b428d56. A refusal after the line still says 'nothing was written' (:713-723), where R42 now asks for 'not recorded'. A failed staging write or fsync leaves its temporary file (:815-820, :844-849). A repair does not refuse when the log grew since its check (:806-829), which R42 now asks of every save. And a save is told recorded as soon as its head record's rename returns (:886-887). Revision 13 found a schedule in which that is wrong. A's re-check before its head rename passes; the lock file is replaced; B takes the new lock, drops A's line as torn and renames the repaired log; A's head record lands; B's lands over it. A was told recorded, B's head record lists B's drop entry and not A's entry, and the log reads intact without A's save. C3 closes it with a read-back and a repair's re-check, which the owner chose as D125 and built in 1d16873 (the section after next).
- The lesson is revision 10's, one level up. A check of the lock always leaves a window before its step, so what a writer may tell its caller has to rest on what it read after the step, not before.

**What T03's reviews after revision 11 showed** (verify-57, verify-59, verify-61, verify-63, verify-65; D115, D117, D119, D121, 2026-11-19 to 2026-11-20)
- D110's validator check was built before the next review (ef057ad): a v2 pattern with a repeat that can run across words is refused at load.
- verify-57 reviewed ef057ad and failed R4 on two majors. A greedy repeat could take text that a later optional part needed, so the first match at a start was shorter than the longest, and the scan looked for a longer end only on parts with alternatives or a lazy repeat. And an `unless` on the bank-code rule could switch the bank's own code off. D115: any repeat of variable length sends the scan looking for the longest end, bounded by the pattern's widest match, and the bank-code rule takes no `unless` or `requires` (9ab9c2f).
- verify-59 reviewed D115 and failed R4 on two majors. A direction that covers debits only switched the bank's code off for credits.
