And forcing a later end cannot reopen an atomic group or a possessive repeat, so the order of their alternatives still picked the match. D117: the bank-code rule must apply to credits, and v2 refuses atomic groups and possessive repeats (91571a0).
- verify-61 reviewed D117 and failed R4 on three majors. A table could keep the code's pattern and drop the flag, so the code's verdict went. Candidates are written joined by + after a colon, and an id could hold a +, so two candidate sets could read the same. And the runner's 10,000-line timing failed at 2.18 seconds. D119: a v2 table must flag a rule for every listed code, and the code's pattern never loads without the flag; v2 ids hold only letters, digits, _, . and -; and the profile names are compiled once per case, reversal linking looks debits up by amount and payee, and spans are computed only when two or more rules match, so 10,000 lines categorise in about 0.65 seconds (5683883).
- verify-63 reviewed D119 and failed R4 on three majors. The rail-plus-cash check read cash wording from the names stage only, so a table that listed cash wording as a cue let a rail and cash reach trading receipts. A pattern that can match no text, such as a lookahead alone, has no span, so the longer-phrase rule could not set it aside. And a rule id could be default, round_trip or conflict, the categoriser's own provenance. D121: cash wording in either stage meets the rail, and v2 refuses patterns that can match no text and the three reserved ids (1a29cd1).
- verify-65, T03's nineteenth review, reviewed D121 and failed R4 on two majors. First, v2 still accepts an `unless` or a `requires` on every rule but the bank-code rule, so `unless: FPS` on the cash rule drops the cash wording from FPS CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE, and the gateway makes the credit a card settlement in trading receipts. Second, the round-trip pass judged whether the table had set a line's category from its provenance text, so an override whose reference read v2:approval-7 was undone. Four minors: an unhashable rule id escapes validation as a TypeError; README.md:78 still says a profile name settles an ambiguous credit, the ninth review to find it; the 10,000-line timing covers categorising only, not the whole recompute R52 times; and the claim's compileall covers evals and tests, which the runner does not compile.
- The owner's review-19 fix (1b592b6, reviewed as verify-67) closes the second: the round-trip pass names overridden lines by id (src/cma/categorise.py:962-967 there; tests/test_rules_v2.py:1827-1835). For the first it makes cash wording never count as inside a payment-method cue's longer phrase (:750-754), so FPS CASH DEPOSIT listed on the rail list stays ambiguous (tests/test_rules_v2.py:1815-1823). That is a second exception to R4's longer-phrase clause, beside the bank's code, and R4's text did not state it. And `unless: FPS` on the cash rule still loads (:96, :622-626). Revision 13 asked the owner about both, and D123 sent 1b592b6 to review as verify-67 (the last section below).
- Still open at 1b592b6: README.md:78, the TypeError, and RETURNED DD reversal linking, whose bank prefixes (:901-903) still hold UNPAID DD but not RETURNED DD.
- The lesson is D52's, at its last remove: an exception inside a rule is a tie-break between lists that no person sees, whether it ships with the table or a table edit adds it. The design has said since D70 that no v2 rule carries one; the validator did not yet say so.

**What T12's twenty-first review showed, and what the owner decided** (verify-66; D122, D125, D128, D131, 2026-11-20)
- D128 approved revision 13, and D122 sent the owner's b428d56 to review as verify-66, T12's twenty-first. It failed P15 on two majors, both in the corner revision 13 found. First, with the case lock file replaced, an append paused just before its head rename and a confirmed repair paused before its log rename were both told that their saves at position 2 were recorded. The log then read intact, with the repair's drop entry in place of the append (src/cma/case.py:822 there). Second, a repair published from a stale check when the log grew after it looked, because the growth check covered appends only. Two minors: a refusal after the line still said nothing was written (:723 there), and the producer again claimed exit 0 with 'appending' for a held torn tail, where the contract test expects exit 4 and 'being saved'.
- D125 took revision 13's default, and the owner built it in 1d16873 (not yet reviewed). A save publishes its head record only if the head record still lists what its check read. It then reads the log and the head record back, and is told recorded only if the log is intact and holds its entry where the head record lists it (src/cma/case.py:921-937 there). Immediately before a repair renames the repaired log, it re-checks its lock, that the log has not grown and that the head record still lists what its check read (:828-838). Immediately before its head rename it re-checks that the head record still does (:867-890). A refusal at the head rename, after the line, says the save was not recorded and that the next holder confirms dropping the entry (:930-932). Four tests mark the change: a repair refused when the log grew (tests/test_case_log.py:2115), a repair that never removes a save recorded after its check (:2137), a save told not recorded when the log no longer holds its entry (:2164), and a save refused after its line saying so (:2183).
- In verify-66's first schedule, B's re-check before its log rename now finds A's head record and refuses, and the log reads intact with A's save. If B's re-check falls just before A's head rename, B's log rename can still follow A's read-back. B's re-check before its head rename then finds A's head record, and the log reads broken, never intact without A's save. In the second schedule the repair finds the log grown and refuses.
- D131: write D125 into R42, and D126 and D127 into R4, keeping everything else in revision 13; revision 14 does that. D131 asks that a repair re-check, before each rename, that the log and the head record are as its check read them. Before the head rename the log is the one the repair's own log rename published, so R42 names what each rename re-checks, as D125's record of 1d16873 does, and the read-back covers the log after.
- Still open at 1d16873. A failed staging write or fsync still leaves its temporary file (:840-845, :871-876). A refusal in the second call of a short write of the line still says nothing was written, though part of the line is on disk (:912, :731); it leaves a torn entry, as a crash does. And the six tests added since review 20 are marked invariants but not named p15 (tests/test_case_log.py:2070-2200), which the project's gotcha asks for.
- The lesson: what a writer tells its caller rests on what it reads after its last step, so the read-back is the last thing a save does.

**What T12's twenty-second review showed, and what the owner decided** (verify-68; D133, D134, D136, 2026-11-20)
- D133 approved revision 14, and verify-68, T12's twenty-second review, judged the owner's 1d16873 against it. It failed P15 on three majors. First, a first append that had checked an empty case paused just before renaming its empty head record into place. With the case lock file replaced, B took the new lock, appended entry 1 and was told it was recorded. A's rename then put an empty head record over B's, and A refused its own line because the log had grown. The next holder saw B's entry as a torn write past the recorded entries, and a confirmed repair left a log that read intact without a save told recorded (src/cma/case.py:902 there). Second, the re-check before each step compared only the case lock file, so a gate removed or replaced during a save stopped nothing (:726 there). Third, a repair's re-check before its log rename compared the log's length, not its bytes, so a recorded entry changed to the same length was overwritten by the repair's copy, and the log read intact (:837 there). Three minors: the producer again claimed exit 0 with 'appending' for a held torn tail, where the contract test expects exit 4 and 'being saved'; the stress test does not assert the interleaving claimed for it; and the claim named the read retries as the only retry cap, though a writer's wait and the Windows rename retries have their own.
- R42 already ruled out the first, a save told recorded and then lost (revision 13), and the third, since 'as the repair's check read them' means the bytes. The second turns on R42's 'its lock'. A reader that finds no gate takes the case as one no writer has started, and one that holds a new gate takes the writer it finds holding the case to have passed it. So a writer that goes on after the gate is removed or replaced writes into a case the reader judges by a file the writer never passed.
- The owner built all three in cd298cc (D134), reviewed as verify-70 (the section after next). The first empty head record is staged and then linked into place, which fails if any head record exists, so a first append from a stale check stops with a conflict and never resets a head that records entries (_create_empty_head, src/cma/case.py:888-906 there). The re-check before each step compares the gate's identity, noted when the writer passed it, as well as the case lock's (_require_lock_in_place, :732-745), and refuses with 'a case lock file was removed or replaced'. An append compares the log's bytes as well as its length before its line (:947-950), and a repair compares them before it reads the log (:836-838) and again before its log rename (:843-851). Three tests mark it: a stale first append that never resets a head (tests/test_case_log.py:2202), a repair refused when the log was rewritten to the same length (:2227), and a gate removed or replaced during a save (:2249, named p15).
- D136: state in R42 that the per-step re-check covers the gate as well as the case lock, keeping everything else in revision 14; revision 15 does that. P15 still names the case lock only, which asks less than R42 and does not contradict it.
- Still open at cd298cc. A failed staging write or fsync of a head record or a repaired log still leaves its temporary file (:912-917, :853-858); only the empty head record's staging file is always removed (:904-905). A refusal in the second call of a short write of the line still says nothing was written, though part of the line is on disk (:953, :744-745). And of the tests added since review 20, eight are marked invariants but not named p15 (tests/test_case_log.py:2070-2244), which the project's gotcha asks for.
- A hard link needs a file system that has them. NTFS, APFS and the usual Linux file systems do; FAT and exFAT, common on USB sticks, do not, and there a first append fails with the file system's error and writes nothing (Failure modes).
- The lesson is revision 13's, at the first step: a step that publishes must refuse to overwrite what another writer published, and the first step publishes too.

**What T03's twenty-first review showed, and what the owner decided** (verify-69; D132, D135, D136, 2026-11-20)
- verify-69, T03's twenty-first review, judged the owner's 4adddf9 against revision 14 and failed R4 on two majors. First, the corner revision 14 asked about: with FPS CASH DEPOSIT added to the cash list, the rail inside it counted only as the cash phrase, and FPS CASH DEPOSIT was a cash deposit recorded as v2:cash_paid_in (src/cma/categorise.py:762 there). Second, the loader refused an unless or a requires but accepted the same exception written into the pattern. A cash rule whose pattern was KIOSK CASH DEPOSIT followed by a negative lookahead for STRIPE, with CASH DEPOSIT off the cash names, loaded, and FPS KIOSK CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE skipped it and reached the gateway as a card settlement in trading receipts (:403 there). **What T12's twenty-third review showed, and what the owner decided** (verify-70; D138, D139, D141, 2026-11-20)
- D138 approved revision 15, and verify-70, T12's twenty-third review, judged the owner's cd298cc against it and failed P15 on two majors. First, a reader that held the gate shared through a steady read never checked that the gate was still the case's. With only the gate file replaced during the read, the writer that held the case let go, a second writer took the same case lock through the new gate, and both of the reader's looks at the case lock found it held. So the reader reported the recorded entries and the rest as being saved, though no one writer held the case through its read (src/cma/case.py:488 there). Second, a read-back that failed after the head record was published, with a one-shot read error, told the save it was not recorded while the head record still listed its entry. A fresh check then read the log intact with that entry, and the next holder had no torn entry to drop (:974 there). Four minors, three of them recurring: the producer claimed exit 0 with 'appending' for a held torn tail, where the contract test expects exit 4 and 'being saved'; the stress test does not force the interleaving claimed for it; owner-only access was claimed for `cma case check`, which relies on the case root's permissions (R31) and checks no owner; and the claim's suite totals and diff-stat describe an earlier patch.
- The owner built a fix for both in f5436bb, which is not yet reviewed; D139, the unblock, is pending. After each read, a reader compares each lock file it holds with what the path names now, and takes the read again if one was removed or replaced (src/cma/case.py:476-480, used at :489, :498, :505 and :509). A save whose read-back fails puts back the head record its check read, only if the head record is still the one the save published, through the same re-checked rename as every head record (_publish, :999-1011). If the take-back cannot be made, the save says so and asks for the case log to be checked before anything else is saved. Two tests mark it: a failed read-back that takes its head record back (tests/test_case_log.py:2275, marked invariants but not named p15), and a read across a replaced gate or case lock that is taken again (:2297, named p15).
- D141: write both into R42, R43 and P15, keeping everything else in revision 15; revision 16 does that. D141 says 'replaced'; R43 and P15 also say 'removed', as R42 does and as f5436bb builds, since a missing file fails the comparison too.
- Still open at f5436bb:
  - A check that finds the gate busy and the case lock free reads holding the case lock alone, and returns without comparing that lock file with its path (:527-533). R43 asks it of every lock file a reader holds since revision 16. With the case lock file replaced during that read, the writer paused at the gate can take the new case lock and append, and the reader judges the whole log with a live append in it.
  - A take-back can fail as well: the head record cannot be read or renamed, or a lock file was replaced. The save then says it was not recorded, but its entry stays among the recorded entries, so the next check reads the log intact with it. P15 says a save refused after its line leaves its entry past the recorded entries. Revision 16 asks the owner (Options considered).
  - A gate replaced before a read, while a writer that passed the old gate is paused before taking the case lock, lets that writer take the case through a gate no reader holds. Its next step refuses (R42), but the case shows as held, and a reader holding the new gate may report being saved over an entry the previous holder left incomplete. The reader's own lock files stay the case's, so it cannot see this. Revision 16 asks whether R43 and P15 should say so (Options considered).
  - A failed staging write or fsync of a head record or a repaired log still leaves its temporary file (:935-940, :876-881). A refusal in the second call of a short write of the line still says nothing was written, though part of the line is on disk (:976, :766-767). Nine tests added since review 20 are marked invariants but not named p15 (tests/test_case_log.py:2070-2275), which the project's gotcha asks for.
- The lesson is revision 13's, at the reader: what a reader may report rests on what it checks after its read, and the lock files it holds are part of that.

Tests mark both: a lock lost after part of the line (tests/test_case_log.py:2488), an operating-system error at the line, the first empty head record and a repair's rename (:2513), and the three tests that crashed a save with an OSError, now expecting not recorded (:52, :717 and :1715). The two new tests are marked invariants but not named p15.
- The major reads R42's 'a writer refused after its line ... SHALL report its save as not recorded' at its finest grain, for a writer refused partway through its line. D158 writes that into R42 and P15.
- Still open at 51c8b68:
  - The reads a save makes only to check something turn the file system's error into a value, so the answer names no error and sometimes gives the wrong cause. The append's comparison of the log's bytes with its check (:1018) and a repair's re-check before its log rename (:921) read an unreadable log as changed and refuse with a conflict that asks to reload. The head record's re-check before its rename (:995) says the head file changed. The read-back (:1067) says the log changed, and a take-back that fails (:1075) names no error. A lock file that cannot be examined is reported as removed or replaced (:795-799). R42 as revision 19 writes it asks each of these to name the error, and the conflicts to answer not recorded; revision 19 asks the owner (Options considered).
  - When a head record's staging write fails and the head record then reads as unreadable, or as another writer's under interference, _publish answers not confirmed (:1060-1066) though no rename was made; R42 asks for not recorded there, since the save made no rename.

  - Fourteen tests added since review 20 are marked invariants but not named p15 (tests/test_case_log.py:2070-2513), which the project's gotcha asks for.
- The lesson is revision 18's, one call deeper: a refusal is an answer too, and it has to say what the save left behind.

**What the owner decided on revision 19** (D159, D160, D163, 2026-11-20)
- D159 answered revision 19's question with its default: the reads a save makes only to check something name an operating-system error, and a re-check that cannot read answers not recorded, never a conflict. The owner built it in 1e35352 (next section).
- D160 approved revision 19. D163 asks for three things, and keeps everything else in revision 19. First, write D159 into R42: the reads a save makes to check something (the log comparison, the re-checks of the head record and the log, the read-back, the lock-file check) name an operating-system error, and a re-check that cannot read answers not recorded (or not confirmed after its head record was published), never a conflict to reload or a lock removed or replaced. Second, write into R42 and P15 that once any of the line is on disk, no part of a save's report says nothing was written (de6a56e). Third, write into R4 that a malformed pattern on a bank-code rule is named by its rule, and that any failure of a changed v1 is named as a changed frozen table first (5139e22). Revision 20 writes all of it.
- Three readings. 'Or not confirmed after its head record was published' is read through revision 19's one rule: a re-check in a take-back answers not confirmed, and a read-back that cannot read is a failed read-back, not recorded once its take-back is made. 'Once any of the line is on disk' is read as once any part of the line is in the log, which for a repair is once its repaired log has been renamed over the log. A repair stages its line in a temporary file that nobody reads, and a repair refused before its log rename leaves the case's files as they were, so 'nothing was written' is true of it. And 'a lock removed or replaced' is read beside R43's rule of revision 18: a lock file missing when a writer examines it was removed, so only another failure to examine it is a read that cannot be made. D163 names R4 alone for the changed v1, and R54 already says that a changed v1 is refused before anything else in it is checked, so R54 does not change.

**What the owner decided on revision 21** (D169, D192, 2026-11-20)
- D169 approved revision 21. The owner then took both tasks over after each review, ten times each, under the standing approval of 2026-11-19 (D170 to D189), and the eleventh round failed both again. D190 and D191, the unblocks it raised, are pending.
- D192 asks for two things and keeps everything else in revision 21. First, write into R43 and P15 the window D99 states for writers, now for readers and holders. A lock-file check is a sequence of looks at the file system, so a lock file removed or replaced, renamed aside and replaced included, and finished within the check's own last look is outside what any check can know. The reader or holder checks each lock file it holds, then the first again, and a holder also checks that the files it holds still have a name (523c5df); a check after the interference stops gives the true answer (D144). Second, write into R4 that only a digest that matches the pin exempts v1 data from the exception checks, that a repeated rules list has the rules of every list checked, and that a value of a repeated field that does not compile is named (4c017d4). Revision 22 writes all of it.
- Three readings.
  - 'Finished within the check's own last look' is read as a removal or replacement made after the check's last look at that file. Any removal or replacement made before a file's last look is caught by that look, whatever order the files are looked at in, and no check can see one made after it, so this is exactly what the check cannot know.
  - 'Holder' is read as a reader that holds the case, sign-off included. R43 and P15 speak of readers, and R42 already states the window for a writer's re-check before each step; T12 uses one check for both (_Hold.in_place, src/cma/case.py:438-453 at 523c5df).
  - 'A value of a repeated field that does not compile is named' is read with the rest of R4: each value is compiled, built and checked for exceptions as a key given once is, whichever value comes first. The owner's 0028830 and 792727b say 'each value of a repeated field is checked' and 'every value of a repeated field is checked and built', and R4 already names a pattern that compiles but cannot be used. The validator's other pattern-form checks (C1) reach every value too.

**What T12's reviews after revision 21 showed** (verify-82 to verify-102; D170 to D190, the even numbers, 2026-11-20)
- T12's twenty-ninth to thirty-ninth reviews each failed P15 on one or two majors, and the owner took each over (D170 to D188, the even numbers). The majors fell into three runs.
  - *A report's words after its line* (reviews 29 to 31). A nested save's refusal, which said nothing was written, became the report of a save whose line was in the log (verify-82; 90c6094). A repair interrupted after its log landed reported only the nested refusal, and a refusal during the read-back skipped the take-back (verify-84; c466717). An interruption just after a rename, or any exception during a take-back, escaped as itself or read a cleared flag (verify-86; 42e687d). Since 42e687d, whatever stops a save once its line has started to reach the log is reported as the save's own answer (_os_errors_as_not_recorded, src/cma/case.py:904-950 at 523c5df), as R42 asks.
  - *Interruptions between publishing and taking back* (reviews 32 to 36). Ctrl-C during the read-back bypassed the take-back, and Ctrl-C that stopped the take-back escaped (verify-88; 50e1e4d). Ctrl-C after a failed read-back, before the take-back began, skipped it, and GeneratorExit during the take-back escaped raw (verify-90; 13d5244). The call into the take-back stayed interruptible (verify-92), so 97b0566 holds Ctrl-C off from publishing the head record to the take-back. A custom signal handler could still interrupt it (verify-94), and b3ba156 then held off a fixed list of signals, which verify-96 found incomplete. Since ba46921 every signal whose handler is Python code is held off in that stretch (_interrupts_deferred, :1252-1291).
  - *The order of the lock-file looks* (reviews 34 to 39). A head rename was approved after the case lock was replaced, because the lock was checked before the head record was read (verify-92; 97b0566 checks the lock last before each rename). The writer's final check sampled the case lock before the gate (verify-94; b3ba156), and the holder's check after its read did the same (verify-96; ba46921). With the case lock looked at last, a gate replaced after its own look went unseen (verify-98). The owner's 2861fd7 then let the holder's read stand on its case lock alone, which P15 does not allow, and verify-100 failed it. 57984ea looked at both paths and then at the link counts of both files held, since a file renamed over has no name left. verify-102 moved the gate to another name and installed a new one, so the old file kept a name. It also found that an ordinary reader looked at the gate before the case lock and not again.
- Other majors. A reader could raise RecursionError while reporting the recorded entries of a log with deeply nested data, through a deep copy of each entry (verify-98; 2861fd7 rebuilds entry lines without one). And the nested-save guard tested and registered in two steps, so a signal handler between them could commit a nested save (verify-100; 57984ea makes it one step, _one_save, :887-902).
- Minors recurred in every review. The producer claimed exit 0 with 'appending' for a held torn tail, where the contract test expects exit 4 and 'being saved'. The rapid-append test does not force the schedule claimed for it. Owner-only access was claimed for `cma case check`, which relies on the case root's permissions (R31). A failed staging write leaves its temporary file (verify-82, verify-100). And with several entries past the recorded head, a later malformed line can be named before the first bad entry (verify-82, src/cma/case.py:325 there).
- The owner built 523c5df, not yet reviewed; D190, the unblock, is pending. Every lock-file check now looks at the first file again last. A hold's check (_Hold.in_place, :438-453) looks at both paths, then at the link counts of both files held, then at both paths again, the case lock last. It serves both the holder's read (verify, :512-523) and a writer's re-check before each step (_require_lock_in_place, :839-859). Any other reader looks at each file it holds and then the first again (:540-549). Two tests mark it, both named p15: a lock file replaced just after its path was looked at (tests/test_case_log.py:3056), and a gate moved aside and replaced after its first look (:3081).
- D192: write the window and the check into R43 and P15; revision 22 does that.
- Still open at 523c5df:
  - A save refused before any part of its line reaches the log still gets the lock check's own refusal, 'a case lock file was removed or replaced while this window held the case; the step was refused' (src/cma/case.py:858-859, re-raised at :932-933). R42 asks for not recorded there, as revision 21 named.
  - A failed staging write or fsync of a head record still leaves its temporary file (:1085-1090). A repair's staging file is now removed whatever fails (:1022-1036).
  - The link-count look sees nothing the second pass of path looks would miss, since a file with no name can never be named by its path again. It costs two status calls, and R43 keeps it as D192 asks.
  - Thirty-two tests added since review 20 are not named p15 (tests/test_case_log.py:2071-3028), which the project's gotcha asks for.
- The lesson is D99's, for readers. No order of looks closes the window after the last one, so each review moved the replacement just past whichever look came last. Stating the window ends that search, as stating it for writers did in revision 10.

**What T03's reviews after revision 21 showed** (verify-83 to verify-103; D171 to D191, the odd numbers, 2026-11-20)
- T03's twenty-eighth to thirty-eighth reviews each failed R4 on one to three majors, and the owner took each over (D171 to D189, the odd numbers). The majors fell into three runs.
  - *One rule field hiding another's fault* (reviews 28 to 31). A malformed exception kept the same rule's pattern from being built, so the two faults revision 21 asks to see together were not (verify-83). Any compile fault on a rule, or a malformed names field, skipped its pattern's matcher (verify-85). 48c5b9c builds each part of a rule on its own, and edc3ff4 builds the pattern and the names alone and then together. verify-83 also found that an invalid bank_code: true flag skipped the anchor check, and 48c5b9c lets only the bank's code text keep its anchor. A changed v1 could then reuse that text on another rule (verify-87; 59860ae) or on a debit-only copy (verify-89; bb5614a). Since bb5614a only the bank's code on its own rule, for credits, keeps its anchor.
  - *What the YAML parser builds* (reviews 30 to 35). A scalar YAML cannot build, such as an invalid date or a tagged boolean, aborted the load before any rule was named, and once caught it became ordinary text (verify-87, verify-89). Then came an unknown tag or an alias that contains itself (verify-91), a collection tagged as a scalar (verify-93, verify-95), and a key repeated inside a malformed field (verify-97). Since 2b09c8f any node YAML cannot build becomes an unbuilt value, named by rule and field, and since d3d9f41 a key given more than once keeps neither value and is named.
  - *A key given more than once* (reviews 36 to 38). Dropping both values hid a forbidden exception in either (verify-99; 0028830 checks each value). A cap of thirty-two variants could still hide one, and the matcher build skipped every value of a repeated pattern (verify-101; 792727b checks and builds every value, with no cap). Then verify-103 found three majors. A repeated rules key hid the faults of every rule in both lists. A repeated pattern whose value did not compile went unnamed. And the check of a table's data, given no digest, took rules that bore v1's id and called themselves frozen as verified.
- Other majors. A non-v1 table that called itself frozen was still checked, and had its matchers built, as v1 (verify-97, verify-99; d3d9f41 and 0028830). The form that forces a match to end at a given place was compiled on the first line it met, and then once for each new length (verify-97, verify-99; d3d9f41 and 0028830). And the matcher forms lived in a shared cache that could evict them (verify-101; 792727b keeps them on the loaded rule).
- Minors recurred in every review. README.md:78 still says a profile name can settle an ambiguous credit, the twenty-eighth review to find it. The producer claimed the rule-order shuffle for debits, which take the first matching rule by design. The claim's compileall covers evals and tests, which the runner does not compile. And the 10,000-line timing covers categorising one fixed statement, not the whole recompute R52 times.
- The owner built 4c017d4, not yet reviewed; D191, the unblock, is pending. validate_ruleset spares the exception checks only where a digest that matches the pin is given (src/cma/categorise.py:403-407). It names a repeated rules key and checks the rules of every list given (:415-423). It compiles a repeated field's values and names one that does not compile (:590-596). Three tests mark it, all contracts: a repeated rules list (tests/test_rules_v2.py:2291), a repeated pattern that does not compile (:2309), and v1-shaped data checked with no digest (:2319).
- D192: write all three into R4; revision 22 does that.
- Still open at 4c017d4:
  - The compile check for a repeated field's values (:590-596), and the matcher build after it (:597-618), sit after the loop over the rule's values (:578-589). So they see only the last value, and each part is built once for each of the three exception keys, so a fault there can be named more than once. A repeated pattern whose first value does not compile, or compiles alone but cannot be used, goes unnamed: verify-101's and verify-103's schedules with the values in the other order. The tests put the bad value last (tests/test_rules_v2.py:2263, :2309). D192's own words ask for any such value, so T03 moves those lines into the loop before its review.
  - The checks a pattern given once gets for a back-reference, a pattern that can match no text, an atomic group or a possessive repeat, and a repeat that can run across words (:501-531) read the rule as given, so none of a repeated pattern's values gets them. C1 says each value gets them. R4 does not name these checks, so the gap is against the design, not against R4.
  - Also open: README.md:78; RETURNED DD reversal linking, whose bank prefixes (:1248) still hold UNPAID DD but not RETURNED DD; the timing of reversal linking and of descriptions with many overlapping matches, which T22's timed recompute (R52) should include; and the exact candidates in the test at tests/test_rules_v2.py:1912.
- The lesson is revision 19's, carried into the parser: a validator that names every fault has to read every value the file gives it, and a key given twice gives two.

**What the owner decided on revision 22** (D193, D210, 2026-11-20)
- D193 approved revision 22. The owner then took both tasks over after each review, seven times each, under the standing approval of 2026-11-19 (D194 to D207), and the eighth round failed both again. D208 and D209, the unblocks it raised, are pending.
- D210, given in chat, accepts these as edge cases outside the guarantee, to push development forward, and asks for two scope limits, keeping everything else in revision 22.
  - R4 and R54: a rule table that is not well formed (a key given more than once, a value YAML cannot build, a pattern that does not compile, fields that contradict one another) is refused as a whole, naming the faults the loader finds. Naming every fault, or every combination of repeated values, is outside R4. The guarantee is that no such table loads or categorises a line, and that a well-formed table is checked rule by rule as R4 says.
  - R42, R43 and P15: removing, renaming or replacing a case lock file while the case is in use, and interrupting a save at an arbitrary instant (a signal, Ctrl-C, a nested save from a handler), are outside the single-writer and reader guarantees, as D99 and D144 already say for writers and readers. Under such interference the tool reports broken, being saved, not recorded or not confirmed as it can, and a check after the interference stops gives the true answer.
  Revision 23 writes both.
- Six readings.
  - 'Naming the faults the loader finds' is read as naming at least one. A reviewer reading R4 at its finest grain could otherwise ask which faults a loader 'found' and fail a refusal that names fewer, the search D210 ends.
  - 'Not well formed' is read to take in a value of a kind the table format does not allow in its field, such as a pattern given as a list, and a pattern that compiles alone but cannot be used in a form the matcher builds from it. verify-77 to verify-85 found these beside D210's examples, and the build treats them alike.
  - 'Fields that contradict one another' is read as the validator's checks of fields against each other: a line-type rule that is not protected or that comes after a names rule, a protected rule after one that is not, the bank-code flag on a rule that is not the bank's code alone for credits, and a category the category order needs and lacks (src/cma/categorise.py:513-529, :588-601 and :688 at 3ae10cc). A table's frozen flag and its id never make it not well formed, because R4 already says what they decide; R4 says so.
  - 'As it can' is read as reaching the two guarantees R42 and P15 have stated under such interference since revision 13: a log reported broken, never intact, when two writers were each told that a save at one position was recorded, and a log that reads intact holding every save told recorded. Revision 23 asks the owner (Options considered).
  - 'A nested save from a handler' is read as a way of interrupting the save the handler runs inside. R42 keeps its refusal of the nested save, which is a check at the nested save's start, not an interruption, and what is outside is what the interrupted save then does, reports or leaves behind. R42 lists a finaliser and a callback beside a signal handler, so the scope names them too. Revision 23 asks the owner.
  - 'Renaming' is added beside removing and replacing. A lock file renamed aside leaves its path naming no file or another one, which the checks already read as removed or replaced (R43 since revision 22).

**What T12's reviews after revision 22 showed** (verify-104 to verify-118; D194 to D206, the even numbers, 2026-11-20)
- T12's fortieth to forty-seventh reviews each failed P15 on one to three majors, and the owner took each over. Most majors fell into three runs, all under an interruption or a replaced lock file.
  - *Something else inside a save* (verify-104, verify-106, verify-108). A callback's conflict or broken-log refusal escaped the outer save's report after its line was written. A signal handler's unrelated refusal was chained into the outer report, saying nothing was written. The save guard registered its token before the region that removes it, so an interruption between them left later saves refused. And an OSError from a deferred handler became not recorded after a confirmed save. 4dc43f8, 46b03be and 33cfac2 answer them.
  - *A holder's retry across a replaced gate* (verify-108, verify-110, verify-112). The retry took the thread's own case lock for another writer's hold, or never went through the current gate. 33cfac2, d9926dd and 50db6d0 answer them.
  - *A save's read-back across a replaced lock file* (verify-112 to verify-118). The read-back accepted a whole-log read without checking the lock files after it. Then it discarded such a read and went straight to the take-back without the fresh read P15 asks for. Then it skipped the check on an I/O error. At 43332c6 it skips it on a KeyboardInterrupt, and the take-back, refused on the replaced lock, answers not confirmed though a fresh read would find the entry (src/cma/case.py:1269; verify-118). 50db6d0, 83f0f9d and 43332c6 answer the first three.
- Two majors fell outside those runs, and both are fixed. An append turned a check that judged nothing into a broken log at entry None (verify-104; 4dc43f8 refuses it as unchecked). A reader holding the gate named entry 1 broken with no recorded count, its head unreadable (verify-108; 33cfac2 names no entry then).
- Minors recurred in every review. The producer claimed exit 0 with 'appending' for a held torn tail, where the contract test expects exit 4 and 'being saved'. The rapid-append test does not force the schedule claimed for it. Owner-only access was claimed for `cma case check`, which relies on the case root's permissions (R31). verify-118 adds a claim that a missing head under a held case is named at entry 1, where the build rightly claims nothing.
- At 43332c6 the only major is verify-118's, which needs both a replaced lock file and an interruption at an arbitrary instant; revision 23 puts it outside P15. A save refused before any part of its line still gets the lock check's own words, 'the step was refused' (src/cma/case.py:898); that refusal comes only from a lock file removed or replaced, so it is outside R42 too.
- The lesson is D99's, carried to its end. Each guarantee stated under interference became the next review's target, and each fix added one more check that an interrupt could split. The promise that holds is the one D144 made: a check after the interference stops gives the true answer, and a signed pack pins the head it saw.

**What T03's reviews after revision 22 showed** (verify-105 to verify-119; D195 to D207, the odd numbers, 2026-11-20)
- T03's thirty-ninth to forty-sixth reviews each failed R4 on one to three majors, and the owner took each over. Every major was a fault left unnamed in a table that was refused anyway, because it had a key given more than once, a value YAML cannot build or a pattern that does not compile.
  - verify-105: the compile and build loop saw only the last value of a repeated field, field checks read the repeat's placeholder, and flattening repeated rules lists dropped values that were not lists (822ccdf).
  - verify-107 and verify-109: repeated category orders, rules lists, rule ids, table ids and frozen flags were checked through the placeholder or merged (6fbea47, c7bf44b).
  - verify-111 to verify-115: stage order, the protected flag, the category order and the bank-code checks read a repeated field's values together, or one at a time where they had to be combined (f15bbe5, c7431d4, e0468e1).
  - verify-117: a repeated pattern hid that one version of the table had no bank code, and a pattern that parsed but did not compile hid its lookaround (3ae10cc).
  - verify-119, at 3ae10cc: a repeated direction that makes one version debit-only leaves the missing code for credits unnamed beside the rule's flag and anchor faults (src/cma/categorise.py:632), and a mapping key YAML cannot hash turns the whole rule into a value YAML cannot build, so its repeated pattern and its lookahead go unnamed (:829).
- Minors recurred in every review: README.md:78 still says a profile name can settle an ambiguous credit; the producer claimed the rule-order shuffle for debits, which take the first matching rule by design; the 10,000-line timing covers categorising one statement, not the recompute R52 times; and the claim's compileall covers evals and tests, which the runner does not compile.
- Every table these reviews built was refused, and each refusal named at least one fault. Both of verify-119's majors therefore meet R4 as revision 23 writes it.
- The lesson is revision 19's, turned round: a validator that names every fault of a file that is not well formed has to model every way the file can be wrong, and each review found one more. What the underwriter needs is that no such table loads, which held throughout.

**What the owner decided on revision 23, and what the reviews against it showed** (D211 to D218, verify-120 to verify-126, 2026-11-20)

Neither changes the build. D213 approved revision 23.

**What T04's first two reviews showed, and what the owner decided** (verify-127, verify-128; D219, 2026-11-20)
- T04 builds the figures layer for R1, R2, R3, R7, P1, P2 and P6: M17, the money-in buckets, M18 and M26. Both reviews failed P2 on the same two majors, and neither is a fault of the build:
  - M8, M11, M12 and M14 report money paid out as positive amounts. Their lines are the debits, whose signed amounts total each figure negated (src/cma/metrics.py:269-270 and :397-414 at d7ec35f). They have done so since before this change, R10 keeps their values, and on the integration branch tests/test_metrics.py:90 and :92-94 assert the negation. T04's P2 test negated the totals too (tests/test_money_in.py:23-28 and :90-97), and the reviewer read P2's 'SHALL total the figure' as written.
  - M9 cites in full a credit from someone the business paid by transfer, and counts it only up to what was sent to them (src/cma/metrics.py:300-302). With £100 sent and £150 sent back, M9 is £0 and its two lines total £50.
  P2 spoke of any sum figure and never said which figures are sums.
- Their other majors are R1's, R2's, R3's and P1's, which revision 24 leaves as they are: an amount ceiling that refuses a valid whole-penny credit and withholds every bucket; M3 converted through binary floating point; a credit with no category reported as a model failure, without the pence difference; the appendix rounding each bucket to whole pounds on its own, so that the buckets shown can disagree with M17 shown; and, in verify-127, the lowest balance and the overdraft compared in floating point, and line ids read as markup in the CLI's refusal. T04 has one attempt left.
- D219: clarify P2, and R1, R11 and R16 where they cite it. A sum figure is one whose value is the total of its lines' amounts. An outflow figure (M8, M11, M12, M14 and every other figure the memo shows as money paid out) is reported as a positive amount, and the signed amounts of its lines total it negated, to the penny. A derived figure (M9, ratios, averages, counts) is not a sum figure: its evidence is every line its calculation reads, P2 does not ask those lines to total it, and M9 says in its evidence note which lines count only in part. Every money-in bucket, M17, M18 and every monthly cell is a sum figure. Keep everything else in revision 23. Revision 24 writes it.
- Three readings.
  - 'Every monthly cell'. R11's average and lowest end-of-day balance and days-overdrawn cells add no lines up, so no lines can total them. Revision 24 reads 'every monthly cell' as the cells that do: money in, money out and trading receipts. The money-out cell shows money paid out, which D219 makes an outflow figure, so revision 24 makes it one and asks the owner (Options considered).
  - 'Every line its calculation reads'. For a lowest balance (M5), the line that set it, and for an average end-of-day balance (M27), the lines that set each day's close, as M5 cites today and R12 asks. Read as every balance the calculation compares, M5 would cite every line of the statement, which helps no one check it. No figure's evidence changes.
  - 'Its evidence note'. M9's method sentence is shown in the prompt's metrics table and in the memo's appendix (src/cma/metrics.py:453 and src/cma/graph.py:170 at d7ec35f), and R30 keeps line-level data out of a prompt. So the note sits beside M9's evidence ids, in the evidence record and MCP, and names lines by id only.
- The lesson is D108's, for figures: a property about 'any sum figure' has to say which figures are sums, or a reviewer reads every GBP figure as one.

**What the owner decided on revision 24, and what T04's third review showed** (D220 to D223, verify-129, 2026-11-20)
- D220 answered revision 24's question with its default: the monthly money-out cell is an outflow figure, a positive amount whose lines total it negated, like M8. D221 approved revision 24.
- verify-129, T04's third review, failed it on R1, R2, R3, P1 and P2, with five majors and one minor. T04 has used its attempts and is blocked; D222, its unblock, is pending.
  - *A penny lost before it was counted (R2).* The loader read each amount and balance as a binary float (src/cma/ingest.py:53 there), so 10000000000000000.01 loaded as 10000000000000000.0, and the round-trip check after it validated the rounded value.
  - *A total no float carries (R1, P1).* Lines that a float each carries could add up to a money in that no float carries to the penny, and the figures layer then withheld every figure (src/cma/metrics.py:146 there).
  - *A display that rounds the binary value (R1).* Two-place formatting of a float near £1,000,000,000,000,000 printed M2 ending in .12 and M17 ending in .25 beside M25 £0.10, so the parts shown did not add up to the whole (src/cma/models.py:144 there).
  - *An early refusal (R3).* A credit with no category was refused before reconciliation, naming only such credits and their sum, so a second unbucketed credit in another category went unnamed (src/cma/metrics.py:236 there).
  - *M6's evidence (P2).* Days overdrawn reads every day's close to count the overdrawn ones, but cites only the line that closed each overdrawn day (src/cma/metrics.py:429 there). The reviewer read P2's 'every line its calculation reads' as every close. M6 has cited only the overdrawn days' lines since before this change (src/cma/metrics.py:151-153 on the integration branch), and the dual run compares that evidence with the previous release (tests/test_previous_release.py:128 and :143).
  The minor: a probe script the producer quoted was not run by the runner.
- D223: write D220, and two clarifications from review 3. R2: amounts and balances are read exactly from the statement text and counted in whole pence; an amount of £10,000,000,000,000 or more, or a statement whose money in or money out totals that much, is refused at import, named, since no figure could then be reported to the penny as the float the DTO carries (fifteen significant digits); below that, every figure is reported to the penny. P2 and 'R1/R54': M1 to M16 keep the evidence the previous release gave them, so M6 cites the line that closed each overdrawn day; the new derived figures, and the monthly balance and overdrawn cells, cite every line their calculation reads. Keep everything else in revision 24. Revision 25 writes it.
- Why £10,000,000,000,000. A decimal of fifteen significant digits or fewer reads back exactly from the nearest float. Below £10,000,000,000,000 an amount in pounds and pence has at most fifteen, and the float's error is at most a thousandth of a penny, so every amount, balance and total a statement can hold is carried exactly, and formatting it to two places gives the counted pence. M17 is at most a statement's money in, and every other money figure but one is a part of money in or money out, a difference of the two, a balance or an average of balances, so none reaches the limit. The one is average monthly revenue (M3), which can exceed M2 over a period shorter than a month; it is n/a with the reason when no float carries it (verify-128), as R2 now says. One check where a statement enters settles three majors: no penny is lost, no total is withheld and no display is a penny out.
- Four readings.
  - 'An amount'. R2 reads it as a line's amount or balance, either way. A balance that size would leave the lowest and average balances unreportable to the penny, and D223's own clause begins with amounts and balances. 0c63ca4 refuses both.
  - 'At import'. `cma run`, the MCP server and, once built, the workspace load a statement through ingest.load_csv, and every importer's output must pass it (Interfaces). R2 says the statement is refused when it is imported or loaded. `cma import monzo` converts an export without loading it, and at 0c63ca4 it still reads Amount and Balance as floats and walks the balance with round(…, 2) (src/cma/importers/monzo.py:71-78 and :186). Below the limit that loses no penny, and above it the loader refuses the file the importer writes, but the importer writes that file first, so T04 has it read exactly and refuse at the limit before its review.
  - 'R1/R54'. The requirements that keep M1 to M16 as the previous release gave them are R10, on committed cases and fixtures, and R54, under v1; P7 validates both, and the dual-run test cites R10 and R53 (tests/test_previous_release.py:116). R1 speaks only of total money in and the buckets, M17 and M19 to M25, which are new. So R10 and R54 name the evidence, and R1 does not change. R54 names the evidence only, not the values: M3 is now computed in exact pence (verify-128), so on a statement no fixture holds it can differ by a penny from the previous release's float.
  - 'Every line their calculation reads'. For a figure read from end-of-day balances, the line that set the close of every day it reads, a day with no lines taking the line that set the balance carried into it, as the end-of-day table already records it (src/cma/metrics.py:222 at 0c63ca4). So the month's days-overdrawn and lowest end-of-day balance cells cite every close in the month, unlike M6 and M5. Revision 24 read it, for a lowest balance, as the line that set it; D223's contrast with M6 replaces that for the new figures, and revision 25 asks the owner to confirm it for the month's lowest balance (Options considered). For a share or ratio, every line counted in its numerator or denominator, as M26 cites its denominator's credits at 0c63ca4 (src/cma/metrics.py:468-471).
- The owner built the limit and the display in 0c63ca4, not yet reviewed. The loader reads each amount and balance as a decimal and refuses, naming the line, one of £10,000,000,000,000 or more or one whose float does not read back as the same decimal (src/cma/ingest.py:53-67). It refuses a statement whose money in or money out totals that much, saying so (:151-155). A money figure is shown from the decimal its float reads back as (Metric.display_exact, src/cma/models.py:140-147). A comment marks M6's evidence as the previous release's (src/cma/metrics.py:287-288). Three tests, marked contracts, cover an amount refused, a total refused and a figure shown to the penny (tests/test_money_in.py:403-434).
- Still open at 0c63ca4: R3's early refusal, which still names only the credits with no category (src/cma/metrics.py:228-236), where R3 asks for the difference in pence and every line involved; and the Monzo importer's float reading.
- The lesson is D108's again, for evidence: 'every line its calculation reads', read at its finest grain, reached days M6 never cited, so the requirement now says which figures keep what the previous release cited.

**What the owner decided on revision 25, and what T04's fourth and fifth reviews showed** (D224 to D229, verify-130, verify-131, 2026-11-20)
- D224 and D225 answered revision 25's questions with their defaults. An import that would bring a case's combined money in or money out to the limit is refused, naming the account, which R41 is to say. The month's lowest end-of-day balance cites every close in the month, as R11 and P2 already say. D226 approved revision 25.
- verify-130, T04's fourth review, judged the owner's 0c63ca4 and failed it on R2, R3 and P2, with four majors. The Monzo importer still read amounts as floats and wrote a converted file past the limit (src/cma/importers/monzo.py:75 there). The appendix and the interactive table showed money figures outside the money-in buckets to the whole pound, so an M5 of £0.29 read £0 (src/cma/graph.py:170 there). Credits with no category were still refused before the full reconciliation (src/cma/metrics.py:236 there). And under v1, with ACME HOLDINGS named as an own account in a case profile, M16 no longer cited FPS CREDIT ACME HOLDINGS, which the previous release counted (src/cma/metrics.py:396 there). Four minors: the totals refusal says 'money in or money out' without saying which; a probe script's claims were not run by the runner; the claim's test counts were stale; and the committed example's appendix shows the money-in figures to the whole pound (examples/cafe_stretched.memo.md:45).
- The owner's aa04f0c (D227) read Monzo amounts in exact pence with the limit, sent credits with no category through the full reconciliation, showed every money figure to the penny in the appendix and the interactive table, and kept M16's evidence under v1 with no profile, with a dual-run test (tests/test_money_in.py:452).
- verify-131, T04's fifth review, failed aa04f0c on R2, R3 and P2, with three majors. The memo's prose quotes money to the whole pound: the drafting prompt's metrics table shows Metric.display (src/cma/graph.py:265, src/cma/metrics.py:492), and the offline model copies it (src/cma/llm.py:53), so a £0.60 Stripe payout reads £1 in the prose and £0.60 in the appendix. A debit with no category stopped the figures before the credits' reconciliation could name an unbucketed credit (src/cma/metrics.py:236 there). And verify-130's M16 schedule still held, since the dual-run test supplies no profile. Three minors: the totals refusal again, a probe script outside the repository, and a formatter assertion cited as proof of both tables.
- The owner's 74ad8af, not yet reviewed, refuses a debit with no category only after the credits' reconciliation (src/cma/metrics.py:390-392), with a contracts test (tests/test_money_in.py:480). D228, the unblock, is pending.
- D229: write D224 and D225, and two clarifications from review 5. R2: every money figure is computed in integer pence and recorded to the penny in the evidence record, the memo appendix's figure table and the interactive table; the memo's prose keeps the previous release's convention of quoting money to the whole pound, which the guard checks against the figures (the committed example and the dual run compare it unchanged), so 'reported to the penny' means the recorded and tabled figures, not the prose. R54: M1 to M16 keep the previous release's evidence for a statement categorised by v1 with no case profile, as the previous release ran; a case profile is new behaviour under any ruleset, and with one M16 and M26 exclude own-account transfers from the share they are a share of. Keep everything else in revision 25. Revision 26 writes it.
- Six readings.
  - *The prose's sources.* The memo's prose copies its figures from the metrics table in the drafting prompt (metrics.as_table, through Metric.display), and the policy check's sentence for each rule quotes a figure the same way (src/cma/policy.py:107), in the appendix's rules table and the interactive policy table. R2 counts both as prose: a prompt table to the penny would change the prose the committed example holds, and a rule sentence is prose wherever it is shown. The guard treats a figure written at the shown precision as exact (src/cma/guard.py:126-132), so prose quoted to the whole pound stays grounded.
  - *The pack's section text.* The pack's memo-ready sections (R26) are the memo the underwriter copies into the firm's credit file. R2 counts them as prose, quoted to the whole pound, while the pack's record, its evidence and the monthly CSV are to the penny. The firm's template is open (E8), so revision 26 asked the owner, and D230 chose the whole pound.
  - *'With no case profile'.* R54 reads it as a statement categorised by v1 on its own: one statement, no case profile, no second account. An own-account pairing (R8) also makes a credit an own-account transfer, and the previous release ran one statement at a time.
  - *P2, P7, R10 and the term.* Each promised the previous release's evidence or values for M1 to M16 under v1 without R54's limit, and P2 is what both reviews failed. Left so, they would ask for what R54 no longer gives, and the requirements would contradict each other; each now says it through R54. M16 cites its evidence by the previous release's rule, the credits in its denominator, except that the denominator, shared with M26, leaves out own-account transfers, of which under v1 on its own there are none.
  - *Under any ruleset.* R5 and R6 apply under v1 as under v2, so a credit both kinds claim is ambiguous under v1 too. At 74ad8af the frozen path takes the director's name first (src/cma/categorise.py:1302-1304); T05, which builds R5 and R6, makes it ambiguous (C1).
  - *R41's combined total.* R41 counts each account's money in and money out in full, before own-account pairs are removed: that total bounds every combined flow figure and is known when an account is imported, while pairing runs across the whole case. A combined balance needs no refusal: it is a derived figure, n/a with the reason under R2 when no float carries it.
- Still open at 74ad8af: the committed example's appendix shows money to the whole pound (examples/cafe_stretched.memo.md:30 and :45), though the appendix code writes it to the penny (src/cma/graph.py:172), so T04 regenerates it before its review; the totals refusal says 'money in or money out' without saying which (src/cma/ingest.py:153, src/cma/importers/monzo.py:195), which R2's words allow and one condition would name; and no test shows a profile's own-account credit leaving M16 and M26 alike.
- The lesson is D223's, one input further: a requirement that keeps a previous release's output has to say what the previous release ran, one statement under v1 with no profile, or the first new input reaches past it.

**What the owner decided on revision 26, and what T04's sixth and seventh reviews showed** (D230 to D234, verify-132, verify-133, 2026-11-20)
- D230 answered revision 26's question with its default: the pack's memo-ready section text quotes money to the whole pound, as the memo's prose does, while the pack's record, its evidence and the monthly CSV carry the pence. D231 approved revision 26.
- verify-132, T04's sixth review, judged the owner's 74ad8af and failed it on R2, R3 and P2, with three majors. An import refusal said 'money in or money out' where only one had reached the limit (src/cma/ingest.py:154 and src/cma/importers/monzo.py:200 there). A returned direct debit whose reversed debit is not in the statement raised before the reconciliation, so an unbucketed credit beside it lost its pence and its id (src/cma/metrics.py:250 there). And M9's evidence note named every credit from a payer whose credits were capped, though at most one of them can count in part (src/cma/metrics.py:329 there). Four minors: the committed appendix still showed whole pounds; a claim about amounts with no ceiling rested on a test that converts a figure directly instead of loading a statement; the display proof called the formatter, not `cma run`; and a claimed historical test count was not a runner result.

- verify-133, T04's seventh review, failed 3886917 on R2, R3 and P2, with three majors. A statement starting in January of year 1 reached a VAT quarter in year 0 before the reconciliation, and `cma run` took the error for a model failure (src/cma/metrics.py:202 there). And, on Python 3.11, M10 cited fewer lines than the previous release on a standalone v1 statement (src/cma/metrics.py:459 there). The reviewer's statement holds thirty-two loan repayments of £0.01 and one of £9,000,000,000,000, each later returned unpaid, below the import limit either way. Python 3.11's sum adds floats one at a time and leaves -£0.0078125 of loan lines, which the previous release negates and rounds to £0.01 of debt service, so its M10 is a ratio citing every operating, drawings and loan line. T04 counts whole pence: debt service is £0.00, and M10 is n/a with no lines. Python 3.12 and later sum floats with compensation, and the runner ran 3.13, so its dual run saw no difference; the project supports 3.11 (pyproject.toml:6), and CI runs it (.github/workflows/ci.yml:17). Three minors: the historical test count, the over-limit probe and the display proof again.
- The owner's f147255, not yet reviewed, regenerates the committed example with the appendix to the penny (examples/cafe_stretched.memo.md:50 now shows M22 £189.91) and skips a VAT quarter outside the calendar's years instead of raising (src/cma/metrics.py:201-203), with a contracts test (tests/test_money_in.py:546-555). It leaves M10 as verify-133 found it. D233, the unblock, is pending.
- D234: clarify R54 and R2 where they meet. R54 gives M1 to M16 the previous release's evidence wherever the previous release's arithmetic was exact; where its binary-float sums came out a penny away from the exact pence (only on statements totalling about a trillion pounds or more, and only on some Python versions), R2's exact pence prevail and the figure and its evidence follow them, the dual run comparing the statements where both agree. Keep everything else in revision 26. Revision 27 writes it.
- Four readings.
  - *'Wherever the previous release's arithmetic was exact'.* R54 reads it statement by statement, as D234's last clause compares statements, and defines it by the previous release's own outputs: each money figure among M1 to M16 that it computed by adding amounts as floats, M2, M8, M9, M11, M12 and M14, came, as it rounded it, to the exact pence. The integration branch, which has not taken T04, still computes them so (src/cma/metrics.py:128, :161-162, :194-195, :288 and :292 at e695f55). Of M1 to M16, only M10's evidence turns on such a sum, through M8 (:201 and :284-285); M4's turns on whether any revenue fell in the first quarter, which no rounding changes, and every other figure's evidence is a list no arithmetic decides. So a statement on which the six agree gets the previous release's lines for every figure, and the definition is one a reviewer can test from both releases' outputs.
  - *'Only on statements totalling about a trillion pounds or more'.* R54 records it as D234's estimate and knows the case by its outcome, not by a size. A float sum's drift grows with the number of lines as well as with the amounts, so on Python 3.11 a long enough statement can drift half a penny below a trillion pounds, and a bound would be one more rule a review could test past.
  - *'The figure and its evidence follow them'.* R54 has named the evidence only, not the values, since revision 25, and R2 already reports every figure from the exact pence. The change is to the evidence, which follows the previous release's rule applied to the exact pence, so M10 cites no lines when exact debt service is £0.00.
  - *P2, P7, R10 and the term.* Each promised the previous release's lines or values for M1 to M16 without D234's limit, and P2 is what verify-133 failed. Each now says it through R54, as revision 26 did for the case profile.
- Still open at f147255: a test of verify-133's statement, which T04 adds before its review, and the recurring minors about claims the runner does not prove, a probe that converts a figure without loading a statement and a display proof that calls the formatter, not `cma run`.
- The lesson is D229's, one level down: a requirement that keeps a previous release's output has to say what that output rested on, its float arithmetic as well as its inputs, or the first statement where the floats drift reaches past it.

**What the owner decided on revision 27, and what T04's eighth to twentieth reviews showed** (D235 to D247 and D249, verify-134 to verify-146, 2026-11-20)
- D235 approved revision 27. T04 was then reviewed thirteen times, and every review failed. The owner took the first twelve over (D236 to D247); the fix for the thirteenth, 3f4987c, is not yet reviewed.
- verify-134 to verify-140 failed T04 on R2 and R3, one corner at a time, each where a statement enters or where its figures are first computed:
  - a statement starting on the first supported date, or ending on the last, stepped outside the calendar before the reconciliation (verify-134, verify-135; 9474421, 1cc1868);
  - the Monzo importer read a supplied opening balance through a float, and held it to a different limit from an inferred one (verify-134, verify-136; 9474421, 3172bce);
  - returned-payment matching, and the loader's and the importer's flow totals, did money arithmetic outside the figures layer (verify-137 to verify-139; 71b7fdc, 19813df, 4a63021);
  - the CLI's or the MCP server's truncation of a refusal could drop which flow or which line reached the limit (verify-138, verify-139; 19813df, 4a63021);
  - a column named twice let a second amount replace the first before the limit was checked (verify-140; 35fcabd).
- verify-141 to verify-146 turned to `cma resume`, which R53 keeps working. A review that the previous release left pending holds the figures that release computed with its float arithmetic, and no total money in or money-in buckets.
  - verify-141 resumed one for a statement today's loader refuses, and its stored figures reached the table and the export (R2). 3b7a591 re-checks the statement with today's loader first.
  - verify-142 resumed one whose stored float sum was a penny from the exact pence, and found its evidence without M17 or M19 to M25 (R2, R1). 5e7d90c decides such a review on the figures this release computes from its stored lines, and checks its memo against them.
  - verify-143 replaced the statement while it was loading. It also found that the refresh reopened the policy file, raising an uncaught PolicyError when the file had gone, and could record a policy edited after the reviewer saw it (P2, R2). ab883d3 reads each statement once and keeps the stored policy result.
  - verify-144 failed R53: the refresh refused every review in which a figure a policy rule reads had moved, though M15's 50.01% becoming 50.00% left the rule's outcome as it was. bff7ddc re-evaluates the stored result from the comparison and limits each stored rule holds.
  - verify-145 failed R53 again. Under a custom rule that passes when M3 cannot be computed, M3 became n/a, the stored result does not hold that action, and the review was refused. It also failed R2 on a CSV field longer than the parser's limit, whose error reached the caller as itself. 94ced9c makes reader errors refusals. Where the stored result cannot settle a rule, it reads the policy file and uses it only if its hash is the one stored with the review (src/cma/policy.py:101-117 at 3f4987c).
  - verify-146 failed R53 a third time, on that review with the policy file moved: neither the stored result nor the file settles M3's rule, and `cma resume` refused (src/cma/graph.py:234-239). It also failed R2 twice. The Monzo importer let the CSV parser's own error through on an oversized field, and a refusal counted records rather than the file's lines, so a blank line put the line number one short. 3f4987c reads both importers' records through one reader that numbers lines as the file has them.
- Minors recurred in every review: test counts from an earlier commit and a scratchpad probe the runner did not run, a no-ceiling claim true only of the conversion helpers, and a note that the committed example lacks evidence_note, which it has. verify-145 added that the MCP server forwards a whole-file refusal in full, so a missing-column refusal echoes the statement's header (src/cma/ingest.py:148 and src/cma/mcp_server.py:62-73 at 3f4987c).
- D249: write one scope limit under R53. A review left pending by an earlier release resumes and is decided, finalised and recorded on the figures this release computes (R1, R2, P2). Where a figure a policy rule reads differs, each rule's outcome is taken from the stored policy result when it settles it, and otherwise from the policy file the review was drafted with, identified by the hash stored with it. When neither settles it, `cma resume` refuses, naming the figure and saying to run `cma run` on the statement again; the stored review is untouched and nothing is recorded. Resuming a review whose outcome cannot be known is outside R53's guarantee, and storing a copy of every policy in the checkpoint is outside this release. Keep everything else in revision 27. Revision 28 writes it.
- Four readings.
  - *'When it settles it'.* D249 gives the cases: the value passes, with or without a referral, or fails a rule it already failed. The stored result can say so only where it holds the rule's comparison and limits, from which passing and the referral bands are read (RuleResult's op, threshold and referral bands, src/cma/models.py:182-185 at 3f4987c). A stored rule without them settles nothing and falls to the policy file.
  - *A rule whose figure is unchanged* keeps its stored outcome, as 'where a figure a policy rule reads differs' implies; _from_stored keeps it (src/cma/policy.py:133-135).
  - *Per rule or the whole file.* The file with the stored hash is the policy the stored result came from, and policy.evaluate is unchanged, so for every rule the stored result settles the file gives the same outcome. 94ced9c evaluates the whole file once it has read it (:117), which R53 allows.
  - *'Decided ... on the figures this release computes'.* The grounding check follows the figures: the stored memo is checked against them as any draft is (src/cma/graph.py:240), and approving one that no longer holds needs the grounding override, as for any draft that fails the check (src/cma/cli.py:306-319). 'Naming the figure' is read as naming a figure whose rule neither settles, at least one; the build names the first it meets.
- Two gaps remain at 3f4987c, both against R53 as revision 28 writes it.
  - The review node refreshes the figures again when the reviewer's answer resumes the graph (src/cma/graph.py:347), and reads the policy file again where the stored result does not settle a rule. If the file is changed or removed between what `cma resume` showed and the answer, StaleReviewError is raised through graph.invoke (src/cma/cli.py:289), and nothing catches it. Nothing is recorded and the review stays pending, but the reviewer gets a traceback instead of `cma resume`'s refusal and exit 2. T04 catches the refusal there, or hands the node the policy result `cma resume` showed, before its review.
  - RuleResult's op and threshold have defaults (src/cma/models.py:182-183), and _band reads OPS[op] and compares with the threshold (src/cma/policy.py:96). A stored rule that holds neither, with its figure changed, raises a KeyError or a TypeError instead of falling to the policy file. T04 makes such a rule one the stored result does not settle before its review.
  No test runs `cma resume` to a refusal for a review that neither settles, checking that the checkpoint is unchanged and nothing is written; tests/test_policy.py:71-81 refuses at the function only. T04 adds one, marked contracts, before its review.
- The lesson is D234's, carried to the checkpoint: a release that recomputes what an earlier one stored has to say what it does when the earlier one stored too little to decide, and stating the refusal ends the search.

**What the owner decided on revision 28, and what T04's twenty-first to twenty-seventh reviews showed** (D250 to D256 and D258, verify-147 to verify-153, 2026-11-20)
- D250 approved revision 28. T04 was reviewed seven more times against it, and every review failed. The owner took the first six over (D251 to D256); the fix for the seventh, c2460a0, is not yet reviewed.
- The two gaps revision 28 named are closed. A stored rule without its comparison settles nothing and falls to the policy file, and an unreadable policy file falls to R53's refusal (9efc40c and 76f230c; src/cma/policy.py:103-145 at c2460a0). A refusal raised while a decision is applied is reported, with nothing recorded (23acaa1; src/cma/cli.py:365-370). Since 52914cc, `cma resume` writes this release's figures and policy result into the stored review before it shows it, so nothing is read again after the reviewer answers (:161-173).
- The R2 majors came in the reading of a statement or a Monzo export:
  - verify-147: a record was named by its last physical line, so a refusal could name a description's continuation, and the MCP server took a field too long to read for an amount at the limit (9efc40c names a record by its first line).
  - verify-148: a description too long to read discarded the records before it before their limits were checked, and the MCP server still inferred the limit from a cell's text (76f230c checks the records before it, and records each problem's kind where it is found).
  - verify-149 to verify-153, in the Monzo importer: an unreadable later field, an invalid later date, a missing opening balance, and then a malformed Balance and a bad date on the row that takes money in to the limit were each reported without saying that money in had reached the limit among the rows read (23acaa1, 52914cc, 3a5770d, 6b2f80c and c2460a0, one field at a time).
- The R53 majors came where `cma` processes share a checkpoint file, or in the export:
  - verify-149 and verify-150: the refresh read the policy file again after the reviewer answered, first raising uncaught and then refusing and losing a decision made on the figures shown (23acaa1, 52914cc).
  - verify-151: a resume wrote today's figures into a review another process had decided meanwhile, reopening it (3a5770d compares the checkpoint first).
  - verify-152: that comparison and the write were two operations, and a decision could land between them (6b2f80c holds a lock file beside the checkpoint file across both, and across applying a decision).
  - verify-153: `cma run` checked that a review id was unused outside the lock, so two runs could both start it. And a folder made at the evidence path while the reviewer was prompted made the evidence write fail after the decision was recorded and the memo written, and the decided review could not be written out again.
- Minors recurred in every review: test counts from another commit and a scratchpad probe the runner did not run, a no-ceiling claim true only of the conversion helpers, and a note that the committed example lacks evidence_note, which it has. Since verify-147 the reviews have also noted that `cma import monzo --opening-balance` refuses exponent notation, which the previous float option took (src/cma/importers/monzo.py:146).
- The owner built c2460a0. `cma run` checks the review id and runs the review's first steps holding the lock (src/cma/cli.py:102-123). The memo and its evidence are made before either is written, and each is written beside its target and moved into place, evidence first (:328-339); the previous release wrote the memo and then its evidence in place (src/cma/cli.py:226-229 on the integration branch). The Monzo importer reads a row's amount before its date (src/cma/importers/monzo.py:198-202). Three tests mark it: a run waits for the lock (tests/test_cli.py:487), a failure making the evidence writes nothing (:514), and a bad date on the crossing row (tests/test_money_in.py:887, marked contracts). The two in tests/test_cli.py are not marked contracts.
- D258: write four scope limits under R2 and R53, keeping everything else in revision 28. (1) `cma import monzo` reads each row in order and stops at the first problem it meets, reading the amount before the other fields, and with that problem says money in or money out at the limit among the rows read so far; naming every problem in an export is outside R2. (2) `cma` processes that share a checkpoint file serialise starting a review, refreshing a resumed one and applying a decision with a lock file beside it; another program writing to the checkpoint file, removing or replacing the lock file, and a process stopped part-way through a write are outside the single-decision guarantee, as D99 and D144 say for case files. (3) The memo and its evidence are made in full before either is written and are moved into place, evidence first; a disk failure, a full disk or an interruption after a decision is recorded and before both files are in place is outside the guarantee, the decision stays recorded in the checkpoint, and a second export of a decided review is outside this release. (4) Statement lines are named by the line on which each record starts, blank lines counted; a field too long for the CSV reader is named as too long to read, not as an amount at the limit. Revision 29 writes them.
- Five readings.
  - *'Reading the amount before the other fields'.* A row in another currency is skipped, as the previous release skipped it, so its amount never counts in money in. R2 reads the currency first and then, for a row that counts, the amount before every other field, its Transaction ID included. 'The rows read so far' include the row the importer stopped on once its amount is read, as 6b2f80c builds.
  - *Where the importer stops after its rows.* Once every row is read, money in or money out at the limit is the first problem it meets, before the opening balance, as 3a5770d builds. Its options and the export's header are checked before any row.
  - *'The single-decision guarantee'.* R53 did not state it. The previous release's `cma resume` refused a decision made after another and named who decided first (src/cma/cli.py:240-263 on the integration branch), so R53 now states that guarantee with the three steps the lock serialises, and D258's limits have a guarantee to limit.
  - *Renaming.* D258 says removing or replacing the lock file; R53 names renaming too, as revision 23 did for a case's lock files.
  - *An output path taken meanwhile.* The output paths are checked as free before the review is shown, and the reviewer may take minutes.
