The owner's review-39 fix (523c5df) waits for its review, and its unblock, D190, is pending. Revision 22 asks no question. Still open in T12 are three things: a save refused before any part of its line, which is told only that the step was refused; the temporary file that a failed staging write of a head record leaves; and the tests not yet named p15. Still open in T03 are the values of a repeated field that 4c017d4 does not check (D63, D69, D73, D80, D85, D87, D89, D93, D95, D98, D105, D107, D114, D116, D118, D120, D122, D124, D125, D136, D141, D164, D168, D170, D172, D174, D176, D178, D180, D182, D184, D186, D188, D190, D192). Since revision 21 the two tasks have taken twenty-two reviews between them, and no other task has started. The cut line is nine days away, and of 41 tasks only T01 and T02 have passed. Since revision 22 each task has failed eight more reviews (verify-104 to verify-119), and the owner has taken each over seven times (D194 to D207); T12 is at 43332c6 and T03 at 3ae10cc, and D208 and D209, their unblocks, are pending. Revision 23 changes no code: both builds already do what it asks, and their latest majors fall outside it (D210). Since revision 23 T03 has passed (verify-126, at 2576e07) and been merged; T12 is blocked at 04ff28a, with D217 pending; T17 failed its first review (verify-122); and T04 has failed two of its three attempts (verify-127, verify-128). Its P2 majors are settled by revision 24's three kinds of figure, and its R1, R2, R3 and P1 majors stand. Of 41 tasks, three have passed. T04 then failed its third attempt (verify-129) and is blocked, with D222 pending. Revision 25 settles its R1, R2, P1 and P2 majors, and the owner built the loader's limit and the display in 0c63ca4, not yet reviewed; its R3 major, an early refusal that names only the credits with no category, stands, and the Monzo importer still reads floats. T04 then failed its fourth and fifth reviews (verify-130, verify-131) and is blocked, with D228 pending. Revision 26 settles their R2 prose and P2 profile majors (D229); aa04f0c answered verify-130's other majors, and the owner's 74ad8af, not yet reviewed, answers verify-131's R3 major. The committed example's appendix is still to regenerate. T04 then failed its sixth and seventh reviews (verify-132, verify-133) and is blocked, with D233 pending. 3886917 answered verify-132's three majors; f147255, not yet reviewed, regenerates the committed example and answers verify-133's R3 major, and revision 27 settles its P2 major (D234). T04 then failed thirteen more reviews (verify-134 to verify-146), each on one corner of import or resume. The owner's 3f4987c, not yet reviewed, answers verify-146's two R2 majors, and revision 28 settles its R53 major (D249); two resume gaps remain for T04 before its review (Context). T04 then failed seven more reviews (verify-147 to verify-153), each on one more reading corner of a statement or a Monzo export, or one more interleaving at the checkpoint file. The owner's c2460a0, not yet reviewed, answers verify-153, and revision 29 states the scope those reviews explored (D258); two small changes remain for T04 before its review (Context). T04 then failed twenty-six more reviews (verify-154 to verify-185), each on one more way its reader met a later part of a file, and verify-185 passed 82758a3 on every criterion but R2; revision 30 states the reading's reach (D289), and T04 rewords what two docstrings, a comment and a test name claim before its review. T17 failed three more reviews (verify-157, verify-182, verify-184). Revision 30 settles its ASGI major (D289), the owner's 6b43212, not yet reviewed, answers its other three, and revision 30 asks when a lock turned on during a run takes effect (Options considered). T04 was then reviewed four more times (verify-187, verify-188, verify-190, verify-192). Revision 31 settles verify-192's two R2 majors (D301), and T04's remaining change is two docstrings and a comment. T17 failed four more reviews (verify-186, verify-189, verify-191, verify-193). The owner's 9b10daa, not yet reviewed, answers verify-193's tracer major. Revision 31 asks about T17's other two runs of majors and about writing D291 into R29 and P18 (Options considered).
- A server, even one reachable only from the same machine, is new attack surface (see Security notes).
- Twelve more figures make the `cma run` prompt longer and the appendix wider.
- A statement with an amount or balance of £10,000,000,000,000 or more, or whose money in or money out totals that much, is refused (revision 25, D223). No small business's statement comes near it, so the cost is one check where a statement enters and a refusal no real file meets. A month's days-overdrawn and lowest end-of-day balance cells cite every close in the month, more lines than M6 and M5 cite for the same days. The memo's prose can quote a figure as £1 beside an appendix that shows £0.60 (revision 26, D229); a person checking it reads the appendix or the drill-down. Under v1 with a case profile, M16 can differ from what the previous release would give the same statement, since an own account the profile names leaves its share (revision 26, D229); the previous release ran no profile, so no committed or signed figure moves. Under v1 on a statement where the previous release's float sums drift from the exact pence, which D234 puts at about a trillion pounds on some Python versions, M8, M10 and M10's evidence differ from what the previous release gives on that interpreter (revision 27, D234); they follow the pence the lines hold. Suppose the previous release left a review pending, a figure its policy rule reads newly fails the rule or becomes n/a, and its policy file has moved, changed or was never hashed. `cma resume` then refuses the review, and the reviewer drafts again with `cma run` (revision 28, D249). A Monzo export is refused at its first problem, so a person mending one may meet its problems one at a time (revision 29, D258). A `cma run` holds its checkpoint file's lock while it drafts, so another process sharing that file waits for it. A failure writing the memo and its evidence after a decision leaves the decision recorded with no files, and this release cannot write them again; the reviewer runs `cma run` again for a memo (revision 29, D258). A statement or an export is read whole, decoded and split into lines before its first record is checked, so a refusal of a file with a very large later part waits for that part to be read (revision 30, D289). An embedder that saved the MCP server's ASGI app while the lock was off, and starts it under the lock in a server with lifespan events turned off, has a listening socket open that answers every request with 503 (revision 30, D289). A loader's refusal can name a balance that does not follow, or a date out of order, several records after the record it concerns. A Monzo refusal at a later row's own problem leaves an earlier walked balance that disagrees for the next import to name (revision 31, D301).
- Cash paid in by the owner now counts as trading receipts. It lifts M2, M9 and M10, and can turn an illustrative fail into a pass (D31). The single-deposit, monthly-share and round-trip indicators catch the common patterns. They miss personal cash split into deposits below the limit, in months where cash stays under the share limit. The underwriter sees that only in M18 and the cash lines.
- More lines in the judgement queue (D52). A genuine cash deposit at a branch whose name holds a listed word, a card settlement whose reference names a lender, a card provider's credit with advance wording (D71), and a counter deposit of cash and cheques together (D70) sit in unexplained money in, outside M2, M9 and M18, until the underwriter moves them. The policy can understate trading receipts; it never overstates them.
- More lines again (revision 7, D78): an ambiguous credit from a director or an own account named in the profile waits for one override that revision 6 made for it. Its candidates show the profile's bucket, so the override starts there.
- More lines again (revision 9, D86): a credit that names both a director and an own account waits for one override that revisions 3 to 8 made as director funding. A director's name inside an own account's name (JANE DOE in JANE DOE LTD) sends every credit naming that account to the queue, and so does a name the profile lists under both kinds. Both buckets are non-trading, so M2 does not move; the queue grows.
- A director's loan written with generic loan wording and the director's name (JANE DOE LOAN) counts as a lending drawdown, not director funding, because the protected verdict outranks the name (R5, revision 8). Both are non-trading, so M2 does not move; the underwriter moves it with an override. Written DIRECTOR LOAN, it is director funding by the longer phrase.
- Longer candidate lists (D60). An ambiguous credit lists every rule it matched in either stage, payment-method cues among them, and since revision 7 each kind of profile name it matches. Since revision 9 a credit only the profile makes ambiguous lists the same, not only the profile kinds. A candidate's bucket can be one the deciding stage outranked, such as trading receipts for a gateway name beside two disagreeing cues; it is shown so the underwriter can choose it, never counted.
- Names stretch across separators (D71, D72). A short listed name can match letters split across words: S O SMITH reads as a standing order, which can only make cash wording ambiguous; E BAY reads as eBay and counts as trading receipts, as the plain spelling always did, and the irregular-acquirer indicator reads it. The separator set is fixed and short, so the stretch stops at separators.
- The category order inside a bucket (revision 4) sends a gateway payout that carries an invoice reference through the round-trip check and counts it as a named customer in M15, and counts a line with both returned-payment and refund wording as a returned direct debit in M7. Both lean toward more checks, and both are one list in the table.
- The longest alternative inside a pattern (D102) is built by trying, at each start of a rule part with alternatives or a lazy repeat, the latest end first (D100), and since D115 any repeat of variable length. That is up to one extra attempt per character after the start, on short descriptions, within the 2-second target at 10,000 lines (R52) that T22's timed test checks. D119 measured categorising 10,000 lines at about 0.65 seconds; the whole recompute R52 times is T22's to measure (verify-65). verify-71 added that the timing covers one mixed statement: 5,000 equal-amount debits to one payee, each undone by a returned direct debit, make reversal linking rescan the debits already taken for every return, which nothing times yet. The bank's code outranking a longer phrase (D101) is one exception to R4's longer-phrase clause, which R4 states since revision 11; cash paid-in wording and a payment-method cue inside each other's phrase are a second, which R4 states since revision 14 (D127) and both ways since revision 15 (D132). A table author who wants a phrase with a gap lists it as names, because D110 refuses a pattern that can match across words, and one who wants an exception lists a longer phrase instead, since an exception is refused in the table's keys (D126) and in the pattern (D136).
- Payer-written cues move credits: a payer who writes REFUND or TO SAVINGS in a reference takes their credit out of trading receipts or into the queue. Every cue is non-trading, so this can only lower M2.
- A profile name still decides a credit that the table does not find ambiguous, that no protected verdict decides and that only one kind of profile name matches, wherever the name occurs in the description (R5, R6). So a customer's invoice-referenced payment that mentions a director in its reference counts as director funding. It can only lower M2; the line shows profile:director as its rule.
- A case folder on a network share is unsupported and not detected (revision 5, D66). A firm that redirects Documents to a server, and an underwriter who points CMA_HOME there, could let two CaseLog objects in one process both write. The default root is under the working directory, and the README and the trial sheet say to keep cases on a local disk.
- Interference with the lock files (revision 10, D99). A cleanup script, a sync or backup client restoring a folder, antivirus quarantine or a person deleting files can let two writers save. Two saves told recorded at one position leave a log reported broken, and the second save is redone once it is resolved. A repair under such interference may remove the entry of a save told it was not recorded; the person saves again (D124). Since revision 15 a writer that holds the case also stops when only the gate is removed or replaced, though its case lock is still its own. Since revision 23 none of this is a guarantee (D210): under such interference, or a save interrupted at an arbitrary instant, a save told recorded can be missing from a log that reads intact, as after a hand edit, and a report can give the wrong answer. What the underwriter can rely on then is a check of the case log made after the interference stops, and the head a signed pack pinned.
- A rule table that is not well formed is refused naming at least one fault (revision 23, D210), so a person mending a hand-edited table with several structural faults may see them one at a time. Nothing loads from it meanwhile, and a well-formed table still names every rule that carries an exception.
- The first empty head record is linked into place (revision 15, after verify-68), so a case folder needs a file system with hard links. NTFS, APFS and the usual Linux file systems have them; FAT and exFAT, common on USB sticks, do not, and there the first append writes nothing and answers not recorded, naming the file system's error (R42 since revision 18). The README and the trial sheet name the file systems to use.
- More re-checks (D108; R42 since revision 12; the gate since revision 15): a writer checks both lock files before every write call and every rename attempt of a step a reader reads, each one file status call. A step refused after the line leaves an unrecorded entry, which the next holder drops, and the window between each check and its step remains. Since D125 (R42 since revision 14) a save also reads back the log and the head record after publishing, and a repair re-checks the log and the head record before its log rename and the head record before its head rename: a few reads of short files per save. Since cd298cc an append and a repair also read the whole log to compare its bytes with their check, a read that grows with the log; a case log holds a few hundred short entries. A repair refused after its log rename leaves a broken log to resolve by hand. A save whose read-back fails takes back its head record (revision 16), one more rename, so its entry is past the recorded entries for the next holder to drop; one whose take-back is not made is reported as not confirmed (D143), and `cma case check` shows whether its entry is among the recorded entries. A reader also compares each lock file it holds with its path after each read (revision 16), one file status call each. These happen only under interference or a read error. Since revision 18 a save the file system fails answers not recorded or, when its head record cannot be made durable, not confirmed, and the person checks the case log before saving again. Since revision 19 a step refused partway through the line answers not recorded as well, and since revision 20 (D159, D163) a read a save makes only to check names its error instead of answering a conflict.
- While another writer holds a case, a reader says nothing about anything after the recorded entries (revision 6), and since revision 7 does not say intact even when nothing follows them. A torn entry a crash left is reported only when the holder looks or the case is free, and a `cma case check` run while another window saves exits 4 and should be repeated. Sign-off and every append judge the whole log while holding the case, so neither passes a torn entry. A check that meets a writer paused at the gate while another holds the case names only a fault at or before the recorded entries, or says the case is being saved (D109; R43 since revision 12).
- Two lock files per case folder, and checks that hold the gate (revision 10). A writer waits at the gate for checks to finish, up to a set time, and is refused only if another writer still holds the case after a moment, or checks keep the gate or the case held past that time. CI runs on Linux, so the Windows lock path (LockFileEx) is proven only by running the P15 tests on Windows (Rollout).
- An outcome on screen can be read as the tool deciding. The friend said they don't want that, and the approver may object to it. The label sits beside every outcome, the recommendation stays blank, and show_policy_verdict turns the outcome off without a release.
- On the owner's own statements the illustrative policy will probably refer or fail, because a personal account has little trading income. The demo banner (R48) says the case demonstrates the mechanism; the presenter should say why before showing it.
- The categoriser still reads payer-written text, and a payer can name themselves after an acquirer. Indicators reduce this but do not remove it.
- The requirement most likely to be wrong is that the reply's 13 generic fields are the firm's memo. It is cheap to check (E8: a redacted template) and cheap to fix, because the field map is data. Next most likely: the cash default (D31) and its limits (D35), which rest on one anecdotal source; the category order inside a bucket (D61), which is one list in the table; the protected verdict above a profile name (R5, R6), which files JANE DOE LOAN as lending; and the both-kinds rule (revision 9), which queues every credit naming a director's own company when its name holds the director's. The overrides in `cma report` will show each.

### Perspective check

- **Security.** *Who can call it:* the operating-system user who owns the case root. The workspace also needs a per-launch token and a loopback Host header (R33). The case log's lock files order honest writers and are not access control; removing them is a hand edit of the case folder (revision 10). *Trust boundary:* imported files and payer text are checked by the loader and escaped on output (R34); rule tables are data, but which wording counts as the bank's own code is code (D97). *Secrets:* only an optional model key in the environment, never logged. *Personal data:* kept under one case root readable only by its owner, never committed to git (R31), never in prompts (R30); evidence holds only hashes of the profile, and an ambiguous credit's candidates name the profile kind (profile:director, profile:own_account), never the name. *Risk path:* lending data; the human acknowledgement is the named sign-off bound to a snapshot that includes the policy hash and whether the check was shown (R22). The illustrative outcome is not an acknowledgement. A review resumed from an earlier release takes no policy outcome from a file other than the one it was drafted with, known by the hash stored with it. So a policy file swapped while the review waits changes nothing it records (R53 since revision 28). The lock file beside a checkpoint file orders the tool's own processes and is not access control, as a case's lock files are not (R53 since revision 29). An MCP app saved while the lock was off serves no request and runs nothing of the server once the lock is on; a listening socket an embedder's own server opens for it before start-up is that server's, outside R29 (revision 30). *Worst case:* see Security notes. It covers cash paid in to look like trading, wording that mixes lists, a listed name hidden inside a longer word or behind a typographic dash, and a director or an own account named in a payment reference. It also covers a writer that keeps saving to hold off readers, deleting the lock files so two windows save, and a log rewritten together with its head record.
- **Platform.** *Blast radius:* one Python package; new optional parts; the evidence JSON gains fields; PolicyResult and the checkpoint allow-list do not change. *Old and new side by side:* `cma run` is untouched, new commands are additive, a review the previous release left pending resumes on this release's figures or is refused when its policy outcome cannot be known (R53 since revision 28), and CMA_RULESET=v1 reproduces today's categories for a statement on its own with no case profile, cash included, with first-match order and no ambiguity verdict from the table; a case's profile names still apply (R54 since revision 26). The MCP server's ASGI factories hand out the SDK's app behind the lock check while the lock is off, as before but for its class (R53 since revision 30). *Resources:* files on disk only, including each case log's head record and two lock files, and a lock file beside each checkpoint file (revision 29); no tables, queues, cron jobs or IAM. The longest alternative is built with the standard library (D100), so there is no new dependency. *Rollback:* an environment flag (CMA_RULESET), a config flip (show_policy_verdict) or a reinstall, with no data repair. *Observability:* see that section. *Platforms:* the locks take a different call on Windows (LockFileEx) than on Linux and macOS, and CI runs only Linux (Rollout step 3). The checkpoint file's lock takes msvcrt.locking on Windows, which gives up after about ten seconds until T04 makes it wait (revision 29). The first empty head record is linked into place, so a case folder needs hard links, which FAT and exFAT lack (revision 15).
- **Staff.** *Smallest change:* Tier 1 is the smallest set that shows the mechanism in the reply's terms; Tier 2 can be cut off. The cash change is one category mapped into an existing set, and the policy check reuses policy.evaluate and the guard as they are. The ambiguity policy replaces per-pair exceptions with one verdict and keeps revision 2's precedence, inserting that verdict between the protected verdict and firm rules. Revision 4 made R4 smaller: the queue it named belongs to R15. Revision 5 wrote down the case log's scope, which let D63 remove the fork machinery. Revision 6 made the reader claim less and removed the lock-file marks; in C1 it replaced an exception and per-list spellings with one cue and one separator set. Revision 7 moved the profile name one step down the precedence and made the reader try the lock before it says intact; neither added a component. Revision 8 wrote the existing precedence into R5 and R6 and added nothing to the build. Revision 9 moved one case, a credit both profile kinds match, from step (5) to step (4), and kept R5 and R6 clear of the queue. Revision 10 writes down a scope for the lock files instead of adding machinery, and takes in the gate, which replaced the probe after the read and the hold counts; it adds no component. Revision 11 writes D101 and D102 into R4, which D100 already builds, and adds nothing to the build but one validator check (D110) and a few re-checks (D108). Revision 12 writes D108 and D109 into R42, R43 and P15: T12's reader already does what they say, and the worktree's uncommitted work does the re-checks, so the only build change it asks for is the message after a step refused past the line. Revision 13 writes the repair boundary and one save at a time into R42 and P15, which b428d56 half builds, and adds a read-back per save and a re-check per repair rename to close the corner a repair still leaves; in C1 its default removes a schema feature, exceptions, instead of adding one; it adds no component. Revision 14 writes D125 to D127 into R4 and R42, which 1b592b6, 4adddf9 and 1d16873 already build; its one default makes one condition in T03's longer-phrase filter two-way; it adds no component. Revision 15 writes D132 and D136 into R4 and R42, which 78556a7 and cd298cc already build; its one default adds one validator check in T03, for anchors; it adds no component. Revision 16 writes D137 and D141 into R4, R42, R43 and P15, which 5c9a5bd, 2c72469 and f5436bb already build; its defaults ask T03 to settle matches by length instead of rules in rounds, T12 to compare one more read and to give a failed take-back its own answer, and R43 and P15 to state one more limit; it adds no component. Revision 17 writes D142 to D144 into R4, R42, R43 and P15, which 4b3b21c, 0d339cc and 3076547 build. It keeps the lock re-check on the take-back, which asks T12 to turn one check back on, and it adds no component. Revision 18 writes D149 and D153 into R4, R42 and R43, which 9042fb4 and 7c8fba2 build; its default reads R42's answer for a save the file system fails across every step of the save, which asks T12 to map four more call sites, and it adds no component. Revision 19 writes D154 and D158 into R4, R42, R54 and P15, which 9c265a3, 51c8b68 and 7cb2e71 build; its default reads R42's naming of the error into the reads a save makes only to check, which asks T12 to carry the error out of six reads, and it adds no component. Revision 20 writes D159 and D163 into R4, R42 and P15, which de6a56e, 1e35352 and 5139e22 build; its default asks T12 to word two refusals for the take-back, and it adds no component. Revision 21 writes D164 and D168 into R4 and R42, which 26bc51e, 7fb3a23 and 5f5bb2e build; it asks T12 to word one refusal for the save and T03 to build a pattern's forms beside a malformed field, and it adds no component. Revision 22 writes D192 into R4, R43 and P15, which 523c5df and 4c017d4 build. It asks T03 to run the checks it already has over every value of a repeated field, whichever comes first, and it adds no component. Revision 23 writes D210's two scope limits into R4, R42, R43, R54 and P15. It asks for no code and adds no component: it narrows what a review may ask of T03 and T12 to what both already build. Revision 24 writes D219 into P2, R1, R11 and R16: it names which figures are sums, outflows and derived, as the figures layer already computes them, and adds one note to M9's evidence; it adds no component. Revision 25 writes D220 and D223 into R2, R10, R11, R54 and P2: it adds one check where a statement enters, which the loader already makes, and names which derived figures keep the previous release's evidence; it adds no component. Revision 26 writes D224, D225 and D229 into R2, R10, R11, R41, R54, P2 and P7: it says where the pence show and what the previous release ran, which 74ad8af already builds, and adds one check at a case's import, for Tier 2; it adds no component. Revision 27 writes D234 into R2, R10, R54, P2 and P7: it limits the previous release's lines to the statements on which its float sums were exact, which f147255 already computes, and asks T04 for one test; it adds no component. Revision 28 writes D249 into R53, which 94ced9c already builds. It asks T04 to catch one refusal, to settle nothing from a stored rule without its comparison and to add one test, and it adds no component. Revision 29 writes D258 into R2 and R53, which c2460a0 mostly builds. It asks T04 to read a Monzo row's amount before its Transaction ID and to drop one clause from a refusal, and it adds no component. Revision 30 writes D289 into R2, R29, R53 and P18, which 82758a3 and 6b43212 already build. It asks T04 to reword what two docstrings, a comment and a test name claim, and, under its default for a lock turned on during a run, T17 to read firm.yaml at a resume, and it adds no component. Revision 31 writes D301 into R2, which bedf5ff already builds. It asks T04 to reword two docstrings and a comment, and it adds no component. *Ownership:* arithmetic and the queue's assembly stay in metrics.py and indicators.py, the outcome in policy.py, categorisation in categorise.py plus the rule data, the log and its lock files in case.py; the workspace and pack only render. *Six-month misunderstandings removed:*
  - M16 is renamed so it cannot be confused with M26 (unexplained); M18 is the cash inside M2, not a bucket beside it; [R1] to [R9] in a pack are policy rules, not requirement ids.
  - The action reads 'sign off figures', not 'approve the loan'.
  - OTHER means unexplained. An ambiguous credit is unexplained because the lists disagree, not because nothing matched, and its rule says conflict and names every rule it matched in either stage, not only the stage that decided.
  - A profile name on an ambiguous credit is a candidate, not a settlement, because a description cannot tell the payer from someone named in the reference. A credit naming both a director and an own account is ambiguous, not director funding. A profile name never outranks a protected verdict, in either direction, so JANE DOE LOAN is lending, not director funding.
  - The category inside a bucket comes from the table's declared order, not rule order, while a debit still takes the first matching rule. Inside a pattern, the longest alternative counts, not the first, and R4 says so.
  - The bank's code at the start of a line is the bank's, listed in code; no table edit can grant it, and no longer listed phrase takes it in, as R4 says since revision 11. Likewise only the code knows which table is frozen: v1, by the hash it pins; a table's own frozen flag grants nothing (revision 18), and v1's bytes are compared before anything else in it is checked (revision 19); only v1's pinned bytes spare it the exception check, and a changed v1 is otherwise judged as the kind of table it says it is (revision 21).
  - No v2 rule carries an exception (D126, R4 since revision 14), in its keys or, as a lookahead, a lookbehind or an anchor, in its pattern (D136, D137; R4 since revisions 15 and 16), the bank's code apart; where two lists meet, a longer phrase or the queue decides, never a rule's own exception. Cash wording and a payment-method cue are the one pair whose longer phrase decides nothing: neither is hidden inside the other's phrase, whichever list holds it (D127, D132; R4 since revision 15).
  - A combining mark is part of a word. A listed name ignores separators between its letters, so SUM UP and SUMUP are one name. A cheque is a payment method like the rail, not an exception on the cash list.
  - The case log separates writers only on a local disk, in processes that do not fork, and while nobody removes or replaces its lock files, so a forking server, a network share or a tool that cleans the case folder is outside it.
  - A check is not a writer and shares the gate and the lock with other checks, on every platform. Being saved is neither intact nor broken. A reader says intact only after finding the case free, and being saved only while the writer it found still holds the case. A recorded entry cut short is damage, never a torn write to drop. A reader reports the recorded entries and the rest as being saved only after holding the gate through its read; one that meets a writer at the gate while another holds the case names only a fault at or before the recorded entries. A read counts only while the lock files the reader counts on are still the case's, a holder's own read and a busy-gate read included (revisions 16 and 17); interference before a read is outside what a reader can know, and so is a lock file replaced after the check's last look at it, since a check is a sequence of looks (revision 22).
  - The gate and the case lock are the tool's own files, not the user's; deleting them is like editing the log by hand. A writer re-checks both before each step, so removing or replacing either stops a save (revision 15).
  - A save is recorded when its writer says so, not when its line is on disk: a save refused after its line leaves an entry the next holder may drop, and a log without it is intact (D124). A writer says so only after reading back an intact log that holds the entry (D125), and a save whose read-back fails takes its head record back, so its entry is past the recorded entries (revision 16), and one whose take-back is not made is not confirmed, which a check of the case log settles (revision 17). A save the file system fails answers as well, never with the operating system's error: not recorded before its head record's rename, not confirmed after it unless the head record was taken back (revisions 18 and 19). A save refused partway through its line is not recorded, never nothing written (revision 19). Each report gives one answer, its causes included: a save not confirmed is never also told it was or was not recorded, and whether its head record was published is known from the rename, not from reading the head record back (revision 21).
  - Pseudonymised data is still personal data.
  *Most likely wrong:* the memo fields (E8), the cash default (D31) and limits (D35), the category order inside a bucket, the separator set's reach across words, the protected verdict above a profile name, which files JANE DOE LOAN as lending, the both-kinds rule, which queues a director's own company, the assumption that the firm keeps working files on a local disk, the assumption that nothing in the firm's setup cleans or restores case folders, D109's answer for a check that meets a writer at the gate while another holds the case, which claims nothing where a reader might expect the recorded entries, and D124's boundary, which lets a confirmed drop remove a save told it was not recorded, D142's set-aside rule judged by match, D143's not-confirmed answer, D149's take-back that re-checks the lock files, which answers not confirmed more often under interference, revision 18's default that R42's answer for a save the file system fails reaches every step of the save, D159's naming of the error in the reads a save makes only to check, D164's not-confirmed report, which gives one answer and quotes no other, revision 21's reading that a changed v1 still calling itself frozen is not held to the checks only the maintained lists need, and revision 22's reading of D192's 'finished within the check's own last look' as a change made after the check's last look at that file, and revision 23's readings of D210: 'naming the faults the loader finds' as at least one, 'as it can' as reaching R42's two guarantees under interference, and 'a nested save from a handler' as leaving the nested save's refusal a guarantee, and revision 24's reading of D219's 'every monthly cell' as the cells that add lines up, and revision 25's readings of D223: 'an amount' as a line's amount or balance, either way; 'at import' as reaching the Monzo importer; 'R1/R54' as R10 and R54; and 'every line their calculation reads' as every close in the month for the month's lowest end-of-day balance; and revision 26's readings of D229: the drafting prompt's metrics table and the policy check's rule sentences as prose, the pack's memo-ready sections as prose, 'with no case profile' as a statement categorised on its own, and P2, P7 and R10 as following R54; and revision 27's readings of D234: 'wherever the previous release's arithmetic was exact' as a statement on which the six money figures it summed came to the exact pence, and the trillion-pound estimate as D234's, not a bound; and revision 28's readings of D249: a stored rule settles a changed figure only with its comparison and limits, the file with the stored hash may be evaluated whole, and a resumed review's grounding check follows this release's figures; and revision 29's readings of D258: a Monzo row's currency read before its amount, money in or money out at the limit the first problem once every row is read, the single-decision guarantee stated in R53, renaming named beside removing and replacing the lock file, and an output path taken while the review waits as outside the export guarantee; and revision 30's readings of D289: an app handed out 'as the previous release did' as the SDK's app behind a lock check, not its class, 'with lifespan events turned off' as one case of a socket the embedder's server opens or holds before start-up, 'before every request' as before every request the app begins, and 'once the next has been read' as at most one record ahead; and revision 30's default that a lock turned on during a run takes effect from the next run; and revision 31's readings of D301: a record's own values as every check that needs that record alone, its id and its date among them; 'once reading has stopped' as once the reader has read the last record it reads; and R2's naming of the Monzo importer's first problem as revision 29's, with the limit. *Out of scope:* listed above.
- **Clean code.** *What changes most:* the rule lists, the category order and the thresholds, kept apart as data. *Names:* follow the code: money in (inflows in metrics.py), trading receipts (M2 keeps its name, Total revenue, so signed memos still read), override, firm rule, case, preview, pack, sign-off, policy check, line-type cue, payment-method cue, name list, protected verdict, conflict, candidates, case-log writer, recorded entries, being saved, gate, case lock, save, repair. *Tests:*
  - properties P1-P20, P2 by figure kind since revision 24 (each sum figure against its lines' signed total, each outflow figure against that total negated, M9's note on a capped round trip, no derived figure asked to total its lines, and, since revision 27, verify-133's statement, on which M8 is £0.00 and M10 n/a with no lines on every supported Python); synthetic truth for every bucket and indicator; golden files for the importers;
  - a pairwise matrix over every two credit rules;
  - a separator matrix that puts each separator class between the letters of every listed name;
  - a profile matrix that crosses a director's and an own account's name, alone and together, with every ambiguous form, an unambiguous trading form and every protected rule. It asserts that an ambiguous credit stays unexplained with every table match and the profile candidates recorded, that a credit both names match is ambiguous with every table match then both kinds, and that a protected verdict keeps the table's category and rule in either direction;
  - a shuffle test on rule order within each stage that recategorises whole statements and compares credit categories and the money-in figures; table-edit cases for verify-53's two findings (D100 has them) and for verify-57 to verify-69's; since D110, a pattern that can match across a space refused at load; since D126, an exception on a v2 rule refused at load (4adddf9 has it); since D132, a payment-method cue inside a longer phrase on the cash list that still meets cash wording, and since D136, a lookahead or lookbehind refused at load (78556a7 has both); since D137, an anchored pattern refused at load (2c72469 has it); since D141, a phrase set aside inside a longer one that hides nothing (5c9a5bd has it), and, since D142, a set-aside match of a rule that counts by another match that hides nothing either (4b3b21c has it); since D153, a table that calls itself frozen under another id checked rule by rule, and a changed copy of v1 refused by its hash (9042fb4 has both); since D158, a changed v1 refused as changed whatever else it breaks, and a pattern given as a list, a mapping or a number named by rule (7cb2e71 has both), and, since D163, a bank-code rule whose pattern is a list named by rule and a changed v1 with such a rule refused as changed (5139e22 has both); since D168, a changed v1 that names an exception it gained and a pattern that compiles alone but cannot be used named beside another rule's fault (5f5bb2e has both), and such a pattern named beside a malformed field of its own rule (48c5b9c and edc3ff4 have it); since D192, a repeated rules key with a fault in a rule of either list, a repeated pattern one of whose values does not compile, and v1-shaped rules checked with no digest (4c017d4 has all three, each with the bad value last), and, since revision 23, a table that is not well formed refused as a whole, naming at least one fault, whatever else it holds (3ae10cc's tests name more);
  - the case log's exclusion and reader cases run as P15 tests. These include a writer paused at the gate, alone and while another holds the case, and a holder that lets go during a read. They also include the interference cases: a lock file replaced before each step a reader reads (the empty head record, the line, each rename; refused, nothing further published), and replaced after a re-check (two saves at one position, reported broken, never intact). Since revision 13 they include one save at a time (a save begun inside another on the same thread, and a line added after a save's check; b428d56 has both) and the repair schedules: verify-64's, which leaves an intact log and a save told not recorded; verify-66's two, an append and a repair at one position and a repair after the log grew; and a save whose head record lands between a repair's log rename and its head rename, which ends broken or with the save told not recorded, never intact with a save told recorded missing. 1d16873 had four of these. Since revision 15 they also include the gate removed or replaced during a save, a first append from a stale check that never resets a head record, and a repair refused when the log was rewritten to the same length (verify-68). cd298cc has all three; the gate's test is named p15, and none of the eight tests added since review 20 yet is. Since revision 16 they also include a read across a replaced gate or case lock that is taken again (f5436bb, named p15) and a failed read-back that takes its head record back (f5436bb, not yet named p15); since revision 17 they include a busy-gate read and a holder's read across a replaced case lock (0d339cc, both named p15) and a take-back that is not made, answering not confirmed (3076547, not yet named p15). As the revision 17 default they also include a take-back that a replaced lock file stops, answering not confirmed, and the schedule in which a take-back without that re-check would unrecord a repair told recorded. 0d339cc's test that a take-back goes through a replaced lock file (tests/test_case_log.py:2401) changed with that default, and at 7c8fba2 it expects not confirmed. Since revision 18 they include a head record that cannot be made durable, answering not confirmed, and a busy-gate read whose case lock is removed during it, which looks again (7c8fba2, the second named p15), and a failure by the file system at the line, the first empty head record and a repair's rename, answering not recorded and naming the error (51c8b68, not yet named p15). Since revision 19 they include a lock lost after part of the line is written, answering not recorded (9c265a3, not yet named p15). Since revision 20 they include a lock lost after the whole line, whose report never says nothing was written (de6a56e). They also include a read error injected into the log comparison, the head record's re-check, the read-back, the lock files' examination and a repair's re-check, answering with the error named and never as a change (1e35352). Neither is named p15 yet, and the second accepts either answer at every site. Since revision 21 they include a take-back stopped by a replaced lock file or by a head record no longer the save's own, whose not-confirmed report, causes included, never says the save was or was not recorded or that nothing was written; a lock lost after the whole line whose causes never say nothing was written; and a head record that cannot be read before its rename, answering not recorded (26bc51e and 7fb3a23; none named p15 yet). They should also include a save refused before any part of its line, whose report says it was not recorded. Since revision 22 they include a lock file replaced just after its path was looked at, and a gate moved aside and replaced after its first look (523c5df, both named p15). Since revision 23 the interference and interruption tests show what the tool tries; P15's guarantee is the cases with no such interference (D210).
  *Duplication removed:* all importers share one balance walk; sign-off reuses ReviewDecision's validation; the pack's policy section reuses policy.evaluate and the guard's [Rn] check; exceptions that steer one list away from another give way to one verdict and longer phrases; rules and profile names share one whole-word definition and one separator set, so lists stop carrying spelling variants; the reader no longer reads writer marks or counts; one candidate definition serves every ambiguous credit, whatever made it ambiguous. *Duplication introduced:* the v1 rule table, kept as data for one release; and R5 and R6 carry the same paragraph on a credit both kinds claim, so that each can be judged by its own text. *No single-caller abstraction:* no storage interface for a future hosted version; no shared-handle registry for the case log; the label is one constant, not a templating layer; the importer contract has four callers; the rule-to-category lookup the queue uses is the table itself, plus two fixed profile kinds.
- **Data-intensive.** *Idempotency:* imports are keyed by file hash, an identical override changes nothing, and a second sign-off is refused. *Determinism:* a credit's table verdict, category and table candidates are a pure function of its description, the sign of its amount and the tables with their category order. Its profile candidates, a profile settlement and a profile-made ambiguity add the case profile's names, which the snapshot hashes. Neither a credit's bucket nor its category depends on rule order within a stage or, since revision 10, on the order of alternatives inside a pattern; only which of several same-category rules is recorded, and the order candidates are written in, follow table order. A debit's category follows table order by design. *Ordering and concurrency:* one writer at a time per case, across windows, commands, threads and CaseLog objects in one process, on a local disk, while nobody removes or replaces the lock files, and one save at a time per holder; enforced by a gate, a lock, a save-in-progress mark and the expected log position. A second tab is refused while the first writes, and gets a conflict and reloads if it writes from a stale position. A reader holds the gate shared while it reads, so no writer takes the case during the read; it holds the case lock shared too when the case is free, and a writer waits for it. While another writer holds the case, a reader judges only the recorded entries, from a read the head record held still across. `cma` processes that share a checkpoint file take turns through a lock file beside it to start a review, refresh a resumed one and apply a decision, so a review is decided once (R53 since revision 29). *Partial failure:* append-only logs with a check for a torn last entry; the head record is written before the first line, so a lost head record is never mistaken for a torn first append; a repair replaces the log in one rename; the pack is written to a temporary folder and then renamed. Two saves told recorded at one position under interference break the chain or the head record there. A step refused once any part of the line is written leaves an unrecorded entry, whole or torn, as a crash does, and the writer says the save was not recorded, never that nothing was written (revision 19). A step the file system fails answers the same way, naming the error, and one after the head record's rename, which cannot be made durable, answers not confirmed (revisions 18 and 19). A decided review's memo and evidence are made before either is written and moved into place, evidence first; a failure after the decision leaves it recorded with no files (R53 since revision 29). *Consistency:* the workspace reads the latest state; the preview and sign-off bind to a snapshot that includes the ruleset, the profile, thresholds, the policy hash and the show_policy_verdict setting, so a sign-off cannot attach to an outcome the underwriter did not see. *Migration:* additive, nothing to backfill. *Money:* integer pence, GBP only, thresholds compared in pence, and amounts with more than two decimals are refused. Amounts and balances are read exactly from the statement's text, and a statement with an amount or balance, or a money in or money out, of £10,000,000,000,000 or more is refused where it enters (R2 since revision 25), so the float the models carry always reads back as the counted pence. A case of more than one account is held to the same limit across its accounts (R41 since revision 26), and the pence show in the evidence and every table, while prose quotes whole pounds (R2 since revision 26). Where the previous release's float sums came to other than the exact pence, M1 to M16 and their evidence follow the exact pence, never the previous release's float (R54 since revision 27). A review an earlier release left pending is recomputed from its stored lines when it resumes. Its policy outcome comes from what it was drafted with, or it is refused and left pending, never decided on a guess (R53 since revision 28).

## Data changes

**Category enum (src/cma/models.py).** Six values are added and none of the existing values change. Credit categories map to buckets as follows:

| Credit category | Bucket |
|---|---|
| revenue_card_settlement, revenue_transfer | trading receipts (M2) |
| cash_deposit (new) | trading receipts (M2); also counted in cash deposits within trading receipts (M18) |
| own_account_transfer (new; also used for debits) | own-account transfers in (M19) |
| director_funding (new) | director and shareholder funding (M20) |
| loan_drawdown | lending drawdowns (M21) |
| refund_in (new), returned_dd | refunds and returned payments in (M22) |
| hmrc_repayment (new) | HMRC repayments (M23) |
| other_explained_in (new: grants, insurance claims, interest) | other explained money in (M24) |
| other | unexplained money in (M25), ambiguous credits included |

cash_deposit joins REVENUE_CATEGORIES, so M2, M3, M4, M9, M10, M28 and the monthly trading receipts include cash. M15 does not, because it reads named customer transfers only. Under v1 no line is ever categorised cash_deposit, so v1 figures do not move. In the code and the UI, `other` now means unexplained. Merchant cash advances use loan_drawdown and loan_repayment. Gambling and cash withdrawals are read by indicators, not categories. A credit in any other category is a reconciliation failure (R3). Where a bucket holds two or three categories, the table's declared category order picks one (C1, step 6).

**Transaction (src/cma/models.py).** Three optional fields are added: the account key (default 'main'), the id of the rule, firm rule or override that set the category, and the provider's category as an untrusted hint. Amount and balance keep their current types where the model meets the rest of the code, floats, and the figures layer converts them to pence. Since revision 25 the loader reads each from the statement's text as an exact decimal and refuses one of £10,000,000,000,000 or more either way, and a statement whose money in or money out totals that much (R2), so each float it hands on carries at most fifteen significant digits and reads back as the same pence, and the figures layer's conversion is exact.

**Line provenance.** The rule field holds `<ruleset>:<rule id>`, `<ruleset>:default`, `<ruleset>:round_trip`, `profile:director`, `profile:own_account`, `firm:<rule id>`, or the override's reference. An ambiguous credit holds `<ruleset>:conflict:` followed by every table candidate rule id in table order, then, since revision 7, `profile:director` and `profile:own_account` for each kind of profile name its wording matches, in that order, all joined by +; its category is other (unexplained). Since revision 4 the table candidates are every rule the credit matched in either stage, payment-method cues included, after a match inside a longer listed phrase counts as the longer one; since revision 5 a phrase that starts inside another match of the same rule is found too; since revision 6 a cheque is a payment-method cue and a listed name is found whatever separators stand between its letters. Since revision 9 a credit that both kinds of profile name match, and that no protected verdict decides, takes the same form whatever the table finds. Its table candidates are every rule it matched in step 1, and none when nothing matched: FPS CREDIT JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS holds v2:conflict:payment_rail+profile:director+profile:own_account, and JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS holds v2:conflict:profile:director+profile:own_account. The candidates form a set; table order only fixes how they are written. A credit that an override or a pairing settles, and a credit that a single kind of profile name decides because the table does not find it ambiguous, carries the rule of what settled it, as any other line does; a line a protected verdict decides carries the table's rule even when a profile name also matches. An ambiguous credit's candidates can be recomputed from its description, the ruleset and the case profile. The ruleset is always the text before the first colon. A v2 rule id holds only letters, digits, _, . and -, and default, round_trip and conflict are the categoriser's own (D119, D121), so a line's provenance always names one decision and a conflict's candidates read back one way. A per-line override is known by the line it names, not by its reference, so an override whose reference reads like a table rule's provenance is still a person's decision (verify-65; 1b592b6). The queue looks up each table candidate's category in that ruleset; a profile candidate gives director_funding or own_account_transfer whatever the ruleset.

**New figures.** No id is ever reused. M1-M16 keep their ids and units. M16 is renamed 'Non-trading money in (share)'; M2 keeps the name Total revenue, and its method sentence now names cash paid in. Values move only on lines a v2 rule now recognises or finds ambiguous (R10).

| id | name | unit |
|---|---|---|
| M17 | Total money in | GBP |
| M18 | Cash deposits within trading receipts | GBP |
| M19 | Own-account transfers in | GBP |
| M20 | Director and shareholder funding | GBP |
| M21 | Lending drawdowns | GBP |
| M22 | Refunds and returned payments in | GBP |
| M23 | HMRC repayments | GBP |
| M24 | Other explained money in | GBP |
| M25 | Unexplained money in | GBP |
| M26 | Unexplained share of money in | pct |
| M27 | Average end-of-day balance | GBP |
| M28 | Debt service cover including the proposed facility | ratio |

Reconciliation: M17 = M2 + M19 + M20 + M21 + M22 + M23 + M24 + M25, in pence. M18 is part of M2, is not added again, and its evidence is a subset of M2's. M16 and M26 share one denominator: money in less lending drawdowns, returned direct debits and own-account transfers. Under v1 on its own no line is an own-account transfer, since v1 has no such rule, so M16 reads the lines the previous release read; under any ruleset a case profile's own-account name or a pairing makes one, and it leaves both denominators and both figures' evidence (R54 since revision 26, D229). Cash deposits are trading receipts, so they are in neither numerator; an ambiguous credit is unexplained, so it is in both, whatever profile name it carries as a candidate. Credits that fall to OTHER today keep the same treatment in M9 whichever new bucket they land in, with one exception: cash deposits now count as trading cash (D31). No committed fixture or synthetic case has a cash line (Consumer trace, item 13), so no committed value moves because of cash. M27 averages the closing balance of every calendar day in the period, carried over days with no lines, as M6 does. M28 adds the proposed monthly repayment for each month of the period to existing debt service.

**Figure kinds** (revision 24, D219; P2). Sum figures: M2, M17, M18, M19 to M25, and the monthly money-in and trading-receipts cells; the signed amounts of each one's lines total it. Outflow figures: M8, M11, M12, M14 and the monthly money-out cell, each reported as a positive amount whose lines' signed amounts total it negated, as M8, M11, M12 and M14 always have. Derived figures: M1, M3 to M7, M9, M10, M13, M15, M16 and M26 to M28, and the monthly balance and overdrawn-day cells. A derived figure among M1 to M16 cites its evidence by the rule the previous release cited it by (R10), M16's denominator leaving out own-account transfers as M26's does, and the previous release's lines for a statement categorised by v1 on its own with no case profile on which its float sums came to the exact pence, the previous release's rule applied to the exact pence on any other (R54; revisions 25 to 27): M5 the line that set the lowest balance, M6 the line that closed each overdrawn day, a day with no lines taking the line that set the balance carried into it, and M9 every line it adds or offsets. Every other derived figure cites every line its calculation reads: M26 every credit in its denominator, which holds its numerator; M27 the line that set each day's close (R12); M28 every line of its cash flow and its debt service; and each monthly balance and overdrawn-day cell the line that set the close of every day of the month. M9's evidence note lists, by id only, each credit it counts only in part: a credit from someone the business paid by transfer, when that payer's credits exceed what was sent to them, so that together they offset no more than was sent. The note holds no description, payee or amount. No figure's value or sign changes, and neither does the evidence of M1 to M16, except on a statement on which the previous release's float sums came to other than the exact pence, where both follow the exact pence (R54 since revision 27, D234).

**Monthly summary.** One row per calendar month: the month, a part-month flag, money in, money out, trading receipts (cash included), average and lowest end-of-day balance, and days overdrawn. Each cell carries its evidence ids. The money-in and trading-receipts cells are sum figures and carry their lines' signed total. The money-out cell is an outflow figure, a positive amount carrying its lines' signed total, which is the cell negated (D220). The balance and overdrawn-day cells are derived and carry no total; each cites the line that set the close of every day of the month, a day with no lines taking the line that set the balance carried into it (P2 since revision 25), so the month's lowest end-of-day balance cites every close in the month, as D225 confirmed.

**Judgement queue.** One entry per line, built by C2 and read by C5 and `cma report`: the line id, the reason it is listed (unexplained, ambiguous, possible own-account transfer, or the indicators that flagged it), and for an ambiguous credit its candidates, each with its rule id, the category that rule or profile kind sets and the bucket that category maps to (none for a payment-method cue). Ordered by amount, largest first. It holds no money arithmetic.

**Indicators.** Each has an id, a kind, the threshold that fired, evidence ids and a one-line reason. Thresholds live in a packaged, validated YAML file that the firm can replace. Amounts in it are pounds with at most two decimals and are compared in pence. While a threshold still has its shipped value, the workspace and the pack call it illustrative, as they do the policy. Kinds: a large single credit against typical trading receipts; a cash deposit at or above the single-deposit limit; a month with cash above a set share of its trading receipts; cash paid in within a window of days after a cash withdrawal, the two amounts within a tolerance; a payment to a gambling operator; a returned direct debit; a run of consecutive overdrawn days; an irregular credit from a named acquirer, read from the acquirer and gateway name lists whatever category the line took. Shipped cash values: a single deposit of £2,500 or more and cash above 50% of a month's trading receipts (D35), and a round trip within 7 days and 10% of the amount (design default). An ambiguous credit is not categorised as cash, so the cash indicators do not read it; it is in the judgement queue as unexplained.

**Policy check.** PolicyResult and policy.evaluate do not change. The workspace and the pack evaluate only the packaged policy. The label is one constant with no digits in it, so it never trips the guard: 'Illustrative thresholds shipped with this tool. They are not your firm's lending policy, and this is not a lending decision: the underwriter decides.' The pack's evidence records whether the check was shown, the label, and the policy's name and hash. These do not go into PolicyResult, so the checkpoint allow-list and `cma run` are untouched. As in revision 1, the description of policy rule R9 is reworded for M16's new name, and the policy hash changes.

**Rule tables.** Packaged YAML under src/cma/rules/. v1 is today's table, frozen and pinned by hash. v2 adds acquirers, gateways, marketplaces and delivery platforms; lenders and merchant cash advance providers; generic advance wording on the loan list (D71); move wording for own pots, spaces and savings; cash paid in (counter, deposit machine, Post Office and PayPoint); cash-withdrawal wording for the round-trip indicator only; HMRC repayments; refunds; explained non-trading credits; and the payment rail and, since revision 6, cheque wording as cues with no category. Every v2 rule carries a stage (line_type or names) and whether it is protected, and line-type rules come before names. Protected in v2: the line-type cues, and lender, director, capital-introduced, shareholder and generic loan and advance wording. Not protected: cash paid in, the trading lists, invoice references and explained non-trading words. Compound wording lives as longer phrases on one list (STRIPE CAPITAL with the lenders, DIRECTOR LOAN with the director wording, HMRC REFUND with HMRC), not as exceptions in another list, and since D70 no v2 rule has an exception. Bare pot or savings labels and bare CAPITAL are on no list (D48). v2 also declares its category order inside each bucket that has more than one category (C1, step 6); the order is part of the table and of its hash. The returned-payment rule carries the bank-code flag (D96), and the bank return codes it may carry are listed in code, not in the table (D97). Tables are validated on load like the policy, including each pattern in every form the matcher compiles it in. v2 refuses back-references and conditional groups, atomic groups and possessive repeats, patterns that can match no text and, since D110, any repeat whose body can match both a space and a letter or digit; it requires the bank-code flag on a rule for every listed code, and restricts rule ids (C1, Table validation). Since D126 it also refuses an exception on any rule (R4 since revision 14; 4adddf9), and since D136 a lookahead or lookbehind in a pattern (R4 since revision 15; 78556a7), and since D137 an anchor in a pattern (R4 since revision 16; 2c72469), except the bank's code listed in code. Each table has an id and a hash. The matcher itself (stages, whole words, separators, the longest match at each start, the bank's code, profile candidates and the both-kinds rule) is code, so an evidence record names the package version beside the table's id and hash, and verify names both when it refuses (R45).

**Case root.** Defaults to private/cma under the working directory, or CMA_HOME, on a local disk (R42 scope). Layout:

    firm.yaml            offline_lock, show_policy_verdict, paths
    firm_rules.yaml      promoted rules: pattern, direction, category, by, at, reason
    indicators.yaml      optional firm thresholds
    memo_fields.yaml     optional firm field map
    purge_ledger.jsonl   case id, time, who
    cases/<random id>/
      case.yaml          profile: label, demo flag, business name, directors and
                         shareholders, own accounts, VAT stagger, proposed monthly repayment
      inputs/            original files, read-only, named by hash
      statements/        five-column files the loader reads, one per account, plus hint files
      overrides.jsonl    append-only
      log.jsonl          append-only, hash-chained; a repair replaces it in one rename
      log.jsonl.head     the head record: the recorded entries' count and every entry's hash;
                         written empty before the first entry's line
      log.jsonl.gate     the gate: passed alone by a writer to take the case, held shared by a
                         check while it reads; created before the case lock; holds no data
      log.jsonl.lock     the case lock: held alone by the writer that holds the case, or
                         shared by checks; holds no data
      packs/<snapshot>/  written once at sign-off: memo.md, record.html, evidence.json, monthly.csv

The gate and the case lock are the tool's own; removing or replacing either while the case is in use is outside R42's single-writer guarantee (revision 10), and R42 says what holds instead (revision 13). Each write stages in a temporary file of its own beside its target (log.jsonl.*.tmp, log.jsonl.head.*.tmp), which nobody reads and which is removed when its write, its fsync or its rename fails; at cd298cc the empty head record's staging file is always removed, and the others only when their rename fails (verify-62, verify-64). The first empty head record is linked into place rather than renamed, so the case folder needs a file system with hard links (revision 15). Case ids are random and never the applicant's name. The profile never leaves the case folder; evidence records carry only its hash.

**Evidence JSON v1.** Everything in today's record, with its policy key unchanged. Added: M9's evidence note, the lines it counts only in part by id (revision 24), schema_version, case id, accounts (key, source format, file hash, period, line count), ruleset id and hash, firm rules hash, thresholds hash, profile hash, each line's category and rule (the conflict form with every candidate included, profile kinds among them), overrides (sequence number, line, old and new category, reason, name, OS user, time), money-in buckets, monthly summary, indicators with the thresholds that fired, policy_check (shown or hidden, the label, the policy name and hash), snapshot hash, sign-off (name, OS user, time, notes), egress (offline, or the model's name) and package version.

**Synthetic truth (src/cma/synth.py).** New opt-in generator features, with truth fields for each bucket, for own-account pairs, for each line's category and for each indicator flag. Cash deposits are among them: at least one at or above the single-deposit limit and one paid in after a similar withdrawal, with truth bucket trading receipts. A new feature's description carries wording from one list only, unless its truth is unexplained with its candidates, profile candidates included where the feature's profile names someone in the description. A generated credit that names both a director and an own account from its profile has truth unexplained, with both profile kinds among its candidates. Existing archetypes and profiles produce byte-identical statements. In their truth files, the director capital line's category becomes director_funding.

## Interfaces and contracts

**CLI (all additions)**
- `cma case new [--demo] [--label TEXT]` prints a random case id.
- `cma case import CASE FILE --format csv|monzo|openbanking|truelayer|bank-csv[:PROFILE] [--account KEY] [--statement-balance AMOUNT --at DATE]`
- `cma case profile CASE` edits the profile.
- `cma case check CASE` checks the case log against its head record (D75):
  - exit 0 only when it judged the whole log intact, which a reader that does not hold the case does only after finding it free;
  - exit 3 naming the first bad or incomplete entry, by number only, whoever holds the case, a recorded entry cut short, a lost head record and a log that two saves told recorded at one position left included;
  - exit 4 when it reports the case as being saved, with how many recorded entries check out and the rest not yet checked, whether or not anything follows them yet, or with nothing checked yet; and when the lock cannot be taken or tested and the log changed during every read, saying so and naming no other window;
  - exit 2 when there is no case folder.
- `cma review CASE` starts the workspace, prints the URL with its token, and opens a browser if there is one.
- `cma case preview CASE` prints the memo sections, the policy check when it is shown, and the snapshot hash. It writes nothing.
- `cma case sign CASE` asks for a typed name, checks the snapshot against the preview and writes the pack once. It is interactive only; there is no --yes. Preview and sign-off are also available in the workspace.
- `cma case purge CASE`
- `cma verify EVIDENCE [--case CASE]`
- `cma pseudonymise FILE --format F --out PATH [--profile CASE]`
- `cma report` prints a summary of every case with no personal data and no durations.
- `cma generate` gains opt-in features: director funding, own-account transfers, cash (with large deposits and a round trip), real acquirers, lenders, two accounts.
- Environment variables: CMA_HOME (case root, on a local disk) and CMA_RULESET (v1 or v2).

**Importer contract.** Input: one source file and an optional statement balance. Output: the five-column statement for one account, the account metadata, and an optional hints file. The output must pass ingest.load_csv unchanged, and all importers share one step that walks and verifies the balance. Refusals give line or record numbers, never cell values, as the MCP server does today. Every importer reads amounts and balances as exact decimals and counts them in whole pence, and refuses a statement with an amount or balance, or a money in or money out, of £10,000,000,000,000 or more, naming the line or the total (R2 since revision 25). `cma case import` also refuses an account that would bring the money in of the case's accounts together, or their money out together, each counted in full, to that limit, naming the account and which total, and leaves the case unchanged (R41 since revision 26). Since revision 29 every refusal names a line by the line of the file its record starts on, the header and blank lines counted, and a field too long for the CSV reader only as too long to read, never as an amount at the limit; `cma import monzo` stops at its first problem and says with it money in or money out at the limit among the rows read so far (R2). Since revision 30 the loader and every importer that reads through the shared reader (ingest.iter_data) read the file into memory, decode it and split it into lines before its records, then read the records in one pass, one record ahead. Since revision 31 (D301) that holds a record's own values; the checks across records are made once reading has stopped, and the Monzo importer, stopping at a record's own problem, names it with the limit as revision 29 says, not the walk of the rows before it. Reading as a stream is outside R2 (D289).

**Case log contract** (revisions 5 to 22; internal to the package, with the writers listed in Consumer trace item 20).
- A writer passes the gate alone and briefly to take the case, then holds the case lock alone, and states the log position it last saw. It waits while checks hold the gate or the case, waits a moment for another writer and is then refused, and gets a conflict if its position is stale. The first append creates the empty head record before its line, only where none exists, and never renames over one (revision 15).
- One save runs at a time per holder: a save begun inside another on the same thread is refused before it writes anything, and a save refuses when the log grew since its check (R42 since revision 13).
- Before each step that changes a file a reader reads (the empty head record, the line, each rename that publishes a head record or a repaired log), a writer re-checks that both lock files, the gate it passed and the case lock it holds, are still the files it took. It refuses the step if either was removed or replaced; refused once any part of the line is written, it leaves what it wrote unrecorded and says the save was not recorded, never that nothing was written, in any part of its report (revisions 10 to 13, 15, 19 and 20; D108, D124, D136, D158, D163). Refused before any part of the line is in the log, it writes nothing of its entry and says the save was not recorded.
- A save tells its caller it was recorded only after reading back, once its head record is published, an intact log that holds its entry where that head record lists it, and it publishes its head record only if the head record still lists what its check read. A repair re-checks before its log rename that the log, byte for byte, and the head record are as its check read them, and before its head rename that the head record is (R42 since revision 14; D125). A save whose read-back fails takes back its head record, when that record is still its own, leaving its entry past the recorded entries, and says the save was not recorded (R42 since revision 16); one whose take-back is not made, because its head record is no longer its own, a lock file was removed or replaced, or the head record cannot be read or renamed, says the save is not confirmed and asks for the case log to be checked (D143; R42 since revision 17). The take-back re-checks both lock files and the head record before its rename, as every head rename does (D149; R42 since revision 17). A save that meets an operating-system error after its check, in a step or in a read it makes to re-check or read itself back, answers, naming the error, never with the error itself: not recorded, unless its head record was published and not taken back, when it answers not confirmed and asks for the case log to be checked (R42 since revision 18; one rule since revision 19). Its reads to check something are among those steps. One that cannot be made is named as its error, never answered as a conflict or as a lock file removed or replaced, and a lock file missing when examined was removed (R42 since revision 20). Whether its head record's rename was made is known from the rename, never read back from a head record that may be unreadable, so a head record that cannot be read before its rename answers not recorded. Each report gives one answer, its causes included: no part of a not-recorded report says the save was recorded or is not confirmed, and a not-confirmed report names what stopped its take-back in the save's own words, and no part of it says the save was or was not recorded or that nothing was written (R42 since revision 21; D164, D168).
- A check is not a writer: it holds the gate shared while it reads, and the case lock shared when the case is free, on every platform, so checks never exclude each other.
- A reader gets one of five answers:
  1. the whole log intact, with its head and entries, only from a reader that holds the case or found it free;
  2. the first bad or incomplete entry with its reason;
  3. the case being saved, with the recorded entries it judged and the rest not yet checked, only while one writer held the case throughout the read, which the reader knows by holding the gate through it (R43 since revision 12), and still holds it after;
  4. the case being saved with nothing claimed, when that writer gave no steady read, or when every attempt found a writer at the gate and the case held and no steady read showed a fault at or before the recorded entries, which answer 2 would name;
  5. when the lock cannot be taken or tested and no read held still, not checked, with the lock error named and nothing claimed.
- A reader gives any of these answers only from a read across which the lock files it held stayed the case's; a read across one removed or replaced is taken again, and with every attempt so, the reader claims nothing (R43 since revision 16). The holder's read counts on the case lock it holds and the gate it passed, and a check that found the gate busy and holds the case lock alone counts on that case lock (R43 since revision 17). Lock-file interference before a read is outside what a reader can know, and a check after it stops gives the true answer (D144). A read taken again goes through the gate and the case lock as the first attempt did; a lock file missing at the comparison after a read was removed during it, and never stands for a case with no gate (R43 since revision 18). The check after a read looks at each lock file the reader counts on and then at the first again, and a holder's check also looks at whether each file it holds still has a name. A lock file removed or replaced after the check's last look at it is outside what any check can know (R43 since revision 22).
- Whatever needs the whole log intact (an append, a sign-off) decides holding the case, and no caller presents being saved or not checked as intact or as broken.
- Messages name entries by number and never quote their content.
- No caller forks while it holds the lock, every caller keeps case folders on a local disk, and no caller removes, replaces or moves the gate or the case lock while the case is in use. Interference with them, and a save interrupted at an arbitrary instant, are outside the single-writer and reader guarantees (D99, D144, D210). Under them the tool tries to leave a log that answer 2 names, never answer 1, when two saves told recorded share a position, and a log that answer 1 calls intact only when it holds every save told recorded (D124), and gives the answers above as it can; a check after the interference stops gives the true answer.

**Review workspace API.** Internal to the package; the page and the server ship together, so it is not a shared contract. It can:
- read the case summary and the judgement queue;
- read the lines behind a figure, bucket, monthly cell or indicator;
- read the policy check when it is shown;
- post an override with the expected log position;
- preview and promote a firm rule;
- build a preview, sign off a preview's snapshot, and post a comparison figure.
The case summary carries the case log's state as one of the case log contract's answers, so a second window says the case is being saved rather than warning of a broken log. A line in the judgement queue carries its rule and, for an ambiguous credit, its candidates, each with the category its rule or profile kind sets and the bucket that category maps to, or no bucket for a payment-method cue (R15). A line in a drill-down carries its rule as recorded (R16). The firm-rule preview counts the lines the rule matches and will not apply to, because a protected or ambiguous verdict, or a profile name, settled them (R20, R21, R4). Every request carries the session token. Every write carries the expected log position, gets a conflict if that position is stale, and is refused while another writer holds the case, when another save is under way in the same window, when the log changed since its check, or when a case lock file, the gate or the case lock, was removed or replaced. A write whose read-back does not find its entry is reported as not recorded (R42 since revision 14), and its head record is taken back when it is still the write's own (R42 since revision 16); one whose take-back is not made is reported as not confirmed (R42 since revision 17). A write that meets an operating-system error is reported, naming the error, as not recorded or, when its head record was published and not taken back, as not confirmed (R42 since revision 18; one rule since revision 19), and a write refused partway through its line as not recorded (R42 since revision 19).
