No report about a write whose line is in the log says nothing was written, and a write whose own check could not read names that error (R42 since revision 20). Each report gives one answer: a not-confirmed report names what stopped the take-back and says only that whether the write is in the log is not confirmed, never that it was or was not recorded (R42 since revision 21).

**Pack.** memo.md has the sections in field-map order, figures with their ids and money quoted to the whole pound, as the memo's prose quotes it (R2 since revision 26; D230), and blanks left for the underwriter. record.html's figure tables, evidence.json and monthly.csv carry every money figure to the penny. While the check is shown, it then has a 'Policy check (illustrative)' section that no memo field maps to. Its first line is the label, followed by the overall outcome and each rule, with the metric value tagged [Mn] and the threshold tagged [Rn] after a limit word. record.html is self-contained and makes no external requests; it shows the label wherever an outcome appears, and shows the sign-off. The pack also holds evidence.json (v1) and monthly.csv. It is written once, at sign-off, to a new folder named by the snapshot hash.

**Memo field map.** A YAML file giving, for each field, its name, the figures or tables it shows, and a sentence template whose numbers are placeholders for figure ids. Fields with nothing mapped are marked for the underwriter. The default reproduces the 13 fields in the reply: business details, loan request, trading history, monthly turnover and credits, average and minimum balances, existing commitments, returned direct debits, VAT and HMRC, director loans and transfers between the business's accounts, credit history, affordability, key risks and recommendation. The turnover field states the cash within trading receipts (M18) next to M2. Credit history, key risks and recommendation stay blank. The policy check is not a memo field; the recommendation stays blank whatever the outcome.

**MCP tools.** compute_metrics returns M1-M28 (an addition), M9 with its evidence note (revision 24), and a new read-only tool returns the money-in buckets. check_policy is unchanged. The server refuses to start under the offline lock. Since revision 30 (D289), while the lock is off, sse_app() and streamable_http_app() hand out the SDK's ASGI app behind a lock check. It reads the lock at start-up and before each request it begins and, once the lock is on, fails start-up, answers each HTTP request with 503 and closes each websocket; any attribute read on it is the SDK app's. Under the lock both refuse. A listening socket an embedder's own server opens for a saved app before start-up is outside R29.

**Graph and prompt.** Unchanged, except that the metrics table has more rows and M16 has its new name. That table and the policy check's rule sentences show money to the whole pound (Metric.display), so the memo's prose does too; the appendix's figure table and the interactive table show every money figure to the penny (Metric.display_exact; src/cma/graph.py:172 and src/cma/cli.py:292 at 74ad8af), as R2 says since revision 26. FakeLLM parses rows by id and is unaffected; its sentence about M16 is reworded. Under v2 the evidence lines `cma run` writes carry each line's rule, the conflict form included; the terminal review shows no queue. `cma resume` decides a review an earlier release left pending on this release's figures and grounding check, and takes a changed rule's outcome from the stored policy result or the policy file with the stored hash. Otherwise it refuses with exit 2, naming a figure and saying to run `cma run` on the statement again, and leaves the checkpoint as it was (R53 since revision 28). Since revision 29 `cma run`, `cma resume` and the decision take a lock file beside the checkpoint file (NAME.lock) to start a review, to refresh a resumed one and to apply a decision, and a decided review's memo and evidence are each written beside their target (NAME.part) and moved into place, evidence first (R53).

## Failure modes

Since revision 23 the rows on lock files removed, renamed or replaced, and on saves interrupted at an arbitrary instant, say what the tool does as it can, not what it guarantees (D210); a check after the interference stops gives the true answer. The rows on rule tables that are not well formed say what the build names; R4 asks for at least one fault. Since revision 29 the rows on a Monzo export's later problems, on interference with a checkpoint file or its lock file, and on a failure after a decision is recorded say what the tool does outside the guarantee (D258). Since revision 30 the rows on a file whose later part is very large, and on a saved MCP app that an embedder's server binds before start-up, likewise say what the tool does outside the guarantee (D289). Since revision 31 the rows on checks that relate a statement's records to one another say when those checks are made (D301). The rows on T17's open corners say what revision 31 asks.

| What happens | What the system does | Req |
|---|---|---|
| The same file is imported twice | Same hash: the case is unchanged; the log notes it was already imported | R39 |
| The same account arrives as a CSV and as Open Banking JSON, with different ids | Periods overlap and most (date, amount) pairs match: the second is refused as probably the same account | R40 |
| Open Banking JSON has no per-line balances and no statement balance was given | Refused, saying which balance to supply | R36 |
| A paged Open Banking export repeats a transaction id | Identical content: kept once. Different content: refused | R36, R39 |
| The feed includes pending transactions | Left out, and counted in the import message | R36 |
| An account or line is not in GBP | The account is refused; lines are never converted | R36, R2 |
| Lines with equal timestamps cannot be ordered so that the balance chain holds | Refused, naming the first break | R36 |
| Two equal-amount credits could pair with one own-account debit | Not paired; all listed for judgement | R9 |
| A firm rule pattern matches far more lines than expected | The preview shows how many lines in the open case it matches before saving | R20 |
| A credit's line-type cues point to two buckets (an HMRC credit whose reference says TO SAVINGS; a refund from a merchant whose name says SAVINGS TRANSFER) | Ambiguous: unexplained money in, with every rule it matched in either stage recorded as candidates; the judgement queue lists it with each candidate's bucket; never trading receipts unless the underwriter moves it | R4, R15 |
| The cues disagree and a name from a third bucket also matches (FPS CREDIT HMRC TO SAVINGS STRIPE) | Ambiguous; the candidates are the savings move, HMRC, the rail and the gateway, so the queue shows trading receipts as one reading and no bucket for the rail | R4, R15 |
| A credit with no cue matches names in two buckets (COUNTER DEPOSIT at a branch whose name holds INSURANCE CLAIM or a lender's name; cash wording with DIRECTOR) | Ambiguous, as above. A genuine cash deposit waits in the queue, outside M2 and M18, until the underwriter moves it | R4, R15, R55 |
| A payment-method cue and names from two buckets, with no cue that points to a bucket (FPS CREDIT STRIPE GRANT; CHQ STRIPE GRANT) | Ambiguous: the rail or the cheque points to no bucket, so the names decide, and they disagree (R4's names clause, revision 7). The candidates include the payment-method cue | R4, R15 |
| A payment rail appears with cash paid-in wording (FPS CREDIT ... CASH DEPOSIT), with or without another cue (BACS CREDIT HMRC CASH PAID IN) | Ambiguous whatever other cue matches: the rail says the money did not arrive as cash. The candidates include the rail, the cash wording and any other cue | R4 |
| A cheque appears with cash paid-in wording (CHQ CASH DEPOSIT; CHEQUE PAID IN AT BRANCH; a counter deposit of cash and cheques) | Ambiguous, as the rail with cash (D70), and since revision 7 by R4's own text (D76): the candidates include the cheque cue and the cash wording; never a cash deposit or trading receipts until the underwriter moves it | R4, R15 |
| A cheque with no cash wording (CHQ 004512 J SMITH) | The cheque decides nothing; the names decide, and with no listed name the credit is unexplained unless the profile names J SMITH | R4, R7 |
| A longer phrase starts inside another match of the same rule (FROM POT TRANSFER CASH DEPOSIT) | Both phrases are found; POT TRANSFER takes in TRANSFER, so no rail is left over and the own-account cue decides: own-account transfer | R4 |
| A pattern has two alternatives that start at the same place (ACME, and ACME FASTER PAYMENT LTD, in one pattern) | The longest alternative counts, so FASTER PAYMENT inside it is no rail; ACME FASTER PAYMENT LTD CASH DEPOSIT matches a trading name and cash wording, which agree on trading receipts, as a cash deposit by the category order (D102; R4 since revision 11; verify-53) | R4 |
| A table edit adds a v2 pattern with a repeat that can match across a space (SQUARE.* on the card list) | Refused at load, naming the rule (D110; built in ef057ad): its match would take in every other list's wording after it, so SQUARE UK CASH ADVANCE would read as a card settlement instead of ambiguous | R4 |
| A table edit gives a v2 rule an exception (unless: FPS on the cash rule) | Refused at load, naming the rule (D126; R4 since revision 14; 4adddf9). Before 4adddf9 it loaded, the cash wording was dropped, and FPS CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE counted as a card settlement in trading receipts (verify-65, verify-67) | R4 |
| A table edit writes an exception into a v2 pattern (the cash rule's pattern KIOSK CASH DEPOSIT followed by a negative lookahead for STRIPE, with CASH DEPOSIT off the cash names) | Refused at load, naming the rule (D136; R4 since revision 15; 78556a7). At 4adddf9 it loaded, and FPS KIOSK CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE counted as a card settlement in trading receipts (verify-69) | R4 |
| A table edit anchors a v2 pattern (the cash rule's pattern CASH DEPOSIT$ or ^CASH DEPOSIT, with CASH DEPOSIT off the cash names) | Refused at load, naming the rule; the bank's code listed in code keeps its ^ (D137; R4 since revision 16; 2c72469). At 78556a7 it loaded, FPS CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE matched no cash wording, and the gateway made it a card settlement in trading receipts | R4 |
| A table calls itself frozen to skip the table checks (frozen: true on a copy of the v2 table whose cash rule's pattern is CASH DEPOSIT$; verify-75) | Checked rule by rule like every table but v1, and refused naming the cash rule's anchor (D153; R4 since revision 18; 9042fb4). At 4b3b21c the checks trusted the flag, and the table was refused only for having no pinned hash, naming no rule | R4 |
| A copy of v1 is changed (a rule edited or added, CRLF line ends, a key added, its rules list emptied), whatever it says of itself | Refused as a changed frozen table, naming the table, its hash and the pinned hash, before anything else in it is checked, with whatever else keeps it from loading named after that ('It also fails to load: ...') and none of its rules used; reinstalling the package restores it (R4 since revision 18, R54; bytes first since revision 19, D158; 7cb2e71). At 9042fb4 a copy with its rules list emptied was refused for having no rules, never as changed (verify-77). At 7cb2e71 a copy set to frozen: false with a bank-code rule whose pattern was a list raised a TypeError past the handler, which caught only the table's own refusals. 5139e22 gives the changed-table refusal whatever the change makes fail (D163; R4 since revision 20; verify-79). A changed v1 is not spared the exception check: with unless: FPS added to a rule of a copy that keeps frozen: true, the refusal names that unless after the changed-table refusal (D168; R4 since revision 21; 5f5bb2e). At 5139e22 the unless went unnamed (verify-81) | R4, R54 |
| A table edit lists a payment-method phrase that holds cash wording (FPS CASH DEPOSIT on the rail list) | The cash wording is not taken in by the rail phrase, so the credit is ambiguous, rail and cash (D127; R4 since revision 14; 1b592b6) | R4 |
| A table edit lists a cash phrase that holds payment-method wording (FPS CASH DEPOSIT, or CHEQUES AND CASH PAID IN, on the cash list) | The cue is not taken in by the cash phrase, so the credit is ambiguous, the rail or the cheque and cash (D132; R4 since revision 15; 78556a7). At 4adddf9 the cue counted only as the cash phrase, and FPS CASH DEPOSIT counted as a cash deposit in trading receipts and M18 (verify-69) | R4, R55 |
| A table edit puts a third list's phrase between a payment-method cue and a longer cash phrase (FPS CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE on the cash list, FPS CASH DEPOSIT on the gateway list; verify-71) | The gateway's match is set aside inside the cash phrase and sets nothing aside, and the cash phrase does not take in the rail, so FPS CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE is ambiguous, rail and cash (D141; R4 since revision 16; 5c9a5bd). At 78556a7 the set-aside gateway match hid the rail, and the credit was a cash deposit in trading receipts and M18 | R4, R55 |
| A match set aside inside a longer phrase belongs to a rule that counts by another match in the line (FPS STRIPE CASH DEPOSIT on the rail list, STRIPE CASH DEPOSIT on the gateway list; FPS STRIPE CASH DEPOSIT REF STRIPE) | Settled by match (D142; R4 since revision 17; 4b3b21c), so the gateway's STRIPE CASH DEPOSIT, set aside inside the rail phrase, sets nothing aside; the cash wording counts and the credit is ambiguous, rail and cash. At 2c72469 the gateway counts by the trailing STRIPE, its first match still hides the cash wording, and the credit is a card settlement in trading receipts | R4 |
| A gateway phrase inside a cash phrase belongs to a gateway whose other name stands elsewhere in the line (FPS CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE on the cash list, FPS CASH DEPOSIT and STRIPE on the gateway list; FPS CASH DEPOSIT STRIPE \| STRIPE; verify-73) | The gateway's FPS CASH DEPOSIT is set aside inside the cash phrase and sets nothing aside, though the trailing STRIPE keeps the gateway counting. The rail counts, so the credit is ambiguous, with the rail, the cash paid-in rule and the gateway as candidates (D142; R4 since revision 17; 4b3b21c). At 2c72469 the set-aside match hid the rail, and the credit was a cash deposit in trading receipts and M18 | R4, R55 |
| A credit has one cue and names from another bucket (PAYPAL REFUND, AMAZON REFUND) | The cue decides: refunds. Every cue is non-trading | R4 |
| A bounced direct debit to HMRC (UNPAID DD HMRC VAT) | The bank's code at the start of the line settles it: a returned payment, linked to the debit it reverses | R4 |
| A table lists a phrase that begins with the bank's code (UNPAID DD TO SAVINGS on the own-pot list) | The bank's code at the start settles the line type and is not taken in by the longer phrase: a returned payment (D101; R4 since revision 11; verify-53) | R4 |
| A table gives the bank-code flag to another rule, writes the code with names, or anchors an ordinary cue at the start of the line | Refused at load, naming the rule; an anchored cue without the flag gets no exception (D96, D97; verify-50, verify-51) | R4 |
| A table edit drops the bank-code flag from the returned-payment rule, gives that rule an exception or a debit-only direction, or leaves a listed code without a flagged rule | Refused at load, naming the rule or the code: the bank's own code cannot be switched off (D115, D117, D119; verify-57, verify-59, verify-61) | R4 |
| A v2 pattern uses a back-reference or a conditional group | Refused at load, naming the rule: wrapping the pattern to find matches at every position would change its meaning (D96) | R4 |
| A v2 pattern uses an atomic group or a possessive repeat, or can match no text (a lookahead alone) | Refused at load, naming the rule: no later end can reopen the first, so the longest match could not be found, and the second leaves no span for the longer-phrase rule (D117, D121) | R4 |
| A v2 rule id holds + or :, or is default, round_trip or conflict | Refused at load, naming the rule: candidates are written joined by + after a colon, and those three ids are the categoriser's own provenance (D119, D121). An id that is not a string is named the same way, never raised (verify-67; R4 since revision 19; 7cb2e71) | R4 |
| A rule's pattern is given as a list, a mapping or a number (pattern: [CASH DEPOSIT] beside unless: FPS on the cash rule; verify-77) | Refused at load, naming the rule and the field, with the table's other faults, never raised as a type error (D158; R4 since revision 19; 7cb2e71). At 9042fb4 the bank-code membership test raised a TypeError before either fault was named. At 7cb2e71 a bank-code rule whose pattern was a list still raised one where the codes the bank-code rules carry are gathered (src/cma/categorise.py:534 there; verify-79, with the returned-payment rule's pattern as [UNPAID DD]). 5139e22 closes it (:534-535), and R4 says since revision 20 that such a rule is named whatever else it carries, the bank-code flag included (D163). A failure no check foresees is still refused, as a malformed value that could not be checked, but names no rule (:886-892 at 4c017d4; :977-983 at 3ae10cc). Since revision 23 such a table is not well formed: R4 asks that it is refused whole, naming at least one fault (D210) | R4 |
| A key is given more than once in a rule table (rules: given twice, with a lookahead in the cash rule of one list; a rule's pattern given twice, one value ( and the other CASH DEPOSIT; verify-97 to verify-103) | A fault: the table is not well formed, so it is refused as a whole, naming at least one fault, and nothing loads from it (R4 since revision 23, D210). At 3ae10cc every value is still checked as a key given once is, the rules of every list rule by rule and each value of a repeated field, so the lookahead and the ( are named beside the repeat; R4 asked for that in revision 22 (D192) and no longer does. At 792727b a repeated rules key hid every rule's faults, and a repeated pattern that did not compile went unnamed (verify-103); at 2b09c8f a repeat inside a malformed field stopped the load before any fault was named (verify-97) | R4 |
| A rule mapping holds a key YAML cannot hash beside a repeated pattern with a lookahead, or a repeated direction makes the bank-code rule debit-only in one version (verify-119) | Not well formed, so refused as a whole. At 3ae10cc the refusal names the rule as a value YAML cannot build, or the bank-code flag and anchor, and leaves the repeated pattern, the lookahead or the missing code for credits unnamed; R4 asks for at least one fault there (revision 23, D210) | R4 |
| Rules that bear v1's id and call themselves frozen are checked with no digest of their bytes, or with another digest | Their exceptions are checked and named, as any table's are: only a digest that matches the pin spares v1 (R4 since revision 22, D192; 4c017d4). At 792727b they were spared, though loading was not affected, since the loader always gives the digest (verify-103) | R4 |
| An override's reference reads like the table's own provenance (v2:approval-7) on a credit the round-trip pass would undo | The override stands: the pass knows overridden lines by id, not by their provenance text (verify-65; 1b592b6) | R4, R17 |
| A returned direct debit worded RETURNED DD (RETURNED DD IWOCA LTD after DD IWOCA LTD) | A returned payment, linked to the debit it undoes: under v2 the payee is compared with the recognised returned-payment wording set aside (revision 10; verify-53; not yet built at 4adddf9) | R4, R14 |
| A lender's name contains an acquirer's (STRIPE CAPITAL, SHOPIFY CAPITAL, SUMUP CASH ADVANCE, and since revision 6 SUM UP CASH ADVANCE) | The longer name counts: lending drawdown | R4 |
| A card provider's credit with advance wording and no listed lender phrase (SQUARE UK CASH ADVANCE) | Ambiguous: the card name says trading, the advance wording says lending (D71); never trading receipts until the underwriter moves it | R4, R15 |
| An acquirer pays out by Faster Payment (FPS CREDIT STRIPE PAYMENTS) | The rail belongs to no bucket, so the acquirer decides: trading receipts | R4 |
| A listed name spelt with separators between its letters (FASTER-PAYMENT, FASTER–PAYMENT with an en dash, FASTER PAYMENT with a zero-width space, SUM.UP) | Matched as the listed name: FASTER–PAYMENT CASH DEPOSIT is ambiguous (rail and cash), never a cash deposit | R4 |
| A short listed name's letters are split across words (S O SMITH; E BAY) | Matched as the name: SO is a rail cue and only makes cash wording ambiguous; EBAY counts as trading receipts, as the plain spelling would, and the irregular-acquirer indicator reads it | R4, R14 |
| A listed name inside a longer word, in any alphabet, including after a combining mark (FPS CREDIT CAFÉSTRIPE LTD, with the É as one character or as E and a combining accent; FPS CREDIT किSTRIPE LTD) | No match: a combining mark belongs to the word it follows, and the word around the name is on no list, so the credit stays unexplained unless another list or the profile matches | R4, R7 |
| Two rules agree on the bucket but carry different categories (FPS CREDIT STRIPE INV 17; RETURNED DD REFUND with no bank code at the start) | The table's category order decides: a customer transfer, which the round-trip check reads; a returned payment, which M7 and the indicator count. Reordering rules changes neither | R4, R14 |
| A debit matches two rules (FROM POT HMRC VAT) | The first in table order decides, as for every debit; the rule-order shuffle covers credits only | R4 |
| A bare pot or savings label in an external payment (FPS CREDIT J BLOGGS TAX POT) | Matches no list; unexplained unless another list matches or the profile names the account | R4, R6, R7 |
| CAPITAL in a branch, merchant or account name | Matches no list; only capital introduced, injected or contributed is director funding | R4 |
| A director named in the profile pays in cash (CASH PAID IN JANE DOE) | The table finds cash wording only, so it is not ambiguous, and only one kind of profile name matches, so the profile name decides: director funding, with the profile as its rule | R5, R55 |
| A credit the table finds ambiguous names a director or an own account from the profile, as the payer or in the reference (FPS CREDIT ACME LTD REF CASH DEPOSIT FOR JANE DOE) | Stays ambiguous (D78): unexplained money in, with the profile's bucket added as one more candidate after the table's (v2:conflict:payment_rail+cash_paid_in+profile:director); the queue shows director funding as one reading; only an override or a pairing settles it | R4, R5, R6, R15 |
| A credit names both a director and an own account from the profile, and no protected verdict decides it (FPS CREDIT JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS; STRIPE PAYMENTS JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS; CASH PAID IN JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS) | Ambiguous whatever the table finds (revision 9, D86): unexplained money in, with every rule it matched in either stage and then profile:director and profile:own_account as candidates (v2:conflict:payment_rail+profile:director+profile:own_account for the first). The queue shows director funding and own-account transfers in, and trading receipts or cash where a trading name or cash wording matched; only an override or a pairing settles it | R4, R5, R6, R15 |
| A credit the table finds ambiguous names both a director and an own account (FPS CREDIT CASH DEPOSIT REF JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS) | Ambiguous, with both profile candidates after the table's, director first (v2:conflict:payment_rail+cash_paid_in+profile:director+profile:own_account) | R4, R5, R6 |
| A director's name lies inside an own account's name (Jane Doe a director, JANE DOE LTD an own account; FPS CREDIT JANE DOE LTD) | Both kinds match, so the credit is ambiguous with both candidates; the longer-phrase rule settles the table's rules, not profile names | R5, R6 |
| One name is entered in the profile as both a shareholder and an own account (a holding company) | Every credit naming it that no protected verdict decides is ambiguous with both candidates; a debit naming it is an own-account transfer (R6). The underwriter overrides each credit, or corrects the profile, which changes the snapshot | R5, R6, R23 |
| A credit that names both kinds carries a protected verdict (IWOCA JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS; REFUND JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS) | The protected verdict decides (lending drawdown; refund), with the table's rule; no profile name is read or recorded (R5 and R6 since revision 8) | R5, R6, R21 |
| A debit names both a director and an own account (FPS DEBIT JANE DOE ACME HOLDINGS) | Never ambiguous: R5 speaks only of credits, so the own account's name decides (own-account transfer, profile:own_account) unless a protected rule matches first | R6 |
| A profile name appears in a credit a protected verdict settles (REFUND JANE DOE; JANE DOE LOAN; IWOCA ACME HOLDINGS with ACME HOLDINGS an own account) | The protected verdict decides (refund; lending drawdown; lending drawdown), with the table's rule; the profile name is not read and adds no candidate (R5 and R6 since revision 8). A director's loan written JANE DOE LOAN therefore counts as lending until the underwriter moves it | R4, R5, R6, R21 |
| A debit that names an own account from the profile carries protected wording (a repayment to IWOCA that names ACME HOLDINGS; UNPAID DD ACME HOLDINGS) | The protected rule decides, first in table order: debt service; a returned direct debit. The own-account name is read only when no protected rule matches (R6, revision 8) | R6, R21 |
| A customer's invoice-referenced payment names a director in its reference (FPS CREDIT ACME LTD INV 42 JANE DOE) | Not ambiguous in the table, no protected verdict and only one kind of profile name, so R5 decides: director funding, with profile:director as its rule; it can only lower M2 | R5 |
| An own-account pairing matches an ambiguous credit (Tier 2), whatever made it ambiguous | The pairing settles it: own-account transfer, with the pairing as its rule; it leaves the queue | R8, R4, R5, R6 |
| A profile value made only of separators (--- as an own account) | Names no one and matches nothing; it settles no credit, adds no candidate and makes no credit ambiguous (D72) | R4, R5, R6 |
| A firm rule matches an ambiguous credit, or one a protected verdict or a profile name settled | The firm rule is not applied and adds no candidate; the preview counts such lines before saving | R4, R20, R21 |
| A rule table pattern compiles on its own but not in a form the matcher uses ((?i)INV[0-9]+; the form that finds matches at every position) | The table is refused at load, naming the rule; it never loads and then fails on the first credit it matches (D94). Since revision 21 such a pattern is named with the table's other faults, never found only once they are mended: every rule's matchers are built while the table is checked (D168, after verify-81, which wrote (?i)CASH DEPOSIT on cash_paid_in beside unless: FPS on another rule; 5f5bb2e). At 5f5bb2e the build is still skipped for a rule with another malformed field (Context) | R4 |
| The underwriter moves an ambiguous credit into trading receipts | Recorded as an override with a reason; the figures recompute and mark the line as overridden; it leaves the queue | R17, R4, R15 |
| `cma run` meets an ambiguous credit under v2 | Its evidence line records the conflict and every table candidate, and the credit counts in M16; `cma run` has no profile, so no profile candidate and no profile-made ambiguity; the terminal review shows no queue (out of scope; the queue is the workspace's) | R4, R53 |
| A payer writes REFUND or TO SAVINGS in a reference to move a credit | The credit leaves trading receipts or joins the queue; a cue can lower M2, never raise it | R4, R34 |
| A payer writes a director's and an own account's names into a reference | The credit joins the queue unless a protected verdict decides it; it can lower M2, never raise it | R5, R6, R34 |
| A payer names themselves after an acquirer | An indicator fires on acquirer credits that are irregular or arrive by Faster Payment under a person's name; the line stays visible | R14 |
| A café banks its takings in cash every day | Each deposit counts as trading receipts and M18 shows the cash total. Deposits at or above the limit and months above the cash share are flagged and listed first | R55, R14, R15 |
| An owner pays personal cash in to lift turnover | It counts as trading receipts (D31). A deposit at or above the limit, a cash-heavy month, or cash paid in soon after a similar withdrawal is flagged; the underwriter moves it to director funding with a reason | R14, R17, R55 |
| A cash line also names a lender or carries DIRECTOR wording | Ambiguous: the queue, never cash or trading receipts. With HMRC wording and no payment-method cue, the HMRC cue decides: HMRC repayment; with a rail or a cheque as well, it is ambiguous | R4, R21 |
| The cash limits in the thresholds file are missing or malformed | Refused at load, naming the line; no case opens with different flags | R14 |
| An override changes the illustrative outcome | The panel recomputes; a sign-off on a preview that showed the old outcome is refused as stale | R25, R23 |
| An underwriter copies the policy-check section into a credit file | The label is the section's first line, so it goes with it; the recommendation field stays blank | R25, R24 |
| show_policy_verdict is off | No outcome or rule result in the workspace or the pack; the evidence says the check was hidden | R56 |
| The packaged policy file is edited in place on the underwriter's machine | The outcome is still labelled illustrative; the pack records the edited file's hash, which differs from the release's | R22, R25 |
| The rule table's category order is edited in place | The table's hash changes; the snapshot records it, so a sign-off on a preview built before the edit is refused as stale | R22, R23 |
| A director or own account is added to the profile after the preview was built | The profile hash in the snapshot changes, so the sign-off is refused as stale, naming the profile; the queue's candidates, and which credits two kinds make ambiguous, are recomputed | R22, R23, R4, R5, R6 |
| An override names a line that a corrected re-import removed | Shown as stale and not applied until confirmed or dropped | R19 |
| Two browser tabs or two terminals write to one case | One passes the gate and holds the case, and the other is refused after a moment's wait; a write from a stale position gets a conflict and reloads | R17, R42 |
| Two threads of the workspace, or two CaseLog objects in one process, write to one case at once | One holds the case; the other is refused and writes nothing | R42, P15 |
| A save is begun inside another on the same thread (a signal handler, a finaliser or a callback runs a save while one is under way; verify-64) | Refused before it writes anything: one save at a time per holder (R42 since revision 13; b428d56). The outer save carries on and commits at the position it checked | R42, P15 |
| A line is added to the log after a save's check (a stray process, or a save that got past the lock under interference) | An append refuses with a conflict before writing its line, and a repair before its log rename (R42 since revision 13; b428d56 for appends, 1d16873 for repairs after verify-66). The next check names the stray line | R42, R43, P15 |
| A check holds the gate or the case lock as a save starts in another window | The writer waits for the check to finish, however many checks share them, and then writes; it is refused only if another writer holds the case after a moment, or checks keep the gate or the case held past the writer's longer wait | R42 |
| Two checks run at once on Windows | Both hold the gate and the lock shared through LockFileEx (D85); neither takes the other for a writer. CI runs on Linux, so this is proven by running the P15 tests on Windows (Rollout step 3) | R42, R43, P15 |
| A second handle opens the case through another path, such as a symlinked folder | The same lock files, so the same case: refused while another writer holds it; in the thread that holds it, the handle reads as the holder and judges the whole log, a torn entry included | R42, R43, P15 |
| Another writer holds the case and has written nothing past the recorded entries | A reader reports the recorded entries and the case as being saved, never intact; `cma case check` exits 4 (revision 7, verify-42; D75) | R43, P15 |
| A reader looks while another writer has written its entry but not yet recorded it | Judges the recorded entries from a read the head record held still across, and, while that writer still holds the case after the read, reports them with the rest as being saved: never intact, never broken; never asks anyone to drop a live append | R43, P15 |
| A reader's read spans a head record that moved | That read yields no verdict; the reader reads again, and claims nothing until a read holds still | R43, P15 |
| A writer holds the case when a reader looks and lets go before the reader's read ends, leaving an incomplete entry | The reader holds the gate, so no other writer takes the case meanwhile; its second try of the case lock after the read finds it free, so it reads once holding the lock and names the incomplete entry (D87, D95; after verify-45, verify-46 and verify-47) | R43, P15 |
| Two writers' holds, one after the other, fall either side of a reader's read (verify-47, verify-49) | Cannot happen while the reader holds the gate: the second writer cannot take the case until the read ends (D95) | R43, P15 |
| A writer pauses while it holds the gate, before it takes the case (verify-52) | It does not hold the case. A check that finds the gate busy tries the case lock shared; free, it reads once holding it and names any incomplete entry, and the paused writer, once it resumes, waits for the check (D98) | R43, P15 |
| A writer pauses at the gate while another writer holds the case, through a check (verify-54, verify-55) | The check cannot know that one writer held the case through its read. From a steady read it names a change, removal or reordering at or before the recorded entries (D105); otherwise it tries again, and when the retries run out reports the case as being saved and claims nothing; `cma case check` exits 4 (D109; R43 and P15 since revision 12) | R43, P15 |
| A crash left a torn entry, and another writer holds the case, to append or only to check | A reader in that moment reports being saved; the holder, when it checks or appends, and every reader once the case is free, reports the torn entry as incomplete, naming it; no append or sign-off passes it | R43 |
| A repair that drops a torn entry is being recorded while another window reads | The repair entry follows the recorded entries, so the reader reports being saved, neither incomplete nor intact | R43, P15 |
| An entry at or before the recorded entries is changed, removed or reordered while another writer holds the case, including one removed after it was recorded | Reported as broken, naming the first bad entry, by any reader whose read the head record held still across, whether or not the writer still holds the case after the read; a removed entry shows as a log that ends before the recorded count | R43, P15 |
| A recorded entry's line is cut short (the head record lists it) | Named as the first bad entry, with the reason that the head record lists it and its line is cut short; never offered for dropping (D85, after verify-43); `cma case check` exits 3 | R43, P15 |
| The head record is lost beside a log of one entry or more | Damage, named as the first bad entry; never taken for a torn first append, because the first append writes the head record before its line (D87, after verify-45) | R43 |
| A check starts on a case with no gate file, and a writer creates the gate and takes the case during the read | The check tries again once it sees the gate: it judges the whole log once it holds the case lock, or reports being saved while the writer holds it; the read without a gate alone never yields intact. Behind the gate, no writer can take the case during the read (D85, D95) | R43, P15 |
| The gate exists but the case lock file does not | No writer holds the case, and none can take it while the check holds the gate: one read is the truth, a torn entry named (D95) | R43, P15 |
| One writer holds the case and keeps appending during every read (a stream of saves from one window) | No read holds still: the reader reports the case as being saved and claims nothing about any entry; `cma case check` exits 4 and says to look again. If the writer lets go during a read, the reader takes the case lock and judges the whole log | R43 |
| The lock cannot be taken or tested (an error that is not another writer holding it) | Not another holder: a reader believes a steady read as it stands, a torn entry included; with no steady read it reports that the log could not be checked because the lock could not be tested, names no other window, and `cma case check` exits 4; a writer's append fails with the error and writes nothing, since taking the case comes before the save's check and R42's answers for a failed step cover the steps after it | R43, P15 |
| Someone removes or replaces the case lock file, or the gate, while a writer holds the case (a cleanup script, a sync or backup client, a person) | The writer re-checks both lock files before each step a reader reads (the gate since revision 15, D136; cd298cc, after verify-68), finds one gone or replaced, and refuses the step. Before any part of the line is in the log, nothing of the entry is written and the save is to be reported as not recorded (R42); at 7fb3a23 that refusal reads only 'a case lock file was removed or replaced while this window held the case; the step was refused' (26bc51e), which T12 words for the save before its review. After the line and before the head record, the entry is left unrecorded, as a crash leaves it ('...; the save was not recorded'), and the next holder confirms dropping it (D108; R42 since revision 12, and since revision 13 that the save was not recorded, which cd298cc says at the head rename; since revision 19 also when it comes partway through the line, in a short write's second call, which 9c265a3 says as 'part of its entry was written', after verify-76; since revision 20 no part of the report says nothing was written once any of the line is in the log, which de6a56e words at the head rename as 'its entry was written', after verify-78; since revision 21 no cause the report carries says it either, which 26bc51e builds, after verify-80). A second writer may meanwhile take a new lock file and save; that is outside R42's single-writer guarantee (D99), and R42 says what holds instead (D124) | R42, P15 |
| The case lock file is replaced between a writer's re-check and the step it guards, and a second writer appends at the same position (verify-52) | Outside R42's single-writer guarantee (D99). Two entries claim one position, so the head record contradicts one of them or the second's link does not name the entry before it: the holder and any reader of a free case report the log broken, naming the entry; a reader while a writer holds the case reports being saved for what follows the recorded entries; none reports it intact (R42 since revision 13: two saves told recorded at one position). Sign-off and appends are refused until the log is resolved | R42, R43, P15 |
| The case lock file is replaced after A's line and before A's head record; B takes the new lock, drops A's entry as torn and appends (verify-64) | A's re-check before its head record refuses, and A says its save was not recorded. B's confirmed drop removes A's entry, and the log reads intact with B's drop entry and B's save: within R42's guarantee, since no recorded save was lost (D124). The person saves A's change again | R42, P15 |
| The case lock file is replaced after A's re-check before its head rename; B drops A's entry as torn and renames the repaired log; A's head record lands; then B's | A reads back, finds its entry gone and says its save was not recorded, and B's re-check before its head rename finds A's head record and refuses, so the log is broken, never intact without a save told recorded (D125; R42 since revision 14; 1d16873). At b428d56 A was told recorded and B's head record landed over A's, so the log read intact without A's save | R42, P15 |
| The case lock file is replaced while A's append waits at its head rename; B, with the new lock, checks, finds A's line torn and stages a repair; A's head record lands (verify-66) | If B's re-check before its log rename comes after A's head record lands, B refuses, and the log is intact with A's save, which A's read-back confirmed. If it comes just before, B's log rename can follow A's read-back, but B's re-check before its head rename then finds A's head record and refuses: the log is broken, never intact without A's save (D125; R42 since revision 14; 1d16873). At b428d56 both were told their saves at position 2 were recorded, and the log read intact with B's drop entry in place of A's save | R42, P15 |
| The case lock file is replaced while A's first append, from a check of an empty case, waits to publish its empty head record; B takes the new lock, appends entry 1 and is told it was recorded (verify-68) | A's empty head record is linked into place only where none exists, so A finds B's head record, stops with a conflict and writes nothing; the log reads intact with B's save (revision 15; cd298cc). At 1d16873 A's rename put an empty head record over B's, B's entry became a torn write to drop, and a confirmed repair left an intact log without a save told recorded | R42, P15 |
| A recorded entry is rewritten to the same length between a repair's check and its log rename (verify-68) | The repair compares the log's bytes with what its check read and refuses; the log is left as it was, and the next check names the changed entry (R42 since revision 14; cd298cc). At 1d16873 only the length was compared, so the repair's copy overwrote the change and the log read intact | R42, R43, P15 |
| Someone removes the gate while a check holds it | A writer that holds the case refuses its next step once it sees the gate gone (revision 15; cd298cc). Another writer can create a new gate and take the case during the check's read; outside R42's guarantee (D99). Since revision 16 the check finds after its read that the gate it holds is gone, takes the read again and claims nothing from that one (D141; R43; f5436bb); it never reports intact a log that two saves told recorded at one position left. A check after the interference stops gives the true answer | R42, R43 |
| A gate or case lock that a reader holds is replaced during its read (verify-70: the gate replaced, the holder lets go, a second writer takes the same case lock through the new gate) | After the read the reader finds the file it holds is no longer the case's, takes the read again and claims nothing from that one (D141; R43 and P15 since revision 16; f5436bb). At cd298cc both looks at the case lock found it held, and the reader reported the recorded entries and the rest as being saved though no one writer held the case through its read. The read a busy-gate check takes holding the case lock alone counts the same way (R43 since revision 17; 0d339cc). At f5436bb it was not compared, and with the case lock replaced and entry 2 appended under the new one, the check reported entry 2 missing (verify-72) | R43, P15 |
| A check that found the gate busy holds the case lock shared, and that case lock file is removed during its read (verify-74) | The comparison after the read finds the file gone: it was removed during the read, so the check looks again through the gate and the case lock and claims nothing from that read (R43 since revision 18; 7c8fba2). At 3076547 the missing file read as a case with no gate, and the check trusted an unprotected steady read, which could report a writer's live append under a new case lock as torn | R43, P15 |
| A writer that holds the case reads its log, and the case lock file is replaced during the read while another handle appends under the new one (verify-72) | The read counts only while the case lock the holder holds and the gate it passed are still the case's. The holder finds the lock replaced and reads again as any other reader, so it never reports the new entry missing from a stale read (R43 and P15 since revision 17; 0d339cc). Its own next step refuses (R42). At f5436bb the holder returned its stale read | R43, P15 |
| A lock file a reader counts on is replaced during its check after the read, after the check has looked at that file (verify-96 to verify-102: the case lock after its look, the gate after its look, and a gate moved to another name, which keeps a name, before a new one is installed) | The check looks at each lock file and then at the first again, so a file replaced after its first look is caught by the later look, and a holder also checks that each file it holds still has a name. The reader takes the read again, a holder as a reader that does not hold the case, and claims nothing from it (R43 and P15 since revision 22; 523c5df). At 57984ea an ordinary reader looked at the gate before the case lock and not again, and a holder's link-count test passed a gate renamed aside (verify-102) | R43, P15 |
| A lock file is removed or replaced after the check's last look at it | Outside what any check can know (R43 and P15 since revision 22, D192), as the window between a writer's check and its step is (R42): the read stands. A check made after the interference stops gives the true answer | R43, P15 |
| A save's read-back fails after its head record is published (a read error, or a log a repair rewrote under interference; verify-70) | The save takes back the head record it published, when it is still its own, by putting back the head record its check read; the save is told not recorded, its entry is past the recorded entries, and the next holder confirms dropping it (D141; R42 since revision 16; f5436bb). A head record another writer published since is left alone. At cd298cc the head record stayed, a fresh check read the log intact with the entry, and the next holder had nothing to drop | R42, P15 |
| The take-back is not made (the head record is no longer the save's own, cannot be read or renamed, or a lock file was replaced) | The save is reported as not confirmed, neither recorded nor not recorded, and asks for the case log to be checked before anything else is saved; the check shows whether its entry is among the recorded entries (D143; R42 and P15 since revision 17; 3076547). At f5436bb it said not recorded, though its entry could stay among the recorded entries. Since revision 21 the report names what stopped the take-back in the save's own words, and no part of it, its causes included, says the save was or was not recorded or that nothing was written (D164, D168; 7fb3a23, src/cma/case.py:1106-1121). At 1e35352 it quoted the refusal: 'nothing was written' when a lock file was replaced, and 'the save was not recorded' when the head record was no longer the save's own (verify-80) | R42, P15 |
| The folder that holds a save's head record cannot be synced after the head record's rename (an I/O error; verify-74) | The head record lists the save but may not last a power cut, so the save is not confirmed, names the error and asks for the case log to be checked before anything else is saved; it makes no read-back or take-back (D153; R42 since revision 18; 7c8fba2). At 3076547 the operating system's error reached the caller while the head record listed the save | R42 |
| The file system fails another step of a save after its check (a full disk while the line is written, an I/O error syncing it, a repair's staging file or log rename) | The save is not recorded and names the error; any part of its line already written stays past the recorded entries, as a crash leaves it, for the next holder to drop (R42 since revision 18; every step, D154). 51c8b68 maps it at every step that changes a file (_os_errors_as_not_recorded, src/cma/case.py:842-852) | R42 |
| A read the save makes only to check fails (an I/O error reading the log for the byte comparison before the line, the head record before its rename or the log for the read-back; a lock file that cannot be examined) | The save names the error and is not recorded, or not confirmed in a take-back. A re-check that cannot read is never answered as a conflict, and a lock file that cannot be examined never as removed or replaced. A read-back that cannot read is a failed read-back, and is taken back (D159; R42 since revision 19, read by read since revision 20, D163). 1e35352 builds it (src/cma/case.py:793-808, :957-963, :1001, :1076-1085). At 51c8b68 the byte comparison and a repair's re-check refused with a conflict that asks to reload, the head record's re-check said the head file changed, the read-back said the log changed, and a lock file that could not be examined read as removed or replaced | R42 |
| The head record's publication fails before its rename (its staging write, or its re-check that cannot read the head record), and the head record then cannot be read, or reads as another writer's under interference | Not recorded, naming the error, since no rename was made (R42 since revision 19; a re-check that cannot read, since revision 20). Whether the rename was made is known from the rename, never read back (R42 since revision 21, D168; 26bc51e, src/cma/case.py:1009, :1076). At 1e35352 _publish read the head record back to decide, and when it could not read it or found another writer's, it answered not confirmed, saying the head record could not be made durable (verify-80) | R42 |
| A save's read-back fails just after a lock file is replaced (verify-72: the gate replaced after the head record was published, then a one-shot read error) | With the take-back's re-check (D149; 7c8fba2), the take-back finds the gate replaced and is not made. The save is not confirmed, and a check shows its entry among the recorded entries. At f5436bb the same refusal said not recorded, which was verify-72's third finding. At 3076547 the take-back checks only the head record and is made, and the save is not recorded | R42, P15 |
| A take-back passes its check of the head record, and a repair that took the case through a replaced case lock, and checked before the save's head record landed, publishes its head record before the take-back's rename (the schedule in Context) | With the take-back's re-check (D149; 7c8fba2), it finds the replaced case lock and the take-back is not made. The save is not confirmed, and the log reads intact with the repair's save. At 3076547 the take-back checks only the head record and lands over the repair's head record. The repair, told recorded, then has its entry past the recorded entries, and a confirmed drop leaves an intact log without it, which verify-74 showed | R42, P15 |
| A writer takes the case after a take-back's re-check and publishes its head record before the take-back's rename lands | That writer checked a head record that lists the save's entry, so its own entry follows it. The take-back leaves two or more entries past the recorded entries, and the log reads broken, never intact | R42, R43, P15 |
| The gate is replaced while a writer that passed the old gate is paused before taking the case lock, and a reader then holds the new gate | The writer can take the case through a gate no reader holds; each later step it tries refuses (R42), but the case shows as held. The reader's own lock files stay the case's, so it may report being saved over an entry the previous holder left incomplete. Outside the single-writer guarantee (D99); a check after the interference stops gives the true answer, as R43 and P15 say since revision 17 (D144) | R43, P15 |
| A staging write or its fsync fails before the rename | The temporary file is removed, nothing is published, and the save answers not recorded, naming the error (R42 since revision 18). At 51c8b68, as at cd298cc, the empty head record's staging file is always removed; for a head record or a repaired log only a failed rename removes it, so a failed write or fsync leaves a file holding case history beside the log, readable only by the case root's owner (R31; verify-62, verify-64) | R42, R31 |
| A save is interrupted at an arbitrary instant: Ctrl-C or a signal, or a handler or callback that runs inside it and raises, or begins a nested save (verify-104 to verify-118) | Outside R42's and P15's guarantees (revision 23, D210). The tool tries: whatever stops a save once its line has started to reach the log is reported as the save's own answer, not recorded or not confirmed, every Python signal handler is held off from publishing the head record to the take-back, and a nested save is refused before it writes anything (43332c6). Where an interruption lands at an instant these do not cover, as a KeyboardInterrupt during a read-back across a replaced case lock does at 43332c6 (verify-118), the save may answer not confirmed where a fresh read would find it recorded, or end with the interruption itself. A check of the case log afterwards gives the true answer | R42, P15 |
| A process forks while it holds a case's lock (not supported; the tool never forks) | The child's writes through the inherited CaseLog are refused, and the child leaving the writer block releases nothing of its parent's | R42 |
| The case folder is on a network share (not supported) | Not detected (D66). Between processes the lock usually still holds; two CaseLog objects in one process might both write. The README and the trial sheet say to keep cases on a local disk | R42 |
| The case folder is on a file system without hard links (FAT or exFAT, as on many USB sticks; not supported) | The first append's link of its empty head record into place fails; the staging file is removed, nothing is written, and the save answers not recorded, naming the file system's error (revision 15; cd298cc; the answer is R42's since revision 18, built in 51c8b68). The README and the trial sheet name the file systems to use | R42 |
| Power is lost while a log entry is being written | The torn last entry is detected; sign-off is refused until the underwriter confirms dropping it. The operating system drops the lock, so the case is not left locked | R43 |
| The tool crashes while writing the pack at sign-off | The pack goes to a temporary folder and is renamed; leftovers are removed on the next start; no partial pack is visible, and the sign-off entry is written only after the rename | R26 |
| The pack's text fails the guard (a template bug, or a firm-edited template with a literal number) | No pack is written; the failing number is named | R28 |
| A line's amount or balance is £10,000,000,000,000 or more, either way (10000000000000000.01, whose last penny a float drops; verify-129) | Refused at import, naming the line, and nothing is computed; the text is read exactly, so no penny is lost before the check (R2 since revision 25, D223; the loader at 0c63ca4, the Monzo importer before T04's review). Before 0c63ca4 the loader read the text as a float and lost the penny | R2 |
| A statement's money in or money out totals £10,000,000,000,000 or more, though every line is below it (two credits of £6,000,000,000,000.00) | Refused at import, saying that money in or money out reaches that amount (R2 since revision 25; 0c63ca4). Before 0c63ca4 such a statement loaded, and the figures layer withheld every figure when M17 could not be reported to the penny (verify-129) | R2, R1, P1 |
| A case's accounts, each below the limit, together bring in or pay out £10,000,000,000,000 or more (Tier 2) | The import that would bring them there is refused, naming the account and which total would reach it, and the case is left unchanged (R41 since revision 26, D224). A combined balance no float carries to the penny is n/a with the reason (R2) | R2, R41 |
| A money figure is shown beside its parts (M2 £1,000,000,000,000,000.10 beside M25 £0.10; verify-129) | Cannot arise: an amount of that size is refused at import. Below the limit the float's error is at most a thousandth of a penny, so two-place formatting gives the counted pence, and since 0c63ca4 the shown pence come from the decimal the float reads back as | R1, R2 |
| A valid statement holds a £0.60 card payout (verify-131) | The evidence record, the appendix's figure table and the interactive table show £0.60; the memo's prose, drafted from the prompt's metrics table, quotes £1 [M2], which the guard accepts at the precision written (R2 since revision 26, D229) | R2, R28 |
| Under v1 a case profile names ACME HOLDINGS as an own account, and the statement holds FPS CREDIT ACME HOLDINGS £50 and CARD SETTLEMENT £80 (verify-130, verify-131) | The first credit is an own-account transfer (R6), so it leaves the denominator and the evidence of M16 and M26 alike; the previous release, which ran no profile, counted it in M16. R54 keeps the previous release's lines only for a statement categorised by v1 on its own with no case profile (R54 and P2 since revision 26, D229) | R54, P2, R6 |
| Under v1 a credit names both a director and an own account from the profile | Ambiguous, as under v2 (R5, R6; a profile is new behaviour under any ruleset, R54 since revision 26), with every v1 rule it matches and both profile kinds as its candidates. At 74ad8af the frozen path takes the director's name first; T05 builds the ambiguity | R5, R6, R54 |
| Average monthly revenue over a period shorter than a month exceeds what a float carries to the penny | M3 is n/a with the reason, never a penny out; every other figure is reported (R2 since revision 25; verify-128) | R2 |
| Under v1, a statement of thirty-two £0.01 loan repayments and one of £9,000,000,000,000, each later returned unpaid, below the import limit either way (verify-133) | M8 is £0.00 in exact pence, and M10 is n/a and cites no lines, on every Python. The previous release on Python 3.11 sums the loan lines to -£0.0078125, rounds the negation to £0.01 of debt service and cites every operating, drawings and loan line for M10; on 3.12 and later, whose sum is compensated, it gives the exact pence. R54 keeps the previous release's lines only where its float sums came to the exact pence, so here the exact pence prevail (R2, R54 and P2 since revision 27, D234) | R2, R54, P2 |
| A returned direct debit names a debit that is not in the statement, or a statement starts in January of year 1 with a VAT stagger, beside a credit no bucket holds (verify-132, verify-133) | The credits' reconciliation runs first and reports the difference in pence and every line not in exactly one bucket; a dangling reversal is refused after it, and a VAT quarter outside the calendar's years is skipped, never raised (3886917, f147255) | R3 |
| A payer the business sent £0.01 by transfer sends back two credits of £0.01 (verify-132) | M9 offsets one penny. Its cap goes to the payer's credits in statement order, so the first counts in full and the second not at all, and the evidence note names neither, since neither counts in part (3886917) | P2 |
| A day closes in credit, and days overdrawn (M6) or a month's days-overdrawn cell reads it (verify-129: L1, a £1 debit, closes day one at -£1; L2, a £2 credit, closes day two at £1) | M6 is one day and cites L1, the line that closed the overdrawn day, as the previous release did and the dual run compares (P2, R10 and R54 since revision 25, D223; 0c63ca4). The month's days-overdrawn cell is one day and cites L1 and L2, every close it reads (P2, R11) | P2, R10, R11 |
| A review an earlier release left pending is resumed, and a figure a policy rule reads moved within the rule's band (M15's 50.01% now 50.00%; verify-144) | Decided on this release's figures; the rule keeps its outcome, read from the comparison and limits the stored result holds, and the policy file is not opened (bff7ddc; R53 since revision 28) | R53, R1, R2 |
| As above, and the figure now fails the rule for the first time or cannot be computed (M3 n/a under a custom rule that passes when missing; verify-145) | The stored result does not hold the rule's fail or when-missing action, so the policy file is read and used only if its hash is the one stored with the review; it then gives the outcome (94ced9c) | R53 |
| As above, and the policy file is missing, unreadable or changed, or the review stores no hash (verify-146) | `cma resume` refuses with exit 2, naming the figure and saying to run `cma run` on the statement again; the checkpoint is left as it was and no file is written. Outside R53's guarantee (revision 28, D249) | R53 |
| A stored rule holds no comparison or threshold, and its figure changed | Settles nothing from the stored result and falls to the policy file (76f230c; verify-148) | R53 |
| The policy file is changed or removed while a resumed review waits for the answer | It changes nothing: `cma resume` writes this release's figures and policy result into the stored review before it shows it, so nothing is read again after the answer (52914cc; verify-149, verify-150). A refusal raised while the decision is applied is reported, with nothing recorded and the review left pending (23acaa1) | R53 |
| A resumed review's memo no longer holds against this release's figures | The grounding check fails; approving needs the grounding override, as for any draft that fails it (5e7d90c) | R53 |
| A Monzo export's row that takes money in to the limit has a bad date or a malformed Balance, or a later row or field cannot be read (verify-149 to verify-153) | Refused at the first problem, naming it, and saying that money in totals £10,000,000,000,000 or more among the rows read so far, the stopped row's amount counted once read (R2 since revision 29, D258; c2460a0) | R2 |
| The same, with an empty or repeated Transaction ID on the row that takes money in to the limit | As above once a counted row's amount is read before its Transaction ID. At c2460a0 the ID is checked first and the refusal names only the ID, which T04 changes before its review | R2 |
| A Monzo export's first problem comes before the rows that take money in to the limit | Refused naming that problem alone; once it is mended, the next import says the limit (outside R2 since revision 29, D258) | R2 |
| A description runs over several lines, or blank lines stand between records | Every refusal names a record by the line of the file it starts on, the header and blank lines counted (9efc40c; R2 since revision 29) | R2 |
| A field is longer than the CSV reader reads (131,072 characters by default) | Refused, naming the line its record starts on and saying that a field is too long to read; the records before it are checked, and nothing says an amount is at the limit (R2 since revision 29). At c2460a0 the CLI's text adds that such an amount would be past the limit, which T04 drops before its review | R2 |
| A Monzo export's first data record has a problem, and a quoted field running over a very large number of lines follows it (verify-185) | The export is read, decoded and split into lines, and the next record read, before line 2 is refused; the refusal names line 2 and nothing after it (R2 since revision 30, D289; 82758a3). Reading the file as a stream is outside R2 | R2 |
| A statement or export is too large to read into memory | Outside this release (revision 30, D289): the loader and the importer read the whole file before its first record, as the previous release did | R2 |
| A newest-first statement has a record whose balance cannot be read between two records with balances (verify-192: A,2026-01-03,A,1.00,30.00, then B with balance x, then C,2026-01-01,C,1.00,10.00) | B's balance is named as not a number when B is read. The chain is compared from A to C, across B, as before this change, once C is read, and named once reading has stopped (R2 since revision 31, D301; bedf5ff) | R2 |
| A newest-first statement's date order or balance can be judged only after a later record is read (verify-188, verify-190) | Within R2: the loader knows which way the file runs from its dates once reading has stopped, and names its order and balance problems then, with every other problem it found (revision 31, D301; bedf5ff) | R2 |
| A Monzo export's walked balance disagrees on one row, and a later row has a bad date (verify-192: lines 2 to 4, --opening-balance 100.00, line 3's Balance 999.00, line 4's date not-a-date) | The importer stops at line 4's date and names it, with money in or money out at the limit among the rows read if they bring it there. It need not walk the balance of the rows before (R2 since revision 31, D301; bedf5ff). Once the date is mended, the next import names line 3's balance | R2 |
| Two `cma run` processes start the same review id on one checkpoint file (verify-153) | The second waits for the lock, then finds the id used and refuses with exit 2 (c2460a0; R53 since revision 29) | R53 |
| A resume's refresh and another process's decision interleave (verify-151, verify-152) | Holding the lock, the refresh writes only if the review is still the pending one it read; a decided review is never reopened, and a decision made after another is not recorded and names who decided first, exit 5 (3a5770d, 6b2f80c) | R53 |
| Another program writes to the checkpoint file, the lock file is removed, renamed or replaced, or a process stops part-way through a write | Outside R53's single-decision guarantee (revision 29, D258); the tool reports as it can | R53 |
| On Windows a process waits more than about ten seconds for the checkpoint file's lock | At c2460a0 msvcrt.locking gives up and its error reaches the caller; nothing is started or recorded. T04 makes it wait, as on Linux and macOS | R53 |
| Making the evidence record fails after the decision is recorded | Nothing is written, and the decision stays recorded (c2460a0; R53 since revision 29) | R53 |
| The disk fills, a move fails or the process is interrupted between the decision and both files in place, or an output path is taken while the review waits (verify-153: a folder made at the evidence path during the prompt) | Outside R53's export guarantee (revision 29, D258). The evidence is moved first, so no memo is in place without it; a .part file can stay; the decision stays recorded, and writing it out again is outside this release | R53 |
| The buckets do not reconcile (a bug) | No figures are shown or exported; the difference in pence and the lines involved are reported | R3 |
| An outflow figure is checked against its lines (one £50 loan repayment: M8 is £50 and its one line is -£50) | As P2 says since revision 24: an outflow figure is reported as a positive amount, and its lines' signed amounts total it negated; the drill-down shows that total and says it is the figure negated (R16) | P2, R16 |
| Someone the business paid by transfer sends back more than was sent (£100 sent, £150 back, nothing else) | M9 is £0: the credit offsets only the £100 sent. M9 cites both lines, and its evidence note names the credit as counting only in part; M9 is a derived figure, so its lines are not asked to total it (P2 since revision 24) | P2, R16 |
| The profile, an override, a threshold or the policy changes after the preview was built | Sign-off is refused, naming what changed | R23 |
| Someone edits the case log by hand | The chain check fails; sign-off is refused and the first bad entry named | R43 |
| A hosted model is requested while the offline lock is on | Refused before any client is created | R29 |
| sse_app() or streamable_http_app() is called while the lock is on | Refused with the lock's error; no app is handed out (R29 since revision 30; 6b43212) | R29 |
| sse_app() or streamable_http_app() is called while the lock is off (verify-182) | The SDK's app is handed out behind the lock check, and an embedder mounts and serves it as before (R53 since revision 30; c1bbe1a). At 478e616 both refused whatever the lock | R53 |
| An app saved while the lock was off is started after the lock is turned on, in a server that starts it up before binding, as uvicorn does with lifespan events on | Start-up fails, and the server stops without binding (R29 since revision 30; 6b43212) | R29 |
| As above, in a server with lifespan events turned off, or one handed a socket already bound (verify-184) | The server binds before anything in the app runs: outside R29 (revision 30, D289). Every HTTP request on the socket gets 503, every websocket is closed, and no tool runs | R29, R53 |
| The lock is turned on while a saved app serves a long-lived request, such as an event stream | The request runs on; each message the client sends after the lock is on arrives as a new request, answered with 503, and a tool already called reads the lock and refuses before reading a file (R29 since revision 30) | R29 |
| A reading of firm.yaml taken while the lock was off, or under another firm root, is handed to a hosted constructor or request after the lock is on (verify-184) | It allows nothing outside its own run and folder, and firm.yaml is read instead (6b43212, not yet reviewed; at c1bbe1a it was accepted) | R29, P18 |
| A review drafted while the lock was off is resumed after the lock is turned on, with tracing configured (verify-184) | The resume is untraced (6b43212). In a long-lived process 6b43212 judges the lock by the graph's latest reading, which can date from before the lock was turned on (src/cma/graph.py:427); under revision 30's default T17 reads firm.yaml at the resume | R29 |
| The lock is turned on while a run is drafting with a hosted model | At 6b43212 the run's reading decides its hosted requests while it runs, so its later requests, such as a redraft, are sent, and the next run is refused. D291 chose that a lock turned on takes effect from the next run, which R29 and P18 do not yet say (Options considered) | R29, P18 |
| A native HTTP runner of the MCP server reads the lock, and the lock is turned on before Uvicorn binds its socket (verify-193) | The runner binds under the lock. Since 25db103 it serves the lock-checked app, so every request gets 503, no tool runs, and the next start is refused. Revision 31 asks whether R29 and P18 state the window (Options considered) | R29, P18 |
| A caller hands a run a LangChainTracer, or another callback, under the lock (verify-193) | 9b10daa, not yet reviewed, takes a LangSmith tracer out of the run's callbacks. Revision 31 asks what R29 says of other callbacks (Options considered) | R29 |
| A custom policy is an exact copy of a packaged rule under another policy name, and the case uses that rule's id as a sentinel (verify-193) | The rule table carries the id, as it does under the packaged policy. Revision 31 asks whether a string the policy in use holds is outside P5 (Options considered) | P5, R30 |
| The hosted model is down or its reply is cut off (offline lock off) | `cma run` behaves as today (exit 4); the pack never depends on a model | R53 |
| The workspace port is in use, or there is no browser | The next free loopback port is used; the URL with its token is printed | R33 |
| The case root is inside OneDrive or another synced folder | A warning before the case is created, recorded in the case log; a sync client that later restores the lock files is the interference above | R32, R42 |
| The case root is inside the repository but not git-ignored | Refused | R31 |
| The accounts in a multi-account case cover periods that do not overlap | Combined balance figures are n/a with the reason; flow figures are still combined | R41 |
| An evidence record was made with a different ruleset | Verify refuses and names the version that made it | R45 |
| The firm rules or thresholds file is malformed | Refused at load, naming the line; no case opens with silently different figures | R20, R14 |
| A very large case (10,000 lines or more) | Lines are paged in the workspace; recomputing has a 2-second target at 10,000 lines | R52 |

## Migration

- There is no database. Case folders are new, so there is nothing to backfill.
- Evidence JSON v1 is a superset of today's record. Records without schema_version are read as v0; verify recomputes M1-M16 for them and says that is all it can check.
- The committed examples (examples/cafe_stretched.memo.md, .memo.evidence.json and .truth.json) are regenerated in the same change. The CSV is byte-identical; the appendix gains M17-M28, M16's new name and M2's reworded method; in the truth file, the director capital line becomes director_funding. test_categoriser_matches_truth runs under v2; v1 is covered by P7, which compares values.
- Cash: no committed fixture, synthetic case or test has a cash deposit line, so moving cash into trading receipts changes no committed value. On real statements, including the owner's Monzo export, cash lines that fell to OTHER now count as trading and show in M18.
- Rule tables (revision 3): the T03 branch's v2 changes before it merges. Cash paid in loses protected; the bare pot label rule goes; exceptions that hide another list's cue go (the HMRC rule's savings exception), and longer phrases join the lists instead; the conflict check covers names in any two buckets. v2 is not yet the default and has never written a signed pack, so no evidence record needs repair.
- Categoriser (revision 4): on the T03 branch the rail-plus-cash check moves ahead of the cues; candidates widen to both stages, the rail included; word boundaries count letters and digits in any script, for rules and profile names alike; and v2 gains its category order, which changes its hash. The branch's tests that expect only the deciding stage's candidates change with it (the HMRC-with-savings case gains the rail). No committed CSV or JSON fixture outside .bridgie holds a non-ASCII character (Consumer trace, item 18), so the boundary change moves no committed figure. v2 has still written no signed pack.
- Categoriser (revision 5, D64): every occurrence of a listed phrase is found, overlaps included, and a combining mark joins its word. Both change only lines that hold an overlapping phrase or a non-ASCII character, and no committed fixture outside .bridgie holds either a non-ASCII character or an overlapping own-pot phrase, so no committed figure moves.
- Categoriser (revision 6, D70-D72): the cheque becomes a cue with no category and the cash rule loses its exception; advance wording joins the loan list; listed names and profile names match across separators. No committed fixture outside .bridgie holds cheque, advance or hyphenated listed wording (Consumer trace, item 21) or any non-ASCII character (item 18), and the runner's P7 and dual-run tiers passed after D71 (verify-39), so no committed figure moves. v2's hash changes with the table; v2 has still written no signed pack.
- Categoriser (revision 7, D78): a profile name no longer settles an ambiguous credit; it is added to the candidates, and both kinds are added when both match. Only credits that the table finds ambiguous and that also name someone in a case profile change. `cma run` takes no profile, no case folder exists outside tests, and no committed fixture or synthetic case carries a profile (Consumer trace, item 22), so no committed figure moves and P7 is untouched. The table's bytes do not change, so v2's hash does not.
- Requirements (revision 8, D81, D82): R5 and R6 say the precedence C1 has held since revision 3 and the T03 branch builds, so no code, table, hash or committed figure changes.
- Categoriser (revision 9, D86, D88, D90): a credit that both a director's and an own account's names match, and that no protected verdict decides, becomes ambiguous instead of director funding, and records every rule it matched (e24f466). Only credits naming both kinds from a case profile change; nothing committed carries a profile, so no committed figure moves and P7 is untouched. README.md:78 in the T03 worktree changes with it before T03 is re-verified.
- Categoriser (revision 10, D94, D96, D97): the validator refuses more (every pattern form, back-references and conditional groups in v2, the bank-code flag anywhere but on a listed code). The returned-payment rule gains the flag, so v2's bytes and hash change; the shipped table passes. The owner took the revision 10 defaults (D101, D102), and D100 builds the first two: the bank's code is exempt from the longer-phrase rule, and a pattern's longest alternative counts. Reading a returned payment's payee under v2 with the recognised returned-payment wording set aside is still to be built. No shipped phrase begins with a bank code and no shipped pattern has two alternatives that start at one place, so no shipped verdict moves; no committed fixture holds RETURNED DD wording that v2 recognises without the bank's code, so reversal links on committed fixtures do not move. v2 has still written no signed pack.
- Requirements (revision 11, D106): R4 says what D101 and D102 decided and D100 builds, so no code, table, hash or committed figure changes for it. D110 adds one form the validator refuses; the shipped table passes, so v2's hash does not change.
- Requirements (revision 12, D112): R42, R43 and P15 say what D108 and D109 decided. T12's reader has done what D109 says since D105, and the worktree's uncommitted work does what D108 says, so no log format, stored data or committed figure changes.
- Categoriser (revision 13, D115-D121): the validator refuses more (atomic groups and possessive repeats, patterns that can match no text, ids outside letters, digits, _, . and -, the three reserved ids, a listed bank code without its flagged rule, and an exception or a debit-only direction on the bank-code rule), and the matcher seeks the longest end for any variable repeat. src/cma/rules/v2.yaml is unchanged between b6494fd and 1b592b6 and passes, so v2's hash does not change and no shipped verdict moves. D126's refusal of exceptions on every v2 rule (4adddf9) moves none either, since no shipped v2 rule has one; the frozen v1 table keeps its exceptions (R54).
- Requirements (revision 13, D124): R42 and P15 state one save at a time per holder and the repair boundary. b428d56 builds one save at a time and the append's growth check, and 1d16873 builds the 'not recorded' message at the head rename, the read-back after publishing and the repair's re-checks. The removal of a staging file after a failed write is still to build. None changes the log format, the head record or a committed figure.
- Requirements (revision 14, D131): R4 and R42 say what D125 to D127 decided and 1b592b6, 4adddf9 and 1d16873 build, so no log format, stored data or committed figure changes. The revision 14 default for a payment-method cue inside a cash phrase moves no shipped verdict, because no shipped cash phrase holds payment-method wording.
- Requirements (revision 15, D136): R4 and R42 say what D132 and D136 decided and 78556a7 and cd298cc build, so no log format, stored data or committed figure changes. The two-way exemption and the lookaround refusal move no shipped verdict, because no shipped cash phrase holds payment-method wording and no shipped v2 pattern has a lookaround; the revision 15 default on anchors moves none either, because the bank's code is the only anchored pattern v2 ships. A first head record made by a link is the same file a rename made.
- Requirements (revision 16, D141): R4, R42, R43 and P15 say what D137 and D141 decided and 5c9a5bd, 2c72469 and f5436bb build, so no log format, stored data or committed figure changes.
