The anchor refusal moves no shipped verdict, because the bank's code is the only anchored pattern v2 ships, and the set-aside rule moves none at either grain, because no shipped phrase holds both cash wording and a payment-method cue. A head record taken back is the one the save's check read, so it leaves the torn tail a crash leaves, which the existing repair drops.
- Requirements (revision 17, D148): R4, R42, R43 and P15 say what D142 to D144 decided and 4b3b21c, 0d339cc and 3076547 build, so no log format, stored data or committed figure changes. The set-aside rule by match moves no shipped verdict, for revision 16's reason. The take-back's lock re-check, the revision 17 default, is one check in T12 and changes no stored data.
- Requirements (revision 18, D153): R4, R42 and R43 say what D149 and D153 decided and 9042fb4 and 7c8fba2 build, so no log format, stored data, table or committed figure changes. The frozen check moves no shipped verdict: v1 loads as before, by its pinned hash, and v2 passes every check. A save the file system fails stores nothing new; under the revision 18 default T12 maps the error at four more call sites, which changes only what the caller is told.
- Requirements (revision 19, D158): R4, R42, R54 and P15 say what D154 and D158 decided and 9c265a3, 51c8b68 and 7cb2e71 build, so no log format, stored data, table or committed figure changes. The shipped v1 matches its pin, so comparing its bytes first changes nothing for it, and v2 holds no malformed value. A save refused partway through its line leaves what a crash leaves, which the existing repair drops; under the revision 19 default T12 carries the error out of six reads, which changes only what the caller is told.
- Requirements (revision 20, D163): R4, R42 and P15 say what D159 and D163 decided and de6a56e, 1e35352 and 5139e22 build, so no log format, stored data, table or committed figure changes. The shipped tables hold no malformed value and the shipped v1 matches its pin, so neither the bank-code check nor the changed-table refusal moves a shipped verdict. Under the revision 20 default T12 words two refusals for the take-back, which changes only what the caller is told.
- Requirements (revision 21, D168): R4 and R42 say what D164 and D168 decided and 26bc51e, 7fb3a23 and 5f5bb2e build, so no log format, stored data, table or committed figure changes. The shipped v1 matches its pin and carries no exception, and every shipped rule's matchers build, so neither the exception check on a changed v1 nor the matchers built while a table is checked moves a shipped verdict. Wording a save refused before its line as not recorded changes only what the caller is told.
- Requirements (revision 22, D192): R4, R43 and P15 say what D192 decided and 523c5df and 4c017d4 build, so no log format, stored data, table or committed figure changes. The shipped tables give no key twice and load with their digests, and the reader's extra looks read no stored data.
- Requirements (revision 23, D210): R4, R42, R43, R54 and P15 state two scope limits, so no code, log format, stored data, table or committed figure changes. 3ae10cc names more of a table that is not well formed than R4 now asks, and 43332c6 keeps every check it has under lock-file interference and interruption; nothing is removed.
- Requirements (revision 24, D219): P2, R1, R11 and R16 say which figures are sums, outflows and derived. No figure's value, sign or evidence changes, so P7 and the dual-run tier are untouched. M9 gains an evidence note, additive, which a record or checkpoint written before simply lacks (R53). T04's P2 test, which negated the outflow totals (tests/test_money_in.py:90-97 at d7ec35f), now asserts what P2 says, as tests/test_metrics.py:90 and :92-94 already do.
- Requirements (revision 25, D223): R2 refuses at import a statement whose amount, balance, money in or money out reaches £10,000,000,000,000; the previous release loaded one and reported its figures through floats. No committed CSV holds an amount or balance of even £1,000,000,000 (Consumer trace, item 26), and the synthetic generator's amounts are far below it, so nothing committed is refused and no figure moves. M1 to M16 keep the previous release's evidence, which the dual run already compares (tests/test_previous_release.py:128 and :143), so no evidence changes. Of the new derived figures only M26 is built, and it already cites its denominator's credits; the monthly cells (T06) and M27 and M28 (T07) have not started, so their evidence is new, not changed. A checkpoint or evidence record written before is read as before.
- Requirements (revision 26, D224, D225, D229): R2 says where a money figure is to the penny, which aa04f0c builds for the appendix and the interactive table; the memo's prose and its prompt table are as the previous release wrote them, so the committed example's prose does not move, and its appendix is regenerated to the penny, which changes only that table's text. R54, P2, P7 and R10 limit the previous release's lines to a statement categorised by v1 on its own with no case profile; no committed or synthetic case carries a profile or a second account, so no figure, evidence or test expectation moves. R41's combined limit is Tier 2 and has no build yet. T05's title (tasks.json:247) also gains that R5 and R6 apply under v1 as under v2.
- Requirements (revision 27, D230, D234): R2, R10, R54, P2 and P7 limit the previous release's lines and values for M1 to M16 to the statements on which its float sums came to the exact pence, and elsewhere let the exact pence prevail, which f147255 already computes. No committed or synthetic case comes near the drift, and the dual run compares values before evidence on Python 3.11, 3.12 and 3.13 in CI, so no figure, evidence or test expectation moves. The pack's memo-ready sections keep the whole pound (D230), as revision 26 wrote.
- Requirements (revision 28, D249): R53 states the scope limit 94ced9c builds, so no stored data, table, checkpoint or committed figure changes, and a refusal rewrites no checkpoint. When this release rewords R9's description in the packaged policy (below), the packaged file's hash changes. A review the previous release drafted with the packaged policy is then settled only by its stored result, and one that the stored result does not settle is refused.
- Requirements (revision 29, D258): R2 and R53 state scope limits that c2460a0 mostly builds, so no stored data, table, checkpoint format or committed figure changes. A checkpoint file gains a lock file beside it, created by the first `cma` process that takes it and holding no data; an older package ignores it. An export that imported before still imports: the Monzo importer reads the same fields and refuses the same files, and only the order within a row, and so which of two problems on one row is named, changes.
- Requirements (revision 30, D289): R2, R29, R53 and P18 state scope limits that 82758a3 and 6b43212 build, so no stored data, table, checkpoint or committed figure changes. The previous release had no offline lock, so an embedder that mounted its MCP app mounts the app again while the lock is off; under the lock the app is refused, and one built before the lock was turned on serves nothing once it is. T04's docstrings, comment and test name change what they claim, not what the reader does.
- Requirements (revision 31, D301): R2 says which checks revision 30's one-record-ahead limit covers, as bedf5ff already builds, so no stored data, table, checkpoint or committed figure changes, and a file that loaded or imported before still does. T04's two docstrings and its comment that say nothing past the first problem is read (src/cma/ingest.py:286 and :343, src/cma/importers/monzo.py:169 at bedf5ff) change what they claim.
- Tasks (revisions 8 to 11): T05's title (tasks.json:247), which says a director's name makes a credit director funding, gains the protected-verdict exception, the ambiguous-credit rule and the both-kinds rule before T05 starts. The queue that lists such credits stays with T21 (R15).
- Committed fixtures (revision 3): the change that lands the verdict lists every committed line whose v2 category differs from v1, ambiguous credits with their candidates among them (R10, P7). The synthetic generator's existing credits carry wording from one list at most: FPS CREDIT DIRECTOR CAPITAL INTRODUCED has a rail and director wording only, so it stays director funding (src/cma/synth.py:225).
- Case log (revision 5): nothing to migrate. The scope is stated, not changed; the T12 branch removed its fork machinery under D63 before merge, and no case folder exists outside tests.
- Case log (revisions 6 to 15): nothing to migrate. The reader rule, the reader taking the lock first (revision 7), a check's shared hold (revision 8), and the head record before the first line (revision 9) change what a reader reports and when a writer waits or is refused. So do the gate and the lock-file scope (revision 10), the re-check before each step a reader reads (revision 11, D108; in R42 since revision 12), one save at a time (revision 13), the read-back and the repair's re-checks (revision 14), and the gate's re-check and the first head record linked into place (revision 15). None changes what the log or the head record holds. With D69's marks and D93's counts gone, the lock files hold no data. Since D95 each case folder also has a gate file, which the first writer creates before the case lock; no case folder exists outside tests, so none lacks one.
- Policy YAML: R9's description (uk_sme_default.yaml:86, 'left unexplained') is reworded to match M16's new name, and the policy hash changes, recorded as before. R9 keeps M16. Whether it should move to M26 is a decision for whoever owns the firm's policy. The workspace and pack read the same packaged file, so there is no second policy to migrate.
- Pending reviews in a --checkpoint-db written by the old version still resume: the new Transaction fields have defaults, no Category value is removed, and PolicyResult does not change. No new type travels through the graph state, so CHECKPOINT_TYPES does not change. Since revision 28 (D249) such a review is decided on the figures this release computes, its policy outcome taken from the stored result or the policy file with the stored hash. One that neither settles is refused, its checkpoint left as it was, and is drafted again with `cma run`.
- Reversible: an older package ignores case folders it does not know and reads the v1 evidence keys it knows. CMA_RULESET=v1 brings back the old categorisation, with cash falling to OTHER and no ambiguity verdict from the table, without a reinstall; a case's profile names still apply, as under v2 (R54 since revision 26).

## Rollout and flag

**Flags**
- CMA_RULESET: becomes v2 by default once evals, P7, the pairwise matrix, the separator matrix, the profile matrix and the rule-order shuffle pass; v1 restores today's categorisation exactly for a statement on its own with no case profile, including cash falling to OTHER and first-match order; a case's profile names still apply under v1, as R5 and R6 say (R54 since revision 26, D229).
- offline_lock in firm.yaml: on by default. Turning it off is a deliberate act that is recorded, and every evidence record states where data went.
- show_policy_verdict in firm.yaml: on by default (D28). Off hides the outcome and every rule result in the workspace and the pack at once, without a release.
- The workspace, previews, packs, importers, pseudonymiser, verify and report are new commands; none of them runs unless invoked.

**Steps**
1. Land C1 (tables as data, v1 frozen) with P7 passing before any category changes.
2. Land the v2 tables (cash wording, payment-method cues, the bank-code flag and the category order included) and the two-stage verdict with the ambiguity policy and the profile candidates, then the buckets, monthly summary, indicators and judgement queue, behind CMA_RULESET. Make v2 the default when these pass:
   - evals;
   - the pairwise matrix, the separator matrix and the profile matrix (protected verdicts and both kinds together included);
   - the rule-order shuffle, over whole statements, comparing every credit's category and the money-in figures, round-trip pass included;
   - the table-edit cases from verify-50 to verify-103, among them: a table that calls itself frozen; a changed v1, including one that gained an exception; a malformed pattern, on a bank-code rule too; a pattern that compiles alone but cannot be used; a value YAML cannot build; a key given more than once, refused whole; and rules checked with no digest. Since revision 23 a table that is not well formed need only be refused whole, naming at least one fault (D210);
   - truth for the new synthetic features, the cash truth included.
3. Land the case store, the workspace, preview, pack and sign-off, and the offline lock:
   - the case store has the case log in its stated scope, its gate and case lock, one save at a time, and the reader R43 describes since revision 12;
   - the workspace has the judgement queue and the illustrative policy check.
   Run the P15 tests once on a Windows machine before the demo, because CI runs on Linux and checks share the gate and the lock there through a different call (D85). Demo on the owner's statements and on a synthetic case shaped like a business, both marked as demos (D23). The README and the trial sheet say case folders stay on a local disk with hard links (NTFS, APFS or a Linux file system, not FAT or exFAT), out of folders a sync, backup or cleanup tool manages, and that the lock files beside the log are the tool's own.
4. Give the friend the stopwatch trial sheet. They time comparable files the usual way and in the workspace, by stopwatch, outside the tool (D32).
5. Send the approver the exact data statement (D25): it runs on the firm's machine, the offline lock is on, and nothing leaves.
6. Do Tier 2 after the approver answers. Pilot only on pseudonymised or approved real files.

**Rollback.** For the policy check, set show_policy_verdict off. For categorisation, set CMA_RULESET=v1 or reinstall the previous wheel; this covers cash, payment-method cues, separators, the category order, the ambiguity verdict, the profile candidates, the both-kinds rule and the revision 10 matcher. Packs and evidence are immutable and record their ruleset, thresholds, policy hash, the show_policy_verdict setting and package version, so nothing needs repair. The old version leaves case folders written by the new one alone. The case log's scope, its reader rule and its gate change no stored data in the log or the head record, so they have nothing to roll back; a reinstall restores the previous reader, and a gate file left behind is ignored.

## Observability

Nothing leaves the machine, so every signal is local, and the underwriter chooses whether to share it.

- **The signal that proves it works:** for each case, in `cma report` and in each pack's evidence: traced figures 100% (every figure has evidence or an n/a reason), reconciled true, guard grounded true.
- **The signal that tests the bet's biggest risk:** M26 (unexplained share of money in) on each real or pseudonymised case, and the size of the judgement queue. If most cases sit above 20%, the tool is handing the hard part back (board risk 1).
- **The signal that tests the ambiguity policy (D52, D60):** per case, the number of ambiguous credits, their share of money in, and their candidate sets, read from line provenance in each pack's evidence and counted among the judgement-queue lines in `cma report`. A candidate set that keeps recurring, and that the underwriter keeps moving the same way, needs a longer phrase on one list, not a rule order. A set that pairs a cheque with cash wording, moved to cash every time, says the firm's banks print cheque and cash on mixed counter deposits.
- **The signal that tests D78 (revision 7):** ambiguous credits whose candidates include a profile kind, and where the underwriter moved them, counted by kind in `cma report`. If they almost always go to the profile's bucket, the profile names the payer on those lines and the queue costs a click each; if they often go elsewhere, the name was in a reference and D78 caught a wrong settlement.
- **The signal that tests D86 (revision 9):** ambiguous credits whose candidates include both profile kinds, and where the underwriter moved them, counted in `cma report`. If one profile entry keeps producing them, it is listed under both kinds, or a director's name sits inside an own account's name, and the profile wants correcting. If they keep going to the same kind, the firm's statements print that payer in a way a fuller profile entry could carry.
- **The signal that tests revision 8 (D81):** overrides away from a protected verdict on lines whose description also matches a profile name, counted by old and new category in `cma report`. If the friend keeps moving lending drawdowns that name a director to director funding, the firm's banks print a director's loan as the director's name and LOAN, and a longer phrase belongs on the director list, not a change of precedence.
- **The signal that tests the category order (revision 4):** overrides between two categories of the same bucket (customer transfer to card settlement, returned payment to refund), counted by kind in `cma report`. If the friend keeps reversing the order's choice, the order is wrong.
- **The signal that tests the separator set (revision 6):** overrides away from a trading category on lines whose listed name matched only across separators, counted in `cma report` from line provenance and the description. If they recur, a short name on a trading list needs a longer phrase.
- **The signal that tests D31:** overrides from cash_deposit to another category, and cash deposits flagged, per case, in `cma report`. If the friend moves most flagged cash out of trading receipts, the default or the limits are wrong.
- **The signal for the case log (revisions 5 to 21):** `cma case check` per case gives one of three outcomes. Intact: exit 0, only from a reader that found the case free. Being saved or not checked: exit 4, with the recorded entries that check out, nothing checked yet, or the lock error named. A bad entry: exit 3, with the first bad or incomplete entry by number, a recorded entry cut short, a lost head record or a log that two saves told recorded at one position left included. A writer refused because another holds the case says so ('another window or command is writing to this case') and writes nothing. A writer refused because a lock file, the gate or the case lock, was removed or replaced says so: 'a case lock file was removed or replaced while this window held the case', followed by 'nothing was written' and that the save was not recorded or, once any part of the line is written, 'part of its entry was written and the save was not recorded' (revision 19), or, refused at the head rename, 'its entry was written and the save was not recorded' (revision 20). No message about a save whose line is in the log says nothing was written. A save refused because another save is under way in the same window, or because the log changed since its check or while it was being recorded, says so and asks to reload. A save whose head record was not taken back after a failed read-back says that whether the save is in the log is not confirmed, names what stopped the take-back in its own words, and asks for the case log to be checked before anything else is saved (R42 since revision 17, D143; one answer since revision 21, D168). A save the file system failed names the error and says the save was not recorded or, when its head record was published and not taken back, that whether it is in the log is not confirmed (R42 since revision 18; one rule since revision 19). No message, and no error it carries, gives a second answer (revision 21). So does a save whose own check could not read the log, the head record or a lock file, and it never says the case changed or a lock file was removed or replaced (revision 20). If the friend sees the first with only one window open, or the second at all, something outside the tool is touching the case folder (a share, a forked process, a stuck command, a sync or backup client, a cleanup script), and the trial sheet says to move the case to a plain local folder.
- **The signal for the outcome:** stopwatch minutes per file, recorded by the friend on the trial sheet outside the tool (D32). The case log keeps event times for the audit trail only, and nothing reports a duration from them.
- **Log lines:** one structured line per event in the case log, with the event, case id, snapshot and counts, and no names or descriptions. For example: event=pack_written traced=100 reconciled=true unexplained_pct=12.4 ambiguous=4 cash_flagged=3 policy_check=shown.
- **What fires when it does not work:** there is no remote alerting, by design. Instead the tool refuses, with a non-zero exit and a red banner, when reconciliation fails, the guard fails, the log chain breaks, a rule table fails validation, or a hosted model is requested under the offline lock. `cma report` exits non-zero if any case has traced figures below 100% or reconciled false, and `cma case check` exits 3 on a broken log.
- **CI:**
  - evals extended with truth for every bucket and indicator, the cash ones included;
  - P1-P20 run as tests, with the pairwise matrix, the separator matrix, the profile matrix (both kinds together included), the rule-order shuffle over whole statements, the table-edit cases, and the case log's exclusion, reader, one-save-at-a-time and interference cases marked as P15 tests;
  - the wheel check also loads the rule tables, thresholds, field map and packaged policy from an installed wheel;
  - CI runs on Linux, so the Windows lock path is covered by Rollout step 3, not by CI.

## Security notes

- **Who can call it:** the operating-system user who owns the case root, whose files are readable by that user only. The workspace listens only on loopback, requires a random per-launch token on every request, rejects any Host header that is not the loopback address (against DNS rebinding) and sends no permissive CORS headers. Writes also need the expected log position.
- **The lock files are not access control** (revisions 5 and 10). The gate and the case lock order honest writers on one machine; access is the case root's owner-only permissions (R31), which `cma case check` relies on rather than checks. Someone with the operating-system user's rights can delete the lock files. A writer then refuses once it sees either file gone or replaced (the gate since revision 15). The tool tries to show two saves told recorded at one position as a broken log, and to let a repair under lock-file interference remove only a save told it was not recorded (D124, with D125's read-back and re-check, R42 since revision 14); since revision 23 these are what it tries, not guarantees (D210). Such a person can also rewrite the log and its head record together, which the files alone cannot show. The sign-off and the pack pin the log head they saw (R22), so a later rewrite no longer matches a signed pack. A writer that keeps saving can keep other readers at being saved, but cannot make them report the log as intact or broken (revision 6); sign-off and every append judge the whole log while holding the case. A process that checks a case in a tight loop can make another window's save wait at the gate and then be refused (revisions 7 and 10); that takes the same user's rights, which already allow worse.
- **Trust boundary:** bank data the applicant controls enters through the importers, and the loader checks its structure and balance chain. Descriptions are written by payers: they are escaped in HTML under a strict content security policy with no inline script, formula characters are neutralised in CSV, and they never go into prompts. The candidate rule ids shown for an ambiguous credit, and their buckets, come from the shipped tables and the two fixed profile kinds, not from payer text. Which wording is the bank's own code is listed in code, so neither a payer nor a table edit can claim it (D97). Case-log messages name entries by number and never quote their content.
- **Secrets:** an optional model API key in the environment, only when the offline lock is off. It is never logged or written to evidence.
- **Personal data:** statements, the profile (director names, account identifiers), override reasons and packs stay in the case folder. Evidence holds ids, figures, rule ids and hashes, not descriptions or the profile; a profile candidate is recorded by its kind, never the name. A staging file a failed write leaves holds case history and sits under the same owner-only case root; it is removed when its write fails (revision 13; at cd298cc only the empty head record's is removed whatever fails, and the others only when their rename fails). Purge deletes the folder and leaves a ledger line with no personal data. Encryption at rest is the firm's disk encryption; the tool adds none of its own. Retention follows the firm's policy, and purge is how the tool carries it out.
- **Risk path:** lending data (risk: data). The human acknowledgement is the named sign-off bound to the snapshot. The name is typed, not authenticated (README known limit), so the OS user is recorded alongside it. Real sign-in belongs to a hosted version. The illustrative outcome is computed, labelled and never signed as a decision.
- **Worst cases with this deployed:**
  1. An applicant writes script into a payment reference to act in the underwriter's browser, for example to change categories or sign off. Escaping, the content security policy and the token stop it.
  2. An applicant makes money look like trading income: by naming the payer after an acquirer, adding an invoice reference, or, since D31, paying cash in.
     - The round-trip check, the director-name rule and the indicators surface it, including the three cash indicators, and the line is one click from its figure. Cash split below every limit is the gap that remains, and it is visible only in M18.
     - Since D52, wording that mixes lists cannot reach trading receipts: a payer who adds a second list's word only sends the credit to the queue. Every line-type cue is non-trading, so payer-written cues can lower M2 but never raise it.
     - Since revision 4, a listed name hidden inside a longer word in any alphabet does not match, and since revision 5 that holds after a combining mark too. Adding a gateway's name to an invoice-referenced credit does not take it out of the round-trip check.
     - Since revision 6, a typographic dash, a dot or an invisible character can no longer hide a payment-method cue or a lender phrase, and a cheque cannot hide cash wording. A payer can still split a short trading name across words to be read as it (E BAY), which counts as trading receipts as the plain spelling always did; the irregular-acquirer indicator reads it.
     - Since revision 7, a payer cannot take an ambiguous credit out of the queue by naming a director or an own account in the reference: the name adds a candidate and settles nothing. A payer can still name a director in the reference of a credit the table does not find ambiguous and no protected verdict decides, which moves it to director funding (R5); that lowers M2, never raises it.
     - Since revision 8 the requirements say what the build already did: a profile name in a refund, a loan or a returned payment cannot move it out of its protected verdict, and both readings are non-trading.
     - Since revision 9, a payer who writes both a director's and an own account's names into a reference only sends the credit to the queue, which lowers M2, never raises it.
     - Since revision 10, a payer cannot write wording that passes for the bank's own returned-payment code, which only a code listed in the program at the start of the line can be.
  3. Someone turns the offline lock off and uses a hosted model. The prompt still carries only tables (P5), and the evidence records where data went.
  4. Someone with the underwriter's login edits the case log and its head record together to hide an override. The files cannot show it, but a signed pack pins the head it saw, so the edit shows against every signed pack. Deleting the lock files and repairing the log while another window saves is meant to remove only a save that window was told was not recorded (D124, with D125's read-back and re-check, R42 since revision 14); since revision 23 that is what the tool tries, not a guarantee, and the signed pack's pinned head is what shows a lost save (D210).
- **Pseudonymised is not anonymous:** amounts, dates and balances can identify a business again, so the output is labelled as personal data.

Each worktree sits under .bridgie/worktrees but is a checkout of its task branch; within it, .bridgie/ is excluded. All other searches exclude .bridgie/. The commands below are equivalent forms with no backslash escapes.

1. **Category enum (values added; cash_deposit maps to trading receipts).** `rg -nw 'Category|REVENUE_CATEGORIES' -g '!.bridgie/**'`; `rg -n '[.](category|reverses)' src`; `rg -n 'category_by_id' -g '!.bridgie/**'`. 20. **Case log, its lock files and its reader.** This covers the revision 5 scope, revision 6's reader answers, revision 7's lock tried first, revision 8's shared hold for checks, revision 9's head record before the first line and damage never dropped, revision 10's gate and lock-file scope, revision 11's re-check before each step a reader reads and its reading for a writer at the gate while another holds the case, which R42, R43 and P15 state since revision 12 (D108, D109), revision 13's one save at a time and repair boundary (D124), revision 14's read-back and the repair's re-checks (D125), revision 15's re-check of both lock files and the first head record linked into place (D136, after verify-68), and revision 16's take-back after a failed read-back and a read taken again across a changed lock file (D141, after verify-70), and revision 17's not-confirmed answer and the holder's and busy-gate reads (D143, D148, after verify-72). On the T12 branch at cd298cc (reviewed as verify-70), a ripgrep search for `CaseLog|for_case|CaseLocked|LogConflict|CaseLogBroken|require_intact|drop_torn_entry|in_progress|as_recorded|past_recorded|[.]checked|gate_path|_require_lock_in_place|_one_save|intact_bytes|SaveNotRecorded|_publish|_create_empty_head|_gate_id` over src, tests, evals and README.md finds 360 matches in three files:
    - src/cma/case.py (101), the definition. LogCheck has its being-saved and not-checked forms. The check of a log against its head record (_check, :271) names a missing head record and a cut-short recorded entry as damage, and takes a complete line past the recorded entries, linked to the last recorded entry, for a torn write to drop. verify (:444) is as Context describes. writer() (:622) waits for checks and a moment for another writer, and releases by depth. _enter passes the gate alone and notes its identity (:666-697), and _take_case_lock refuses a lock file replaced while it was taken (:699). _require_lock_in_place (:732-745) compares both lock files with the ones the writer took, and runs immediately before every write call of the line (:953), before the empty head record is linked into place (:899) and before every attempt of each rename (_replace, :995). Its own message still says nothing was written (:744-745), and _publish turns that into 'the save was not recorded' when the refused step is the head rename after the line (:971-973). _one_save (:769-783) wraps append (:785) and drop_torn_entry (:813), and an append refuses if the log's length or bytes changed since its check (:947-950). _create_empty_head (:888-906) links the first empty head record into place and stops with a conflict if one is there. A repair compares the log's bytes with its check before reading it (:836-838), and re-checks both lock files, the log's bytes and the head record before its log rename (:843-851). Every save publishes its head record only if the head record still lists what its check read (_write_head, :908-931), and is told recorded only after reading back an intact log that holds its entry (_publish, :962-978).
    - src/cma/cli.py (4): `cma case check`, the only reader of in_progress and checked outside case.py, which exits 4 for both answers that check nothing.
    - tests/test_case_log.py (255), among them the gate cases, the interference cases (a lock file replaced after the line, after the empty head record and before a repair's rename, :1859-1916; the gate removed or replaced during a save, :2247-2271, named p15) and the eight tests added since review 20 (:2070-2244), marked invariants but not named p15.

29. **`cma resume` on a review an earlier release left pending (revision 28, D249; R53).** Found by the revision 28 searches above. It refuses with exit 2 on StaleReviewError (:148-150) and with exit 6 on AmountError or ReconciliationError (:145-147); _decide's graph.invoke (:289) catches neither. Effect: no reader or stored field changes; R53's scope names the refusal the CLI already gives. A refusal rewrites no checkpoint. A review drafted by this release resumes as before, since its figures are today's and refreshed changes nothing (src/cma/graph.py:231-233).
30. **The checkpoint file's lock, the export and the record reader (revision 29, D258; R2, R53).** Found by the revision 29 searches above. The lock is cli._review_lock (src/cma/cli.py:228-251), a file named after the checkpoint file with .lock added. `cma run` takes it around its review-id check and the review's first steps (:104), `cma resume` around its comparison with the pending review and its refresh (:167), and _decide around applying a decision (:356); nothing else reads the file. The export is src/cma/cli.py:328-339, which writes NAME.evidence.json and the memo, each through a NAME.part file beside it; the previous release wrote the memo and then its evidence in place (src/cma/cli.py:226-229 on the integration branch). Readers of the written pair: tests/test_cli.py:36, :63 and :143, and the committed example. The interleaving tests are tests/test_cli.py:169, :414, :440, :487 and :514. read_records (src/cma/ingest.py:108-149) is shared by load_csv (:170) and the Monzo importer (src/cma/importers/monzo.py:153). TOO_LONG is read by the MCP server's refusal (src/cma/mcp_server.py:72), and the CLI prints the loader's whole message, the too-long clause included. On the integration branch only the single-decision check exists (src/cma/cli.py:240-263, tested at tests/test_cli.py:169), with no lock. Effect: no stored field or evidence key changes, and a lock file appears beside each checkpoint file. The too-long refusal loses one clause. The Monzo importer reads a counted row's amount before its Transaction ID, which changes which of two problems on one row is named, never whether the export is refused.
31. **How far a file is read before its first record is checked, and the MCP server's ASGI apps (revision 30, D289; R2, R29, R53, P18).** Found by the revision 30 searches above. The reader is ingest.iter_data (src/cma/ingest.py:277-285 at 82758a3). It decodes the whole file (_decoded, :230-239), splits it into lines (_head, :252) and hands out its records one at a time, holding one (_records, :335-425). read_data (:288-310) reads it to the end for the loader (load_csv, :438-440), which src/cma/cli.py:185 and src/cma/graph.py:286 feed the bytes they hash. The Monzo importer reads the file once (src/cma/importers/monzo.py:154), checks its header from the first record alone (read_header, :157; src/cma/ingest.py:261-274) and stops at its first problem (:171, :200). The docstrings at src/cma/ingest.py:281-282 and :338-339, the comment at src/cma/importers/monzo.py:169-170 and test_monzo_reads_nothing_past_the_first_problem (tests/test_money_in.py:1482) claim that nothing past the record a caller stops at is read. The ASGI factories are LockedMCPServer.sse_app and streamable_http_app (src/cma/mcp_server.py:69-79 at 6b43212). They return LockCheckedASGI (:82-107) while the lock is off, and the SDK's own app inside a run method past its lock check (_BUILDING_FOR_RUN, :48-62 and :111). Their tests are tests/test_firm.py:227, :243-244, :574-617 and :663. Effect: no stored field, evidence key or committed figure changes. An embedder's app is the SDK's behind the lock check, and code that checks its class sees LockCheckedASGI unless the owner chooses the alternative. T04's docstrings, comment and test name change to say checked, not read.
32. **When a statement's checks across records are made (revision 31, D301; R2).** Found by the revision 31 searches above. At bedf5ff the loader checks a record's own values as each record is handed out (check, src/cma/ingest.py:513-546), its id against the ids before it included. It compares each record's date and balance with the latest record before it that has one, both ways at once (follow, :489-511). Once reading has stopped, it decides which way the file runs from its first and last dates and names that way's order and balance problems (:576-585), then the flow totals (:587-588). The Monzo importer reads each row's currency, amount and Transaction ID as it reads the row (src/cma/importers/monzo.py:195-251, the duplicate at :249-251). It sorts the rows by date and time (:291) and then walks the balance from the opening balance (:310-326). Readers of the refusals are the CLI's handler and the MCP server's (item 26). The docstrings at src/cma/ingest.py:286 and :343 and the comment at src/cma/importers/monzo.py:169 still say that nothing past the first problem is read. Effect: no reader changes; R2 states the order the build already has, and the docstrings and the comment change what they claim.
